# Balinese App Sentence Curriculum — Section 3: Expanding Horizons (A2)

> **Companion file to:** `balinese_course_vocabulary.md` (Lessons 31–70), plus the Section 1 & 2 sentence files.
> **Format:** `Balinese sentence -> English translation`
>
> **Same construction rules as Sections 1–2:** every entry appears in ≥3 sentences within its lesson (recycled entries included); each sentence introduces at most ONE new entry; all other words are cumulative from Lesson 1; register-consistent; set phrases count as one entry; proper names are placeholders; grammatical glue (-ne, -a?, -ng) is free.
>
> **Grammar unlocked in Section 3 (not before!):**
> - **Lesson 31:** past experiences — `taen/naenin` (ever), `suba taen` (have already), `sing taen` (never), `engsap/lali` (forget), `ilang/ical` (lost), `uug/rusak` (broken).
> - **Lesson 32:** the future markers `lakar` (Andap) and `jagi` (Alus), ability `bisa/nyidaang/mrasidayang`, certainty `pasti/janten/dong`, possibility `mirib/minab`, hearsay `kocap`. Note: standalone `titiang` (the humble Alus "I") only unlocks in Lesson 58 — until then use `tiang`, except inside the taught set phrase `titiang jagi mrika`.
> - **Lesson 36:** taste words (`manis`, `pait`, `lalah`…) and `rame` (crowded).
> - **Lesson 37:** media & activity verbs — `maca` (read), `nulis` (write), `mabalih` (watch), `megending` (sing), `maen` (play), `ngomong` (talk).
> - **Lesson 39:** comparatives (`-an` forms, `lebih`, `paling` = "most" — note `paling` meant "confused" since Lesson 16; context decides from here).
> - **Lesson 40:** `kene/keto/kenten` (like this/that), `unduk/indik` (about).
> - **Lesson 51:** imperatives & prohibitions — `da/eda/sampunang` (don't!), `tolih/cingak` (look!), `oyong` (stay!).
> - **Lesson 52:** storytelling frame — `satua`, `kone/kocap`, `dumun` (in the past), `kaget/prajani` (suddenly).
> - **Lesson 55:** reported speech — `kone`, `jare`, `orahanga`.
> - **Lesson 56:** `dogen/kemanten` (only), `umpami` (for example), `antos` (wait); standalone `jagi` unlocks.
> - **Lesson 57:** cognition — `inget/eling` (remember), `ngerti/resep` (understand), `percaya` (believe).
> - **Lesson 60:** the intensifiers `pisan`/`sajan` (very), `bes` (too), `tuah/wantah` (only), `hampir` (almost).
> - **Lesson 68:** similes — `buka`/`sekadi` (like) and the proverbs.

---

### Lesson 31: Past Tense

**Focus:** talking about the past — ever/never/have already, "when did…?", yesterday/earlier/just now, broken, forgot, lost, found.
**Grammar note:** Balinese has no verb conjugation; the past is marked by words like `suba/sampun` (already), `dibi/dibeng` (yesterday), `mara/wau` (just now), and experience is marked by `taen/naenin` (ever). `suba taen` = have already; `sing taen` = never.
**New in this lesson (teaching order):** taen, naenin, suba taen, sampun naenin, suba (recycled), sampun (recycled), sing taen, nenten naenin, pidan (recycled — past sense), dibi (recycled), dibeng (recycled), ibi sanja, ibak, tuni (recycled), iwau (recycled), mara (recycled), wau (recycled), uug, rusak, engsap, lali, bakat, ilang, ical, tepuk (recycled), panggih, minggu lalu, bulan lalu, tiban lalu, seken (recycled — "it really happened"), suung

1. Taen. -> Ever / to have done (familiar).
2. Icang taen ka Jawa. -> I have been to Java.
3. Cai taen ningal buaya? -> Have you ever seen a crocodile?
4. Ia taen mrika? -> Has he ever gone there?
5. Naenin. -> Ever / to have done (refined).
6. Tiang naenin ka Jawa. -> I have been to Java.
7. Ragane naenin ningal pura nika? -> Have you ever seen that temple?
8. Ipun naenin mrika. -> He / she has been there.
9. Suba taen. -> Have already / have ever (familiar phrase).
10. Icang suba taen ka kota. -> I have already been to the city.
11. Cai suba taen numpak kapal? -> Have you ever ridden a ship?
12. Ia suba taen ningal grobogan. -> He has seen a waterfall.
13. Sampun naenin. -> Have already / have ever (refined phrase).
14. Tiang sampun naenin ka kota. -> I have already been to the city.
15. Ragane sampun naenin munggah pesawat? -> Have you ever boarded a plane?
16. Ipun sampun naenin ningal wukir Agung. -> He / she has seen Mount Agung.
17. Suba. -> Already (familiar). [recycled — fresh sentences]
18. Icang suba madaar. -> I have already eaten.
19. Ia suba mulih. -> He has already gone home.
20. Kopi caine suba telah? -> Is your coffee all gone?
21. Sampun. -> Already (refined). [recycled — fresh sentences]
22. Tiang sampun neda. -> I have already eaten.
23. Ida sampun sirep. -> He / she (honored) is already asleep.
24. Pakaryan ida sampun puput. -> His / her (honored) work is finished.
25. Sing taen. -> Never (familiar phrase).
26. Icang sing taen ka kota. -> I have never been to the city.
27. Cai sing taen numpak bemo? -> Have you never ridden a bemo?
28. Ia sing taen ningal segara? -> Has he never seen the sea?
29. Nenten naenin. -> Never (refined phrase).
30. Tiang nenten naenin ka Jawa. -> I have never been to Java.
31. Ragane nenten naenin mrika? -> Have you never been there?
32. Ipun nenten naenin ningal linuh. -> He / she has never seen an earthquake.
33. Pidan. -> When (past) (familiar). [recycled from Lesson 10 — fresh sentences]
34. Pidan cai suba taen ka peken? -> When did you (ever) go to the market?
35. Pidan cai meli sepeda nika? -> When did you buy that bicycle?
36. Pidan ragane naenin ka driki? -> When have you been here?
37. Dibi. -> Yesterday (familiar). [recycled from Lesson 14 — fresh sentences]
38. Dibi icang tepuk cicing di jalan. -> Yesterday I found a dog on the road.
39. Dibi ujan bales. -> Yesterday the rain was heavy.
40. Dibi icang lan nyama ka peken. -> Yesterday my sibling and I went to the market.
41. Dibeng. -> Yesterday (refined). [recycled — fresh sentences]
42. Dibeng tiang kacunduk ida ring pasar. -> Yesterday I met him / her (honored) at the market.
43. Dibeng sabeh bales. -> Yesterday it rained heavily.
44. Dibeng ida rauh ring jero tiang. -> Yesterday he / she (honored) came to my home.
45. Ibi sanja. -> Yesterday evening (familiar phrase).
46. Ibi sanja icang pules enggal. -> Yesterday evening I slept early.
47. Ibi sanja ujan gati. -> Yesterday evening it rained hard.
48. Ibi sanja cai dija? -> Where were you yesterday evening?
49. Ibak. -> Earlier today (familiar).
50. Ibak icang ka peken. -> Earlier today I went to the market.
51. Ibak ia teka dini. -> Earlier today he came here.
52. Ibak ujan, jani endang. -> Earlier it rained; now it's clear.
53. Tuni. -> Earlier (familiar). [recycled — fresh sentences]
54. Tuni icang ningal tatit. -> Earlier I saw lightning.
55. Cai tuni ngudiang? -> What were you doing earlier?
56. Tuni ia majalan ka banjar. -> Earlier he walked to the banjar.
57. Iwau. -> Earlier (refined). [recycled — fresh sentences]
58. Iwau tiang mireng krebek. -> Earlier I heard thunder.
59. Iwau ida memargi mrika. -> Earlier he / she (honored) walked there.
60. Ragane iwau napi karya? -> What were you doing earlier?
61. Mara. -> Just now (familiar). [recycled — fresh sentences]
62. Ia mara mulih. -> He just went home.
63. Cai mara teka? -> Did you just arrive?
64. Mara pragat, ia pules. -> Just finished — he sleeps.
65. Wau. -> Just now (refined). [recycled — fresh sentences]
66. Ida wau rauh saking kota. -> He / she (honored) just arrived from the city.
67. Tiang wau neda. -> I just ate.
68. Ipun wau puput makarya. -> He / she just finished working.
69. Uug. -> Broken (familiar).
70. Hp icang uug! -> My phone is broken!
71. Gelas nika uug. -> That glass is broken.
72. Montor caine uug? -> Is your motorbike broken?
73. Rusak. -> Broken / damaged (neutral).
74. Jalan nika rusak. -> That road is damaged.
75. Umah icang rusak sangkan linuh. -> My house is damaged because of the earthquake.
76. Sepeda caine rusak? -> Is your bicycle broken?
77. Engsap. -> To forget (familiar).
78. Icang engsap adan caine! -> I forgot your name!
79. Cai engsap buku icange? -> Did you forget my book?
80. Icang engsap, ampura! -> I forgot — sorry!
81. Lali. -> To forget (refined).
82. Tiang lali wastan ragane. -> I forgot your name.
83. Ampura, tiang lali. -> Sorry, I forgot.
84. Ragane lali napi? -> What did you forget?
85. Bakat. -> To be caught by / happen to experience (familiar).
86. Icang bakat ujan di jalan. -> I got caught in the rain on the road.
87. Cai bakat apa? -> What happened to you?
88. Ia bakat panas. -> He was caught by the heat.
89. Ilang. -> Lost / gone (familiar).
90. Hp icang ilang! -> My phone is lost!
91. Cicing icang ilang. -> My dog is lost.
92. Pis icang ilang di peken. -> My money got lost at the market.
93. Ical. -> Lost / gone (refined).
94. Buku tiang ical. -> My book is lost.
95. Montor ida ical! -> His / her (honored) motorbike is lost!
96. Jinah tiang ical ring pasar. -> My money got lost at the market.
97. Tepuk. -> To find / come upon (familiar). [recycled from Lesson 24 — fresh sentences]
98. Icang tepuk pis caine. -> I found your money.
99. Cai tepuk buku icange? -> Did you find my book?
100. Ia suba taen tepuk buaya? -> Has he ever come upon a crocodile?
101. Panggih. -> To find / meet (refined).
102. Tiang panggih jinah ring margi. -> I found money on the road.
103. Ragane panggih buku tiang? -> Did you find my book?
104. Ipun panggih genah becik. -> He / she found a good place.
105. Minggu lalu. -> Last week (phrase).
106. Minggu lalu icang ka kota. -> Last week I went to the city.
107. Cai teka minggu lalu? -> Did you come last week?
108. Minggu lalu ujan bales. -> Last week the rain was heavy.
109. Bulan lalu. -> Last month (phrase).
110. Bulan lalu tiang ka Jawa. -> Last month I went to Java.
111. Ida rauh bulan lalu. -> He / she (honored) arrived last month.
112. Bulan lalu icang malajah gati. -> Last month I studied hard.
113. Tiban lalu. -> Last year (familiar phrase).
114. Tiban lalu icang ka Jawa. -> Last year I went to Java.
115. Cai nongos dini tiban lalu? -> Did you live here last year?
116. Tiban lalu masan ujan suwe. -> Last year the rainy season was long.
117. Seken. -> Really (happened) (familiar). [recycled — fresh sentences]
118. Seken icang tepuk ia dibi! -> I really met him yesterday!
119. Cai seken taen ka kota? -> Have you really been to the city?
120. Seken, hp icang uug. -> Truly, my phone is broken.
121. Suung. -> Empty / deserted (familiar).
122. Peken suung jani. -> The market is empty now.
123. Umah nika suung. -> That house is empty.
124. Warung nika suung. -> That stall is deserted.

---

### Lesson 32: Future Tense

**Focus:** the future — `lakar`/`kel` (will), ability (`bisa`, `nyidaang`, `mrasidayang`), certainty (`pasti`, `janten`, `dong`), possibility (`mirib`, `minab`), hearsay (`kocap`), and the full future-time vocabulary (tomorrow morning, next week/month/year, in a moment, later).
**Grammar note:** the future is `lakar` (Andap) + verb: `Icang lakar mulih.` The refined marker `jagi` is only available inside the set phrase `titiang jagi mrika.` until Lesson 56. Ability: `bisa`/`nyidaang` (familiar-neutral) and `mrasidayang` (refined) = "can/be able to".
**New in this lesson (teaching order):** lakar (recycled), jagi, kel, tiang lakar kema, lakar ngudiang, titiang jagi mrika, bisa, nyidaang, mrasidayang, pasti, janten (recycled), dong, mirib, minab, kocap, mani (recycled), mani semeng, benjang (recycled), buin mani (recycled), buin pidan, malih pidan, puan (recycled), buin kesep, nyanan (recycled), nyan, malih jebos (recycled), minggu depan, bulan depan, tiban depan, tonden (recycled), durung (recycled), dereng

1. Lakar. -> Will / going to (familiar). [recycled from Lesson 21 — fresh sentences]
2. Icang lakar ka pasih mani. -> Tomorrow I'll go to the beach.
3. Cai lakar meli apa? -> What will you buy?
4. Ia lakar majalan mrika. -> He will walk there.
5. Kel. -> Will (short form of lakar).
6. Icang kel mulih. -> I'm going home (I'll go home).
7. Cai kel kija? -> Where will you go?
8. Kel ia teka? -> Will he come?
9. Tiang lakar kema. -> I will go there (familiar set phrase).
10. Nggih, tiang lakar kema. -> Yes, I will go there.
11. Ampura, tiang lakar kema mani. -> Excuse me, I will go there tomorrow.
12. Lakar ngudiang. -> Going to do what (familiar phrase).
13. Cai lakar ngudiang mani? -> What will you do tomorrow?
14. Ia lakar ngudiang jani? -> What will he do now?
15. Lakar ngudiang mani, cai? -> What will you do tomorrow?
16. Titiang jagi mrika. -> I will go there (refined set phrase).
17. Nggih, titiang jagi mrika. -> Yes, I will go there.
18. Ampura, titiang jagi mrika benjang. -> Excuse me, I will go there tomorrow.
19. Bisa. -> Can / to be able (neutral).
20. Icang bisa ngaba motor. -> I can drive a motorbike.
21. Cai bisa malajah Basa Bali? -> Can you learn Balinese?
22. Ia bisa nyetir mobil. -> He can drive a car.
23. Nyidaang. -> Can / to be able (familiar).
24. Icang nyidaang madaar liu! -> I can eat a lot!
25. Nyai nyidaang ngae dedaaran? -> Can you make food?
26. Ia nyidaang mlayu enggal. -> He can run fast.
27. Mrasidayang. -> Can / to be able (refined).
28. Tiang mrasidayang makarya. -> I am able to work.
29. Ragane mrasidayang rauh? -> Are you able to come?
30. Ipun mrasidayang ngaryanang jero. -> He / she is able to build a house.
31. Pasti. -> Definitely (neutral).
32. Ia pasti teka. -> He will definitely come.
33. Pasti ujan mani? -> Will it definitely rain tomorrow?
34. Cai pasti bisa! -> You can definitely do it!
35. Janten. -> Definitely / certainly (refined). [recycled from Lesson 16 — fresh sentences]
36. Ida janten rauh benjang. -> He / she (honored) will certainly come tomorrow.
37. Janten dados, nggih. -> It will certainly be possible, yes.
38. Ipun janten mantuk. -> He / she will certainly go home.
39. Dong. -> Sure / of course (familiar).
40. Dong icang teka! -> Of course I'll come!
41. Cai dong uning! -> You surely know!
42. Dong, ia pasti liang! -> Of course — he'll be happy!
43. Mirib. -> Maybe / seems like (familiar).
44. Mirib ujan mani. -> Maybe it will rain tomorrow.
45. Mirib cai bisa. -> Maybe you can.
46. Cuacane mirib panas. -> The weather looks like it'll be hot.
47. Minab. -> Maybe / perhaps (refined).
48. Minab ida rauh. -> Perhaps he / she (honored) will come.
49. Minab sabeh benjang. -> Perhaps it will rain tomorrow.
50. Tiang minab mrika, minab nenten. -> I may go there, or may not.
51. Kocap. -> It is said (neutral).
52. Kocap pura nika kuna gati. -> It is said that temple is very ancient.
53. Kocap ia lakar rauh. -> It is said he will come.
54. Kocap gunung nika tegeh. -> It is said that mountain is high.
55. Mani. -> Tomorrow (familiar). [recycled from Lesson 14 — fresh sentences]
56. Mani icang lakar ka peken. -> Tomorrow I'll go to the market.
57. Mani cai jumah? -> Are you home tomorrow?
58. Mani ujan? -> Will it rain tomorrow?
59. Mani semeng. -> Tomorrow morning (phrase).
60. Mani semeng icang lakar majalan. -> Tomorrow morning I'll take a walk.
61. Cai teka mani semeng? -> Will you come tomorrow morning?
62. Mani semeng, bapa lakar ka carik. -> Tomorrow morning father will go to the rice field.
63. Benjang. -> Tomorrow (refined). [recycled — fresh sentences]
64. Benjang tiang lakar numbas buku. -> Tomorrow I'll buy a book.
65. Benjang ida rauh ring driki? -> Will he / she (honored) arrive here tomorrow?
66. Benjang tiang jagi mrika. -> Tomorrow I will go there.
67. Buin mani. -> Tomorrow (familiar). [recycled — fresh sentences]
68. Buin mani icang mulih ka Jawa. -> Tomorrow I go home to Java.
69. Buin mani cai megawe? -> Do you work tomorrow?
70. Buin mani icang lakar ngae kopi. -> Tomorrow I'll make coffee.
71. Buin pidan. -> When — future (familiar phrase).
72. Buin pidan cai teka? -> When will you come?
73. Buin pidan ida rauh? -> When will he / she (honored) come?
74. Buin pidan, icang lakar mulih. -> Sometime soon, I'll go home.
75. Malih pidan. -> When — future (refined phrase).
76. Malih pidan ida rauh? -> When will he / she (honored) arrive?
77. Malih pidan ragane mrika? -> When will you go there?
78. Malih pidan tiang lakar numbas nika. -> Sometime I will buy that.
79. Puan. -> Day after tomorrow (familiar). [recycled — fresh sentences]
80. Puan icang lakar ka kota. -> The day after tomorrow I'll go to the city.
81. Puan cai jumah? -> Are you home the day after tomorrow?
82. Puan ujan? -> Will it rain the day after tomorrow?
83. Buin kesep. -> In a moment (familiar phrase).
84. Buin kesep icang teka. -> I'll come in a moment.
85. Ia lakar rauh buin kesep. -> He will arrive in a moment.
86. Buin kesep ujan? -> Will it rain soon?
87. Nyanan. -> Later (familiar). [recycled from Lesson 14 — fresh sentences]
88. Nyanan icang lakar mulih. -> Later I'll go home.
89. Nyanan icang lakar meli nasi. -> Later I'll buy rice.
90. Ia nyanan lakar madaar. -> He will eat later.
91. Nyan. -> Later (short form).
92. Nyan icang mulih. -> I'll go home later.
93. Cai nyan kema? -> Will you go there later?
94. Nyan icang lakar nginem kopi. -> Later I'll drink coffee.
95. Malih jebos. -> Later (refined). [recycled — fresh sentences]
96. Malih jebos tiang lakar mantuk. -> Later I will go home.
97. Malih jebos ida rauh? -> Will he / she (honored) come later?
98. Malih jebos, tiang jagi mrika. -> Later, I will go there.
99. Minggu depan. -> Next week (phrase).
100. Minggu depan icang lakar ka kota. -> Next week I'll go to the city.
101. Cai lakar megawe minggu depan? -> Will you work next week?
102. Minggu depan tiang lakar malajah gati. -> Next week I'll study hard.
103. Bulan depan. -> Next month (phrase).
104. Bulan depan icang lakar mulih ka Jawa. -> Next month I go home to Java.
105. Bulan depan ida rauh? -> Does he / she (honored) arrive next month?
106. Bulan depan tiang lakar numbas montor. -> Next month I'll buy a motorbike.
107. Tiban depan. -> Next year (familiar phrase).
108. Tiban depan icang lakar ka Jawa. -> Next year I'll go to Java.
109. Tiban depan cai lakar ngudiang? -> What will you do next year?
110. Tiban depan ia lakar mrika. -> Next year he'll go there.
111. Tonden. -> Not yet (familiar). [recycled from Lesson 2 — fresh sentences]
112. Icang tonden madaar. -> I haven't eaten yet.
113. Cai tonden mulih? -> Haven't you gone home yet?
114. Tonden ujan, nanging langit gulem. -> It hasn't rained yet, but the sky is cloudy.
115. Durung. -> Not yet (refined). [recycled — fresh sentences]
116. Tiang durung neda. -> I haven't eaten yet.
117. Ida durung rauh. -> He / she (honored) hasn't arrived yet.
118. Durung sabeh, nanging langit gulem. -> It hasn't rained yet, but the sky is cloudy.

---

119. Dereng. -> Not yet.
120. Ida dereng rauh. -> He / she (honored) has not arrived yet.
121. Tiang dereng madaar. -> I have not eaten yet.

### Lesson 33: Occupations

**Focus:** jobs around the village and city — farmer, fisherman, merchant, driver, police, doctor, priest, craftsman, artist, boss, employee — plus busy/unemployed and `melali` (going out for fun).
**Grammar note:** "X is a Y" needs no verb: `Bapa icang petani.` "My father (is a) farmer." "To become": `dadi` + job.
**New in this lesson (teaching order):** guru, petani, madul, nelayan, bendega, dagang, sopir, polisi, tentara, doktor, bidan, perawat, pandita, sulinggih, pemangku (recycled), undagi, tukang, tukang banten, seniman, pangayah, bos, pegawai, megae, makarya (recycled), pagawean (recycled), pakaryan (recycled), gajian, kantor (recycled), nganggur, sibuk, repot, melali
*(Notes: `megae` is a distinct familiar variant of `megawe`. Religious ranks: `pemangku` (temple priest) < `pandita` (ordained priest) < `sulinggih` (high priest). An `undagi` is a master of traditional architecture; a `pangayah` is a voluntary temple worker.)*

1. Guru. -> Teacher.
2. Guru icang becik. -> My teacher is kind.
3. Nyama icang dadi guru. -> My sibling became a teacher.
4. Cai lakar dadi guru? -> Will you become a teacher?
5. Petani. -> Farmer.
6. Bapa icang petani. -> My father is a farmer.
7. Petani nika megawe di carik. -> That farmer works in the rice field.
8. Petani liu di desa nika. -> There are many farmers in that village.
9. Madul. -> To farm / work the fields (familiar).
10. Bapa icang madul. -> My father farms.
11. Cai taen madul? -> Have you ever farmed?
12. Ia madul di carik. -> He works in the rice field.
13. Nelayan. -> Fisherman (neutral).
14. Nelayan nika ngalih iwak. -> That fisherman looks for fish.
15. Nyama icang nelayan. -> My sibling is a fisherman.
16. Nelayan liu di pasih. -> There are many fishermen at the beach.
17. Bendega. -> Fisherman (refined).
18. Bendega nika ngrereh iwak. -> That fisherman seeks fish.
19. Bendega ipun saking desa nika. -> He is a fisherman from that village.
20. Tiang uning bendega nika. -> I know that fisherman.
21. Dagang. -> Merchant / seller.
22. Dagang nika ngadep buah. -> That merchant sells fruit.
23. Meme icang dagang di peken. -> My mother is a merchant at the market.
24. Dagang nika liu jinahne. -> That merchant has much money.
25. Sopir. -> Driver.
26. Sopir bemo nika nyen? -> Who is that bemo driver?
27. Bapa icang sopir. -> My father is a driver.
28. Sopir nika nyetir enggal. -> That driver drives fast.
29. Polisi. -> Police.
30. Ada polisi ditu. -> There is a police officer there.
31. Polisi nika nyen? -> Who is that police officer?
32. Nyama caine polisi? -> Is your sibling a police officer?
33. Tentara. -> Soldier.
34. Beli icang tentara. -> My older brother is a soldier.
35. Tentara nika kuat gati. -> That soldier is very strong.
36. Cai dot dadi tentara? -> Do you want to become a soldier?
37. Doktor. -> Doctor.
38. Doktor nika ring rumah sakit. -> The doctor is at the hospital.
39. Ipun dadi doktor. -> He / she became a doctor.
40. Doktor nika becik gati. -> That doctor is very kind.
41. Bidan. -> Midwife.
42. Bidan nika nulungin meme. -> The midwife helps mother.
43. Mbok icang bidan. -> My older sister is a midwife.
44. Bidan nika nyen? -> Who is that midwife?
45. Perawat. -> Nurse.
46. Perawat nika ring rumah sakit. -> The nurse is at the hospital.
47. Nyai lakar dadi perawat? -> Will you become a nurse?
48. Perawat nika sabar gati. -> That nurse is very patient.
49. Pandita. -> (Ordained) priest.
50. Pandita nika ring griya. -> The priest is at the priestly house.
51. Pandita nika sira? -> Who is that priest?
52. Tiang uning pandita nika. -> I know that priest.
53. Sulinggih. -> High priest (refined).
54. Sulinggih nika linggih ring griya. -> The high priest resides at the griya.
55. Ida sulinggih. -> He / she (honored) is a high priest.
56. Sulinggih nika lingsir gati. -> The high priest is very old.
57. Pemangku. -> Temple priest. [recycled from Lesson 28 — fresh sentences]
58. Pemangku nika nyen? -> Who is that temple priest?
59. Pemangku desa nika jemet. -> The village temple priest is diligent.
60. Cai taen matemu pemangku? -> Have you ever met a temple priest?
61. Undagi. -> Traditional architect / master craftsman.
62. Undagi nika ngaryanang jero. -> The master builder builds a house.
63. Undagi nika saking desa icang. -> That master builder is from my village.
64. Tiang dot dadi undagi. -> I want to become an undagi.
65. Tukang. -> Craftsman / handyman.
66. Tukang nika ngae meja. -> The craftsman makes a table.
67. Cai tukang apa? -> What kind of craftsman are you?
68. Tukang nika megawe di umah icang. -> The handyman works at my house.
69. Tukang banten. -> Offering maker (phrase).
70. Tukang banten nika nyen? -> Who is the offering maker?
71. Biang icang tukang banten. -> My mother is an offering maker.
72. Tukang banten nika jemet gati. -> The offering maker is very diligent.
73. Seniman. -> Artist.
74. Seniman Bali liu. -> There are many Balinese artists.
75. Seniman nika nyen? -> Who is that artist?
76. Tiang dot dadi seniman. -> I want to become an artist.
77. Pangayah. -> (Temple) volunteer worker.
78. Pangayah nika makarya ring pura. -> The volunteer works at the temple.
79. Krama banjar dadi pangayah. -> The community members become volunteers.
80. Pangayah nika liu jani. -> There are many volunteers now.
81. Bos. -> Boss.
82. Bos icang becik. -> My boss is good.
83. Bos caine nyen? -> Who is your boss?
84. Bos icang megawe di kantor. -> My boss works at the office.
85. Pegawai. -> Employee.
86. Pegawai nika megawe ring kantor. -> The employee works at the office.
87. Tiang pegawai. -> I am an employee.
88. Pegawai liu di kota. -> There are many employees in the city.
89. Megae. -> Working (familiar variant of megawe).
90. Icang megae jani. -> I'm working now.
91. Cai megae dija? -> Where do you work?
92. Ia megae di peken. -> He works at the market.
93. Makarya. -> To work (refined). [recycled from Lesson 11 — fresh sentences]
94. Ida makarya ring kantor. -> He / she (honored) works at the office.
95. Ipun makarya sareng tiang. -> He / she works with me.
96. Ida makarya becik. -> He / she (honored) works well.
97. Pagawean. -> Job / work (familiar noun). [recycled — fresh sentences]
98. Pagawean caine di kota? -> Is your job in the city?
99. Pagawean nika melah? -> Is that job good?
100. Icang dot pagawean baru. -> I want a new job.
101. Pakaryan. -> Job / work (refined noun). [recycled — fresh sentences]
102. Pakaryan ida ring kantor. -> His / her (honored) work is at the office.
103. Pakaryan ipun becik. -> His / her work is good.
104. Pakaryan ragane napi? -> What is your work?
105. Gajian. -> Salary / payday.
106. Jani gajian! -> It's payday now!
107. Gajian caine kuda? -> How much is your salary?
108. Gajian nika liu? -> Is the salary much?
109. Kantor. -> Office. [recycled from Lesson 12 — fresh sentences]
110. Kantor icang di kota. -> My office is in the city.
111. Cai megae ring kantor? -> Do you work at an office?
112. Kantor ida agung. -> His / her (honored) office is grand.
113. Nganggur. -> Unemployed / idle.
114. Ia nganggur jani. -> He is unemployed now.
115. Icang ten dot nganggur. -> I don't want to be unemployed.
116. Nganggur, dadi ia sedih. -> Unemployed — so he is sad.
117. Sibuk. -> Busy.
118. Icang sibuk jani. -> I'm busy now.
119. Cai sibuk megawe? -> Are you busy working?
120. Bos icang sibuk gati. -> My boss is very busy.
121. Repot. -> Busy (variant).
122. Icang repot jani. -> I'm busy now.
123. Repot gati ia megae. -> He is so busy working.
124. Meme repot di paon. -> Mother is busy in the kitchen.
125. Melali. -> To travel / go out for fun (familiar).
126. Icang lakar melali ka pasih. -> I will go for an outing to the beach.
127. Cai dot melali kija? -> Where do you want to go for fun?
128. Toris nika melali ring Bali. -> The tourist travels in Bali.

### Lesson 34: Opinions

**Focus:** thinking, agreeing/disagreeing, right/wrong, importance, difficulty/ease, sameness, "no wonder", try it, and "it tastes/feels like".
**Grammar note:** opinions open with `Tiang mapineh …` (familiar) / `Titiang mamanah …` (refined set phrase — standalone `titiang` arrives in Lesson 58, standalone `mapineh/mamanah` in Lesson 57). Agreement: `setuju`; disagreement: `sing setuju` / `nenten cumpu`.
**New in this lesson (teaching order):** pineh, manah, tiang mapineh, titiang mamanah, mirib (recycled — "similar"), minab (recycled), setuju, sing setuju, nenten cumpu, beneh (recycled), pelih (recycled), iwang (recycled), penting, buat (recycled — "important"), biasa, meweh, keweh, aluh, gampang, dangan, patuh, pateh, melah (recycled), jelek (recycled), pantes, tegarang, rasanne, seken (recycled — "really?"), patut (recycled), saja (recycled — "truly?"), ngawag

1. Pineh. -> Thought / to think (familiar).
2. Pineh icang, ia beneh. -> In my opinion, he is right.
3. Cai pineh apa? -> What do you think?
4. Pinehne becik. -> His / her thinking is good.
5. Manah. -> Thought / mind / opinion (refined).
6. Manah tiang, ida beneh. -> In my opinion, he / she (honored) is right.
7. Manah ragane napi? -> What is your opinion?
8. Manah ipun becik. -> His / her mind is good.
9. Tiang mapineh. -> I think (familiar phrase).
10. Tiang mapineh nika beneh. -> I think that is right.
11. Tiang mapineh cai bisa. -> I think you can.
12. Tiang mapineh ujan mani. -> I think it will rain tomorrow.
13. Titiang mamanah. -> I think (refined phrase).
14. Titiang mamanah nika patut. -> I think that is correct.
15. Titiang mamanah ida rauh. -> I think he / she (honored) will come.
16. Titiang mamanah, nika melah. -> I think that is good.
17. Mirib. -> Similar / looks like (familiar). [recycled from Lesson 32 — fresh sense]
18. Baju caine mirib baju icang. -> Your shirt looks like my shirt.
19. Ia mirib adin caine. -> He looks like your younger sibling.
20. Ia mirib gati lan beline. -> He looks very much like his older brother.
21. Minab. -> Maybe (refined). [recycled — fresh sentences]
22. Minab ida nenten rauh. -> Maybe he / she (honored) will not come.
23. Minab ujan benjang. -> Maybe it will rain tomorrow.
24. Minab cai beneh. -> Maybe you are right.
25. Setuju. -> To agree (neutral).
26. Icang setuju. -> I agree.
27. Cai setuju? -> Do you agree?
28. Tiang setuju sareng ragane. -> I agree with you.
29. Sing setuju. -> To disagree (familiar phrase).
30. Icang sing setuju. -> I disagree.
31. Cai sing setuju? -> Don't you agree?
32. Ia sing setuju ajak icang. -> He disagrees with me.
33. Nenten cumpu. -> To disagree (refined phrase).
34. Tiang nenten cumpu. -> I disagree.
35. Ragane nenten cumpu? -> Do you disagree?
36. Ipun nenten cumpu sareng tiang. -> He / she disagrees with me.
37. Beneh. -> Right / correct (familiar). [recycled from Lesson 1 — fresh sentences]
38. Beneh cai! -> You're right!
39. Nika beneh gati. -> That is very right.
40. Beneh, icang setuju. -> Right — I agree.
41. Pelih. -> Wrong (familiar). [recycled from Lesson 3 — fresh sentences]
42. Cai pelih dini. -> You are wrong here.
43. Sing setuju, nika pelih. -> I disagree — that is wrong.
44. Pelih gati pineh caine! -> Your thinking is so wrong!
45. Iwang. -> Wrong (refined). [recycled — fresh sentences]
46. Tiang iwang, sinampura. -> I was wrong — apologies.
47. Nika iwang, tiang nenten cumpu. -> That is wrong — I disagree.
48. Ipun iwang, nanging tiang sabar. -> He / she was wrong, but I am patient.
49. Penting. -> Important (neutral).
50. Nika penting gati. -> That is very important.
51. Malajah Basa Bali penting. -> Learning Balinese is important.
52. Pakaryan ida penting. -> His / her (honored) work is important.
53. Buat. -> Important / for (refined). [recycled from Lesson 26 — fresh sense]
54. Nika buat gati. -> That is very important.
55. Keluarga buat tiang. -> Family is important to me.
56. Buat ipun, pakaryan nika penting. -> For him / her, that work is important.
57. Biasa. -> Normal / usually (neutral).
58. Nika biasa. -> That's normal.
59. Icang biasa madaar nasi. -> I usually eat rice.
60. Biasa, cai majalan ka sekolah? -> Do you usually walk to school?
61. Meweh. -> Difficult (refined).
62. Basa Bali meweh? -> Is Balinese difficult?
63. Pagawean nika meweh gati. -> That work is very difficult.
64. Nika meweh, nanging tiang jemet. -> It is difficult, but I am diligent.
65. Keweh. -> Difficult (familiar).
66. Pagawean icang keweh. -> My work is hard.
67. Keweh gati, nanging icang nyidaang. -> It's hard, but I can (do it).
68. Cai ngrasa keweh? -> Do you feel it's difficult?
69. Aluh. -> Easy (familiar).
70. Basa Bali aluh! -> Balinese is easy!
71. Aluh gati pagawean nika. -> That work is so easy.
72. Nika aluh baang icang. -> That is easy for me.
73. Gampang. -> Easy (neutral).
74. Nika gampang. -> That's easy.
75. Gampang gati malajah Basa Bali. -> Learning Balinese is so easy.
76. Cai ngrasa gampang? -> Do you feel it's easy?
77. Dangan. -> Easy (refined).
78. Nika dangan. -> That is easy.
79. Dangan gati pakaryan ipun. -> His / her work is so easy.
80. Malajah nika dangan baang tiang. -> Studying is easy for me.
81. Patuh. -> Same (familiar).
82. Baju icang lan baju caine patuh. -> My shirt and your shirt are the same.
83. Patuh gati, nggih! -> So alike, yes!
84. Umah icang patuh lan umah caine. -> My house is the same as yours.
85. Pateh. -> Same (refined).
86. Buku tiang lan buku ragane pateh. -> My book and your book are the same.
87. Pateh gati, nggih. -> Exactly the same, yes.
88. Manah tiang pateh lan manah ragane. -> My opinion is the same as yours.
89. Melah. -> Nice / good (familiar). [recycled from Lesson 19 — fresh sentences]
90. Pineh caine melah. -> Your idea is good.
91. Gatra nika melah. -> That news is good.
92. Pagawean caine melah. -> Your work is good.
93. Jelek. -> Bad / ugly (familiar). [recycled from Lesson 15 — fresh sentences]
94. Pineh nika jelek. -> That idea is bad.
95. Jelek gati, icang sing setuju. -> So bad — I disagree.
96. Nika jelek baang icang. -> That is bad for me.
97. Pantes. -> Appropriate / no wonder (neutral).
98. Pantes ia kenyel, ia megawe suwe. -> No wonder he's tired — he worked long.
99. Nika pantes. -> That's appropriate.
100. Pantes gati ia dadi guru. -> No wonder he is a teacher.
101. Tegarang. -> Try it / test it (familiar).
102. Tegarang kopi niki! -> Try this coffee!
103. Tegarang malajah Basa Bali! -> Try learning Balinese!
104. Tegarang, cai nyidaang! -> Try it — you can!
105. Rasanne. -> It tastes / feels like… (phrase).
106. Rasanne jaen gati! -> It tastes so delicious!
107. Rasanne keweh. -> It feels difficult.
108. Jukut niki rasanne jaen. -> These vegetables taste delicious.
109. Seken? -> Really? (familiar). [recycled — question sense]
110. Seken? Cai suba taen ka Jawa? -> Really? You've been to Java?
111. Seken nika? -> Is that true?
112. Seken? Ia dadi doktor? -> Really? He became a doctor?
113. Ngawag. -> Careless / random (familiar).
114. Cai ngae ngawag! -> You work carelessly!
115. Ngawag gati pagawean caine! -> Your work is so careless!
116. Ia megae ngawag, dadi jelek. -> He works carelessly, so it turns out bad.
117. Patut. -> Correct (refined). [recycled from Lesson 1 — fresh sentences]
118. Nika patut gati. -> That is very correct.
119. Patut, tiang setuju. -> Correct — I agree.
120. Manah ragane patut. -> Your opinion is right.
121. Saja? -> Truly? (familiar). [recycled from Lesson 1 — question sense]
122. Saja? Gajian caine liu? -> Really? Is your salary big?
123. Saja nika? -> Is that truly so?
124. Saja, icang setuju! -> Truly — I agree!

### Lesson 35: Culture 1 — Ceremonies

**Focus:** temple ceremony vocabulary — offerings, holy days, prayer, holy water, the seen/unseen worlds, deities, and the kulkul drum.
**Cultural note:** `kajeng kliwon` and `nyepi` are the two most famous calendar moments; `sekala` (the visible world) and `niskala` (the unseen world) frame the Balinese cosmos; `ngayah` is joyful unpaid community work for the temple; the `kulkul` slit-drum summons the village.
**New in this lesson (teaching order):** upacara, yadnya, odalan, rerahinan, kajeng kliwon, nyepi, ogoh-ogoh, canang, canang sari, banten, mebanten, sasajen, gebogan, penjor, muspa, mebakti, sembahyang, dupa, mantra, tirta, toya tirta, nunas pica, prasad, ngayah, suci, sekala, niskala, bhatara, sasuhunan, kulkul (recycled — new sense "slit drum"), mepamit (recycled)
*(Note: `kulkul` meant "thunder" in Lesson 23; here it is the wooden slit drum of the village pavilion — context decides. `muspa` = praying with flowers held before the face; `mebakti`/`sembahyang` = general praying.)*

1. Upacara. -> Ceremony (neutral).
2. Ada upacara di pura. -> There is a ceremony at the temple.
3. Upacara nika gede. -> The ceremony is big.
4. Upacara nika pidan? -> When is the ceremony?
5. Yadnya. -> Sacred sacrifice / ritual (neutral).
6. Ada yadnya ring desa. -> There is a ritual in the village.
7. Yadnya nika pidan? -> When is the ritual?
8. Krama desa ngae yadnya. -> The villagers perform the ritual.
9. Odalan. -> Temple anniversary ceremony.
10. Odalan ring pura niki pidan? -> When is this temple's odalan?
11. Ada odalan jani. -> There is an odalan now.
12. Odalan nika becik gati. -> The odalan is very beautiful.
13. Rerahinan. -> Holy day (refined).
14. Rerahinan niki napi? -> What holy day is this?
15. Jani rerahinan. -> Today is a holy day.
16. Rerahinan niki, krama ka pura. -> On this holy day, the people go to the temple.
17. Kajeng kliwon. -> Spiritually charged day (phrase).
18. Jani kajeng kliwon. -> Today is kajeng kliwon.
19. Kajeng kliwon, icang jumah. -> Kajeng kliwon — I stay home.
20. Kajeng kliwon pidan? -> When is kajeng kliwon?
21. Nyepi. -> Day of Silence.
22. Nyepi benjang. -> Nyepi is tomorrow.
23. Nyepi, krama nenten megawe. -> Nyepi — nobody works.
24. Cai jumah di Nyepi? -> Are you home on Nyepi?
25. Ogoh-ogoh. -> Giant demon effigy.
26. Ada ogoh-ogoh ditu! -> There's an ogoh-ogoh there!
27. Ogoh-ogoh nika gede gati! -> That ogoh-ogoh is so big!
28. Ogoh-ogoh nika nyen ngae? -> Who made that ogoh-ogoh?
29. Canang. -> Small offering tray.
30. Ada canang di merajan. -> There are canang at the family temple.
31. Meme ngae canang. -> Mother makes canang.
32. Canang nika melah. -> The offering is beautiful.
33. Canang sari. -> Daily flower offering (phrase).
34. Meme ngae canang sari semeng. -> Mother makes canang sari in the morning.
35. Canang sari niki melah gati. -> This canang sari is so beautiful.
36. Canang sari ada di ajeng jero. -> There is a canang sari in front of the house.
37. Banten. -> (Elaborate) offering (neutral).
38. Ada banten di pura. -> There are offerings at the temple.
39. Biang tiang ngae banten. -> My mother makes offerings.
40. Banten nika gede. -> The offering is big.
41. Mebanten. -> To make / perform offerings.
42. Meme mebanten semeng. -> Mother performs the offerings in the morning.
43. Cai taen mebanten? -> Have you ever done mebanten?
44. Krama desa mebanten ring pura. -> The villagers make offerings at the temple.
45. Sasajen. -> Offerings (neutral).
46. Ada sasajen di merajan. -> There are offerings at the family temple.
47. Sasajen nika nyen ngae? -> Who made those offerings?
48. Sasajen niki melah. -> These offerings are beautiful.
49. Gebogan. -> Tall fruit offering.
50. Gebogan nika tegeh gati! -> That gebogan is so tall!
51. Nyai ngae gebogan? -> Do you make gebogan?
52. Gebogan nika akeh buah. -> That gebogan holds much fruit.
53. Penjor. -> Decorated bamboo pole.
54. Ada penjor di harep jero. -> There is a penjor in front of the house.
55. Penjor nika tegeh. -> The penjor is tall.
56. Penjor liu di jalan jani. -> There are many penjor on the road now.
57. Muspa. -> To pray (with flowers) (refined).
58. Tiang muspa ring pura. -> I pray at the temple.
59. Ida muspa. -> He / she (honored) prays.
60. Krama muspa ring merajan. -> The people pray at the family temple.
61. Mebakti. -> To pray / worship.
62. Icang mebakti di merajan. -> I pray at the family temple.
63. Cai mebakti pidan? -> When do you pray?
64. Krama mebakti ring pura jani. -> The people are praying at the temple now.
65. Sembahyang. -> To pray (neutral).
66. Icang sembahyang. -> I pray.
67. Nyai sembahyang dija? -> Where do you pray?
68. Sembahyang ring pura, nggih. -> Pray at the temple, yes.
69. Dupa. -> Incense.
70. Ada dupa di merajan. -> There is incense at the family temple.
71. Tiang meli dupa. -> I buy incense.
72. Dupa lan bunga ada dini. -> Incense and flowers are here.
73. Mantra. -> Prayer / chant.
74. Pandita nika maosang mantra. -> The priest recites mantras.
75. Mantra nika basa napi? -> What language is that mantra in?
76. Icang mireng mantra. -> I hear the mantra.
77. Tirta. -> Holy water.
78. Tirta nika ring dija? -> Where is the holy water?
79. Pandita ngicen tirta. -> The priest gives holy water.
80. Tirta nika toya becik. -> The holy water is good water.
81. Toya tirta. -> Holy water (refined phrase).
82. Toya tirta nika ring pura. -> The holy water is at the temple.
83. Pandita ngicen toya tirta. -> The priest gives holy water.
84. Toya tirta wénten ring merajan. -> There is holy water at the family temple.
85. Nunas pica. -> To ask for a blessing (refined phrase).
86. Tiang nunas pica ring pandita. -> I ask the priest for a blessing.
87. Krama nunas pica. -> The people ask for blessings.
88. Nunas pica, nggih. -> May I ask for a blessing, yes.
89. Prasad. -> Blessed food (neutral).
90. Prasad nika jaen. -> The blessed food is delicious.
91. Ada prasad ring pura. -> There is blessed food at the temple.
92. Prasad nika baang krama. -> The blessed food is for the people.
93. Ngayah. -> To do voluntary temple work.
94. Krama ngayah ring pura. -> The people do voluntary work at the temple.
95. Cai taen ngayah? -> Have you ever done ngayah?
96. Tiang ngayah jani. -> I am doing ngayah now.
97. Suci. -> Holy / pure.
98. Tirta nika suci. -> The holy water is pure.
99. Pura nika genah suci. -> That temple is a holy place.
100. Suci gati genah nika. -> That place is so holy.
101. Sekala. -> The visible world (neutral).
102. Gumi niki sekala. -> This world is the visible realm.
103. Tiang ningal ring sekala. -> I see within the visible world.
104. Ring sekala, tiang ningal pura. -> In the visible world, I see the temple.
105. Niskala. -> The unseen world (neutral).
106. Niskala nika apa? -> What is the unseen world?
107. Sekala lan niskala ada. -> The seen and the unseen exist.
108. Niskala nika suci. -> The unseen realm is holy.
109. Bhatara. -> God / deity.
110. Bhatara nika ring pura. -> The deity is at the temple.
111. Krama mebakti ring bhatara. -> The people worship the deity.
112. Dumogi bhatara ngicen rahayu. -> May the deity grant safety.
113. Sasuhunan. -> Revered deity (refined).
114. Sasuhunan nika ring dija? -> Where is the revered deity?
115. Krama muspa ring sasuhunan. -> The people pray to the revered deity.
116. Sasuhunan desa nika suci. -> The village's revered deity is holy.
117. Kulkul. -> Wooden slit drum. [recycled from Lesson 23 "thunder" — new sense]
118. Icang ningeh kulkul. -> I hear the kulkul drum.
119. Kulkul nika ring dija? -> Where is that kulkul?
120. Krama ningeh kulkul, raris ka banjar. -> The people hear the kulkul, then go to the banjar.
121. Mepamit. -> To take leave (refined). [recycled from Lesson 2 — fresh sentences]
122. Tiang mepamit ring pandita. -> I take leave of the priest.
123. Sesampun upacara, krama mepamit. -> After the ceremony, the people take leave.
124. Ida mepamit, dumogi rahayu. -> He / she (honored) takes leave — blessings.

---

### Lesson 36: Descriptive Adjectives

**Focus:** cleanliness and (im)purity, crowds and quiet, light and dark, the five tastes, first-time experiences, amazement, width, thickness, texture, wet/dry.
**Grammar note:** taste follows the food word: `Kopi niki pait.` "This coffee (is) bitter." `Tumben` marks a first: `Tumben icang ka pura niki.` "First time I'm at this temple."
**New in this lesson (teaching order):** kedas (recycled), bersih (recycled), daki (recycled), kotor (recycled), cemer (recycled), rame, sepi (recycled), sunar, galang, peteng (recycled — "dark"), remrem, anyep, lalah, manis, pait, masem, pakeh, tumben, bengong, linggah, cupit, kandel, tipis, abot, baat, inggan, ingan, alus, kasar, belus, tuh (recycled)
*(Note: `cemer` is not mere dirt — it is ritual impurity. `peteng` covered "night" in Lesson 2; here it is the adjective "dark".)*

1. Kedas. -> Clean (familiar). [recycled from Lesson 20 — fresh sentences]
2. Jumah icang kedas jani. -> My house is clean now.
3. Kamar caine kedas? -> Is your room clean?
4. Kedas gati natah jero ida. -> His / her (honored) courtyard is so clean.
5. Bersih. -> Clean (neutral). [recycled — fresh sentences]
6. Pasar nika bersih. -> That market is clean.
7. Toya niki bersih. -> This water is clean.
8. Bersih gati pasih nika. -> That beach is so clean.
9. Daki. -> Dirty (familiar). [recycled — fresh sentences]
10. Sandal icang daki. -> My sandals are dirty.
11. Daki gati jalan nika. -> That road is so dirty.
12. Tangan caine daki, kumbah! -> Your hands are dirty — wash them!
13. Kotor. -> Dirty (neutral). [recycled — fresh sentences]
14. Natah nika kotor. -> The courtyard is dirty.
15. Kotor gati kamar caine! -> Your room is so dirty!
16. Cicing ento kotor. -> That dog is dirty.
17. Cemer. -> Ritually impure / defiled. [recycled from Lesson 19 — fresh sentences]
18. Genah nika cemer. -> That place is ritually impure.
19. Cemer, nenten dados ka pura. -> (It is) impure — (one) may not enter the temple.
20. Genah nika cemer, tiang ajrih. -> That place is impure — I am afraid.
21. Rame. -> Crowded / lively (familiar).
22. Peken nika rame. -> The market is crowded.
23. Rame gati kota jani! -> The city is so lively now!
24. Cai dot ka tongos rame? -> Do you want to go to a crowded place?
25. Sepi. -> Quiet / deserted. [recycled from Lesson 16 — fresh sentences]
26. Desa nika sepi. -> That village is quiet.
27. Sepi gati pura nika jani. -> The temple is so quiet now.
28. Tiang demen genah sepi. -> I like quiet places.
29. Sunar. -> Bright (familiar).
30. Matan ai sunar gati. -> The sun is so bright.
31. Langit sunar jani. -> The sky is bright now.
32. Kamar icang sunar. -> My room is bright.
33. Galang. -> Bright / light (familiar).
34. Galang jani, suba semeng. -> It's bright now — it's already morning.
35. Galang gati wai niki. -> Today is so bright.
36. Bulan purnama, galang. -> Full moon — bright.
37. Peteng. -> Dark. [recycled from Lesson 2 "night" — adjective sense]
38. Gua nika peteng gati. -> That cave is so dark.
39. Peteng, icang jejeh. -> It's dark — I'm scared.
40. Wengi niki peteng, tilem. -> Tonight is dark — dark moon.
41. Remrem. -> Gloomy / overcast (familiar).
42. Langit remrem jani. -> The sky is gloomy now.
43. Remrem, mirib lakar ujan. -> Gloomy — it looks like rain.
44. Cuacane remrem. -> The weather is overcast.
45. Anyep. -> Tasteless / bland (familiar).
46. Dedaaran niki anyep. -> This food is bland.
47. Anyep, uyahne bedik. -> Bland — too little salt.
48. Jukut nika anyep? -> Are those vegetables bland?
49. Lalah. -> Spicy (familiar).
50. Tabia nika lalah gati! -> That chili is so spicy!
51. Dedaaran niki lalah. -> This food is spicy.
52. Cai demen lalah? -> Do you like spicy food?
53. Manis. -> Sweet (neutral).
54. Gula nika manis. -> Sugar is sweet.
55. Buah niki manis gati. -> This fruit is so sweet.
56. Kopi caine manis? -> Is your coffee sweet?
57. Pait. -> Bitter (neutral).
58. Kopi niki pait. -> This coffee is bitter.
59. Kopi nika pait, icang dot gula. -> That coffee is bitter — I want sugar.
60. Jukut nika pait? -> Are those vegetables bitter?
61. Masem. -> Sour (neutral).
62. Buah niki masem. -> This fruit is sour.
63. Masem gati, icang nenten demen. -> So sour — I don't like it.
64. Kopi nika masem? -> Is that coffee sour?
65. Pakeh. -> Salty (familiar).
66. Dedaaran niki pakeh. -> This food is salty.
67. Pakeh gati, uyahne liu! -> So salty — too much salt!
68. Be nika pakeh? -> Is that fish salty?
69. Tumben. -> For the first time / rare (familiar).
70. Icang tumben ka pura niki. -> It's my first time at this temple.
71. Tumben cai teka dini! -> First time you've come here!
72. Tumben icang ngrasa lalah gati. -> First time I've tasted something so spicy.
73. Bengong. -> Amazed / staring blankly (familiar).
74. Icang bengong ningal ogoh-ogoh! -> I was amazed seeing the ogoh-ogoh!
75. Cai bengong kenapa? -> Why are you staring blankly?
76. Bengong ia ningal pura nika. -> He was amazed looking at that temple.
77. Linggah. -> Wide / spacious (familiar).
78. Natah nika linggah. -> The courtyard is spacious.
79. Linggah gati carik caine! -> Your rice field is so wide!
80. Jalan nika linggah? -> Is that road wide?
81. Cupit. -> Narrow (familiar).
82. Rurung nika cupit. -> That alley is narrow.
83. Cupit gati jalan nika. -> That road is so narrow.
84. Kamar icang cupit. -> My room is narrow.
85. Kandel. -> Thick (neutral).
86. Buku nika kandel. -> That book is thick.
87. Tembok nika kandel gati. -> That wall is so thick.
88. Kulit sampi nika kandel. -> That cowhide is thick.
89. Tipis. -> Thin (neutral).
90. Buku icang tipis. -> My book is thin.
91. Tipis gati kamen nika. -> That sarong is so thin.
92. Tipis, nenten kandel. -> Thin, not thick.
93. Alus. -> Smooth / fine / refined (neutral).
94. Wastra nika alus. -> That cloth is fine.
95. Alus gati kulit ipun. -> His / her skin is so smooth.
96. Basa idane alus. -> His / her speech is refined.
97. Kasar. -> Rough (neutral).
98. Kulit icang kasar. -> My skin is rough.
99. Jalan nika kasar. -> That road is rough.
100. Kasar, nenten alus. -> Rough, not smooth.
101. Belus. -> Wet (familiar).
102. Baju icang belus. -> My shirt is wet.
103. Belus gati, ujan bales! -> So wet — heavy rain!
104. Bok icang belus, icang mara manjus. -> My hair is wet — I just bathed.
105. Tuh. -> Dry. [recycled from Lesson 23 — fresh sentences]
106. Baju icang suba tuh. -> My shirt is already dry.
107. Tuh gati jani, nenten ujan. -> It's so dry now — no rain.
108. Rambut icang tuh. -> My hair is dry.
109. Abot. -> Heavy (refined).
110. Buku nika abot. -> That book is heavy.
111. Batu nika abot gati. -> That stone is very heavy.
112. Abot, tiang ten nyidaang ngaba. -> Heavy — I can't carry it.
113. Baat. -> Heavy (familiar).
114. Buku icang baat. -> My book is heavy.
115. Baat gati batu ento! -> That stone is so heavy!
116. Cai nyidaang ngaba nika? Baat! -> Can you carry that? It's heavy!
117. Inggan. -> Light in weight (refined).
118. Buku nika inggan. -> That book is light.
119. Inggan gati, tiang nyidaang ngaba. -> So light — I can carry it.
120. Kamben nika inggan. -> That sarong is light.
121. Ingan. -> Light in weight (familiar).
122. Sampat nika ingan. -> That broom is light.
123. Ingan gati helm caine! -> Your helmet is so light!
124. Ingan, nenten baat. -> Light, not heavy.

---

### Lesson 37: Daily Life

**Focus:** household chores, cooking, arts of singing and dancing, reading/writing/speaking, resting, strolling, playing — the verbs of an ordinary Balinese day.
**New in this lesson (teaching order):** ngedasang, nyampat (recycled), ngepel, numbah, ngumbah, nyetrika, meliang, memasak, ngrateng, megae (recycled), ngajeng (recycled), meyasa, megending, ngigel, nari, mabalih, miarsayang, maca, ngwacen, nulis, nyurat, ngomong, mabaos, ngoyong, majalan-jalan, masandekan, mareren (recycled — "to rest"), nyemak gawe, nulungin meme, nyeduh kopi, maen
*(Notes: `numbah`/`ngumbah` = wash clothes; `nyetrika` = iron. `ngigel` (familiar) vs `nari` (refined) = to dance; watching a performance is `mabalih`/`miarsayang`. `meyasa` = spiritual self-cultivation (fasting/meditation).)*

1. Ngedasang. -> To clean (familiar).
2. Icang ngedasang jumah. -> I clean the house.
3. Nyai ngedasang kamar? -> Do you clean the room?
4. Ia ngedasang paon. -> He cleans the kitchen.
5. Nyampat. -> To sweep. [recycled from Lesson 27 — fresh sentences]
6. Icang nyampat di jumah. -> I sweep the house.
7. Meme nyampat semeng. -> Mother sweeps in the morning.
8. Nyampat jani, cai! -> Sweep now, you!
9. Ngepel. -> To mop (neutral).
10. Icang ngepel kamar. -> I mop the room.
11. Nyai ngepel dija? -> Where do you mop?
12. Suba ngepel? -> Have you mopped yet?
13. Numbah. -> To wash clothes (familiar variant).
14. Meme numbah baju. -> Mother washes clothes.
15. Icang numbah kamen. -> I wash the sarong.
16. Cai numbah pidan? -> When do you do the laundry?
17. Ngumbah. -> To wash clothes (familiar).
18. Icang ngumbah baju di tukad. -> I wash clothes at the river.
19. Nyai ngumbah jani? -> Are you washing now?
20. Meme ngumbah kamen lan baju. -> Mother washes sarongs and clothes.
21. Nyetrika. -> To iron (neutral).
22. Icang nyetrika baju. -> I iron the shirt.
23. Nyai nyetrika kamen? -> Do you iron the sarong?
24. Nyetrika jani, mani nganggo. -> Iron now, wear (it) tomorrow.
25. Meliang. -> To buy (something) for someone (familiar).
26. Bapa meliang icang buku. -> Father buys me a book.
27. Icang meliang adi baju. -> I buy my younger sibling a shirt.
28. Meme meliang icang jukut. -> Mother buys vegetables for me.
29. Memasak. -> To cook (neutral).
30. Meme memasak di paon. -> Mother cooks in the kitchen.
31. Nyai bisa memasak? -> Can you cook?
32. Ia memasak nasi. -> He cooks rice.
33. Ngrateng. -> To cook (refined).
34. Biang tiang ngrateng ring pewaregan. -> My mother cooks in the kitchen.
35. Ipun ngrateng ajengan. -> He / she cooks food.
36. Ida ngrateng napi? -> What is he / she (honored) cooking?
37. Megae. -> To work. [recycled from Lesson 33 — fresh sentences]
38. Bapa megae di kantor. -> Father works at the office.
39. Cai megae dibi? -> Did you work yesterday?
40. Ia lakar megae benjang. -> He will work tomorrow.
41. Ngajeng. -> To eat (refined, for respected others). [recycled from Lesson 5 — fresh sentences]
42. Ida ngajeng ring jero. -> He / she (honored) eats at home.
43. Ipun durung ngajeng jani. -> He / she hasn't eaten yet now.
44. Ragane sampun ngajeng? -> Have you eaten?
45. Meyasa. -> To fast / meditate / cultivate oneself (refined).
46. Ida meyasa ring pura. -> He / she (honored) meditates at the temple.
47. Pandita nika meyasa. -> The priest practices spiritual discipline.
48. Ipun meyasa ring griya. -> He / she practices at the griya.
49. Megending. -> To sing (familiar).
50. Nyai megending melah gati! -> You sing so beautifully!
51. Ia megending di bale. -> He sings at the pavilion.
52. Icang dot megending. -> I want to sing.
53. Ngigel. -> To dance (familiar).
54. Bajang nika ngigel, jegeg gati. -> That young woman dances — so beautiful.
55. Cai bisa ngigel? -> Can you dance?
56. Ia ngigel ring pura. -> He dances at the temple.
57. Nari. -> To dance (refined).
58. Ipun nari ring pura. -> He / she dances at the temple.
59. Ida nari becik gati. -> He / she (honored) dances very beautifully.
60. Ragane bisa nari? -> Can you dance?
61. Mabalih. -> To watch (a show) (familiar).
62. Icang mabalih ngigel di banjar. -> I watch a dance at the banjar.
63. Cai mabalih apa? -> What are you watching?
64. Ia mabalih ogoh-ogoh. -> He watches the ogoh-ogoh.
65. Miarsayang. -> To watch (refined).
66. Ipun miarsayang upacara ring pura. -> He / she watches the ceremony at the temple.
67. Ida miarsayang, nggih. -> He / she (honored) watches, yes.
68. Ragane miarsayang napi? -> What are you watching?
69. Maca. -> To read (familiar).
70. Icang maca buku. -> I read a book.
71. Cai maca apa? -> What are you reading?
72. Guru maca buku di sekolah. -> The teacher reads a book at school.
73. Ngwacen. -> To read (refined).
74. Tiang ngwacen buku. -> I read a book.
75. Ida ngwacen ring griya. -> He / she (honored) reads at the griya.
76. Ipun ngwacen napi? -> What is he / she reading?
77. Nulis. -> To write (familiar).
78. Icang nulis adan icang. -> I write my name.
79. Cai nulis apa? -> What are you writing?
80. Ia nulis di sekolah. -> He writes at school.
81. Nyurat. -> To write (refined).
82. Tiang nyurat wastan tiang. -> I write my name.
83. Ida nyurat napi? -> What is he / she (honored) writing?
84. Ipun nyurat ring buku. -> He / she writes in a book.
85. Ngomong. -> To talk / speak (familiar).
86. Cai ngomong Basa Bali? -> Do you speak Balinese?
87. Icang ngomong ajak timpal icang. -> I talk with my friend.
88. Ia ngomong liu gati. -> He talks so much.
89. Mabaos. -> To speak (refined).
90. Ida mabaos sareng pandita. -> He / she (honored) speaks with the priest.
91. Tiang mabaos Basa Bali. -> I speak Balinese.
92. Ipun mabaos alus. -> He / she speaks in a refined way.
93. Ngoyong. -> To stay / be quiet (familiar).
94. Icang ngoyong jumah. -> I stay at home.
95. Cai ngoyong dini, nggih! -> You stay here, okay!
96. Ia ngoyong, ten ngomong. -> He stays silent, doesn't talk.
97. Majalan-jalan. -> To stroll / walk around (familiar).
98. Icang majalan-jalan ka pasih. -> I stroll to the beach.
99. Nyai dot majalan-jalan? -> Do you want to go for a stroll?
100. Sanja, krama majalan-jalan. -> In the evening, people take strolls.
101. Masandekan. -> To rest (refined).
102. Tiang masandekan. -> I rest.
103. Ida masandekan ring jero. -> He / she (honored) rests at home.
104. Ipun kenyel, ipun masandekan. -> He / she is tired — he rests.
105. Mareren. -> To rest / stop. [recycled from Lesson 25 — "rest" sense]
106. Icang mareren di bale. -> I rest at the pavilion.
107. Cai mareren, nggih. -> You rest, okay.
108. Ia kenyel, ia mareren. -> He's tired — he rests.
109. Nyemak gawe. -> To take on chores / work (phrase).
110. Icang nyemak gawe di jumah. -> I take on chores at home.
111. Cai nyemak gawe apa? -> What work do you take on?
112. Meme nyemak gawe liu. -> Mother takes on a lot of work.
113. Nulungin meme. -> To help mother (phrase).
114. Icang nulungin meme di paon. -> I help mother in the kitchen.
115. Nyai nulungin meme? -> Do you help mother?
116. Ia nulungin meme nyampat. -> He helps mother sweep.
117. Nyeduh kopi. -> To brew coffee (phrase).
118. Icang nyeduh kopi semeng. -> I brew coffee in the morning.
119. Cai nyeduh kopi baang icang? -> Do you brew coffee for me?
120. Meme nyeduh kopi baang bapa. -> Mother brews coffee for father.
121. Maen. -> To play (familiar).
122. Panak icang maen di natah. -> My child plays in the courtyard.
123. Cai maen ajak nyen? -> Whom do you play with?
124. Ia maen lan timpalne. -> He plays with his friend.

---

### Lesson 38: Directions 2 — Navigation

**Focus:** giving and following directions — turning, going straight, the refined cardinal sides (dauh/dangin/delod/daja), passing, junctions, landmarks (hill, beach, harbor, airport), getting lost, and asking.
**Cultural note:** `dauh` (west), `dangin` (east), `delod` (south/seaward), `daja` (north/mountainward) are the refined forms of kauh/kangin/kelod/kaja — they combine with nouns: `dauh pura` "west of the temple".
**New in this lesson (teaching order):** belok, menggok, kiwa (recycled), tengen (recycled), lurus, terus, dauh, dangin, delod, daja, ngeliwat, simpangan (recycled), pempatan, pertigaan, bukit, pantai, pelabuhan, bandara, terminal (recycled), nampek (recycled), paek (recycled), adoh (recycled), joh (recycled), nyabrang (recycled), di samping, di harep, di duri, nyasar, takon, nakenang

1. Belok. -> To turn (neutral).
2. Belok ka kiwa! -> Turn left!
3. Belok ka tengen, warung nika drika. -> Turn right — the stall is there.
4. Cai belok dija? -> Where do you turn?
5. Menggok. -> To turn (familiar).
6. Menggok ka tengen! -> Turn right!
7. Ia menggok ka kiwa. -> He turns left.
8. Menggok dini, nggih. -> Turn here, okay.
9. Kiwa. -> Left. [recycled from Lesson 13 — fresh sentences]
10. Kiwa nika jalan ka pura. -> To the left is the road to the temple.
11. Toko nika di kiwa. -> That store is on the left.
12. Menggok kiwa, ada peken. -> Turn left — there's a market.
13. Tengen. -> Right. [recycled — fresh sentences]
14. Tengen nika jalan ka sekolah. -> To the right is the road to the school.
15. Belok ka tengen, ada banjar. -> Turn right — there's a banjar.
16. Tengen, nenten kiwa, nggih! -> Right, not left, okay!
17. Lurus. -> Straight (neutral).
18. Jalan lurus, nggih. -> Go straight, okay.
19. Margi nika lurus. -> That road is straight.
20. Terus. -> Straight on / continue (neutral).
21. Terus, warung nika di tengen. -> Keep going — the stall is on the right.
22. Terus, nenten belok. -> Straight on, no turning.
23. Cai terus ka dija? -> Where are you continuing to?
24. Dauh. -> West side (refined direction).
25. Umah icang dauh pura. -> My house is west of the temple.
26. Toko nika dauh. -> That store is on the west side.
27. Dauh peken, ada warung. -> West of the market, there is a stall.
28. Dangin. -> East side (refined direction).
29. Jero ida dangin pura. -> His / her (honored) home is east of the temple.
30. Sekolah nika dangin peken. -> The school is east of the market.
31. Ada warung dangin jalan. -> There is a stall east of the road.
32. Delod. -> South side / seaward (refined direction).
33. Pasih nika delod desa. -> The beach is south of the village.
34. Delod nika segara. -> To the south is the sea.
35. Umah icang delod banjar. -> My house is south of the banjar.
36. Daja. -> North side / mountainward (refined direction).
37. Gunung nika daja. -> The mountain is to the north.
38. Daja nika wukir. -> To the north are the mountains.
39. Pura nika daja desa. -> The temple is north of the village.
40. Ngeliwat. -> To pass by (familiar).
41. Icang ngeliwat jeron caine. -> I pass by your house.
42. Cai ngeliwat peken? -> Do you pass the market?
43. Ia ngeliwat dini semeng. -> He passes here in the morning.
44. Simpangan. -> Intersection. [recycled from Lesson 13 — fresh sentences]
45. Belok ka kiwa di simpangan. -> Turn left at the intersection.
46. Simpangan nika nampek? -> Is the intersection near?
47. Di simpangan, terus. -> At the intersection, go straight.
48. Pempatan. -> Crossroads (familiar).
49. Ada pempatan ditu. -> There is a crossroads there.
50. Pempatan nika dija? -> Where is the crossroads?
51. Menggok ka tengen di pempatan. -> Turn right at the crossroads.
52. Pertigaan. -> T-junction (neutral).
53. Pertigaan nika paek di banjar. -> The T-junction is near the banjar.
54. Di pertigaan, belok ka kiwa. -> At the T-junction, turn left.
55. Pertigaan nika dija? -> Where is the T-junction?
56. Bukit. -> Hill (neutral).
57. Ada bukit ditu. -> There is a hill there.
58. Bukit nika ijo gati. -> That hill is so green.
59. Umah caine di bukit? -> Is your house on the hill?
60. Pantai. -> Beach (neutral).
61. Pantai nika melah gati. -> The beach is so beautiful.
62. Toris liu di pantai. -> There are many tourists at the beach.
63. Icang lakar melali ka pantai. -> I will go for an outing to the beach.
64. Pelabuhan. -> Harbor (neutral).
65. Pelabuhan nika dija? -> Where is the harbor?
66. Kapal liu di pelabuhan. -> There are many ships at the harbor.
67. Icang ka pelabuhan, munggah kapal. -> I go to the harbor and board a ship.
68. Bandara. -> Airport (neutral).
69. Bandara nika dija? -> Where is the airport?
70. Toris rauh nganggen pesawat ka bandara. -> Tourists arrive by plane at the airport.
71. Tiang ka bandara benjang. -> I go to the airport tomorrow.
72. Terminal. -> Bus terminal. [recycled from Lesson 25 — fresh sentences]
73. Terminal nika nampek di simpangan. -> The terminal is near the intersection.
74. Bemo liu di terminal. -> There are many bemos at the terminal.
75. Icang ka terminal, numpak bemo ka kota. -> I go to the terminal and take a bemo to the city.
76. Nampek. -> Near (refined). [recycled from Lesson 13 — fresh sentences]
77. Jero tiang nampek ring pura. -> My home is near the temple.
78. Nampek, nenten adoh. -> Near, not far.
79. Sekolah nika nampek ring jero ida. -> The school is near his / her (honored) home.
80. Paek. -> Near (familiar). [recycled — fresh sentences]
81. Warung nika paek di jumah. -> The stall is near home.
82. Paek, nenten joh. -> Near, not far.
83. Cai nongos paek di sekolah? -> Do you live near the school?
84. Adoh. -> Far (refined). [recycled — fresh sentences]
85. Kota nika adoh gati. -> The city is very far.
86. Adoh, tiang nenten lakar mrika. -> Far — I will not go there.
87. Bandara nika adoh ring driki? -> Is the airport far from here?
88. Joh. -> Far (familiar). [recycled — fresh sentences]
89. Pasih nika joh di desa icang. -> The beach is far from my village.
90. Joh gati, icang ngoyong jumah. -> It's so far — I stay home.
91. Cai lakar kema? Joh ento! -> You'll go there? That's far!
92. Nyabrang. -> To cross. [recycled from Lesson 25 — fresh sentences]
93. Icang nyabrang jalan ka peken. -> I cross the road to the market.
94. Cai nyabrang di pempatan? -> Do you cross at the crossroads?
95. Nyabrang tukad nika dadi? -> Can one cross that river?
96. Di samping. -> Beside (phrase).
97. Warung nika di samping terminal. -> The stall is beside the terminal.
98. Montor icang di samping jero. -> My motorbike is beside the house.
99. Di samping pura, ada merajan. -> Beside the temple, there is a family shrine.
100. Di harep. -> In front (phrase).
101. Di harep jero ida, ada penjor. -> In front of his / her home, there is a penjor.
102. Cicing icang di harep jelanan. -> My dog is in front of the gate.
103. Di harep banjar, ada bemo. -> In front of the banjar, there is a bemo.
104. Di duri. -> Behind (phrase).
105. Paon nika di duri umah. -> The kitchen is behind the house.
106. Di duri jero, ada carik. -> Behind the house, there is a rice field.
107. Cai negak di harep, icang di duri. -> You sit in front, I sit behind.
108. Nyasar. -> To get lost (neutral).
109. Icang nyasar di kota! -> I got lost in the city!
110. Cai nyasar? Dija umah caine? -> Are you lost? Where is your house?
111. Toris nika nyasar, icang nulungin. -> The tourist is lost — I help him.
113. Icang nyasar di alas. -> I am lost in the forest.
114. Ia nyasar, ia jejeh. -> He is lost — he is scared.
115. Nyasar gati icang jani, dija jalan ka peken? -> I'm so lost now — where is the road to the market?
116. Takon. -> To ask (familiar).
117. Icang takon ring krama. -> I ask the villagers.
118. Cai takon ka nyen? -> Whom do you ask?
119. Takon ka polisi, cai nyasar! -> Ask the police — you're lost!
120. Nakenang. -> To ask (refined).
121. Tiang nakenang margi ring krama. -> I ask the villagers about the road.
122. Ragane nakenang napi? -> What do you ask?
123. Ipun nakenang genah pura. -> He / she asks for the temple's location.
124. Tiang kimud nakenang. -> I am shy to ask.

---

### Lesson 39: Comparisons

**Focus:** more/less than, sameness and difference, similarity, the -an comparative suffix, superlatives with `paling`, and rich/poor.
**Grammar note:** comparatives use `lebih` + adjective or the suffix **-an**: `gedean` = bigger, `luungan` = better. "Than" = `teken` (familiar) or `saking` (refined): `Montor enggalan teken sepeda.` Superlative: `paling` + adjective: `paling tegeh` = highest.
**New in this lesson (teaching order):** paling (recycled — new sense "most"), lebih, lebian, kuangan, kirangan, pateh (recycled), patuh (recycled), tusing patuh, nenten pateh, mirib (recycled), agengan, gedean, alitan, cenikan, luungan, becikan, jelekan, kaonan, tegehan, endepan, mudaan, maalan, sugih, tiwas, lacur, kuat (recycled), kuatan, enggalan, adengan, bagusan
*(Note: `paling` meant "confused/lost" in Lesson 16 — from here it also means "most"; context decides. `tiwas` = poor in money; `lacur` = poor in luck.)*

1. Paling. -> Most. [recycled from Lesson 16 "lost" — new sense]
2. Gunung Agung paling tegeh ring Bali. -> Mount Agung is the highest in Bali.
3. Sira paling tua di jumah? -> Who is the oldest at home?
4. Kopi nika paling jaen. -> That coffee is the most delicious.
5. Lebih. -> More (neutral).
6. Umah icang lebih gede. -> My house is bigger.
7. Niki lebih melah. -> This one is nicer.
8. Buku caine lebih liu. -> Your books are more numerous.
9. Lebian. -> More than (familiar).
10. Pis icang lebian teken pis caine. -> My money is more than yours.
11. Gajian bapa lebian. -> Father's salary is bigger.
12. Umah ida lebian gede teken umah icang. -> His house is bigger than mine.
13. Kuangan. -> Less than (familiar).
14. Pis icang kuangan teken caine. -> My money is less than yours.
15. Pagawean icang kuangan. -> My work is less.
16. Buku tiang kuangan teken buku ragane. -> My books are fewer than yours.
17. Kirangan. -> Less than (refined).
18. Jinah tiang kirangan saking ragane. -> My money is less than yours.
19. Kirangan akidik, nggih. -> A little less, yes.
20. Pakaryan ipun kirangan. -> His / her work is less.
21. Patuh. -> Same / equal (neutral).
22. Icang lan cai patuh. -> You and I are the same.
23. Patuh gati, nggih! -> Exactly the same, yes!
24. Buku niki lan nika patuh. -> This book and that one are the same.
25. Pateh. -> Same (refined). [recycled from Lesson 34 — fresh sentences]
26. Tiang lan ragane pateh. -> You and I are the same.
27. Manah tiang pateh. -> My opinion is the same.
28. Genah nika pateh lan genah niki. -> That place is the same as this place.
29. Patuh. -> Same (familiar). [recycled — fresh sentences]
30. Icang lan ia patuh. -> He and I are the same.
31. Baju icang patuh lan baju caine. -> My shirt is the same as yours.
32. Patuh, nggih! -> The same, yes!
33. Tusing patuh. -> Not the same (familiar phrase).
34. Baju icang tusing patuh lan baju caine. -> My shirt is not the same as yours.
35. Tusing patuh, nanging pateh melah. -> Not the same, but equally good.
36. Adan icang tusing patuh lan adan ia. -> My name is different from his.
37. Nenten pateh. -> Not the same (refined phrase).
38. Buku tiang nenten pateh lan buku ragane. -> My book is not the same as yours.
39. Nenten pateh, nggih. -> Not the same, yes.
40. Manah tiang nenten pateh lan manah ida. -> My opinion differs from his / hers (honored).
41. Mirib. -> Similar. [recycled — fresh sentences]
42. Cai mirib lan belin caine. -> You resemble your older brother.
43. Mirib gati, nggih! -> So similar, yes!
44. Umah nika mirib lan umah icang. -> That house resembles mine.
45. Agengan. -> Bigger (refined).
46. Jero ida agengan. -> His / her (honored) home is bigger.
47. Agengan teken jero tiang. -> Bigger than my home.
48. Pura nika agengan teken merajan. -> The temple is bigger than the family shrine.
49. Gedean. -> Bigger (familiar).
50. Umah icang gedean. -> My house is bigger.
51. Gedean teken umah caine? -> Bigger than your house?
52. Cicing ento gedean teken meong. -> That dog is bigger than the cat.
53. Alitan. -> Smaller (refined).
54. Jero tiang alitan. -> My home is smaller.
55. Alitan teken jero ida. -> Smaller than his / her (honored) home.
56. Panak ida alitan teken panak icang. -> His / her child is smaller than mine.
57. Cenikan. -> Smaller (familiar).
58. Umah icang cenikan. -> My house is smaller.
59. Cenikan teken umah caine. -> Smaller than your house.
60. Sepatu niki cenikan. -> These shoes are smaller.
61. Luungan. -> Better (familiar).
62. Kopi niki luungan. -> This coffee is better.
63. Luungan teken kopi nika. -> Better than that coffee.
64. Jumah luungan, nenten rame. -> Home is better — not crowded.
65. Becikan. -> Better (refined).
66. Ajengan niki becikan. -> This food is better.
67. Becikan teken ajengan nika. -> Better than that food.
68. Margi niki becikan. -> This road is better.
69. Jelekan. -> Worse (familiar).
70. Cuaca jani jelekan. -> The weather now is worse.
71. Jelekan teken dibi? -> Worse than yesterday?
72. Jalan nika jelekan gati. -> That road is much worse.
73. Kaonan. -> Worse (refined).
74. Genah nika kaonan. -> That place is worse.
75. Kaonan teken genah niki. -> Worse than this place.
76. Kaonan gati, nggih. -> Much worse, indeed.
77. Tegehan. -> Taller / higher (familiar).
78. Bapa icang tegehan teken icang. -> My father is taller than me.
79. Gunung nika tegehan. -> That mountain is higher.
80. Tegehan teken bukit nika. -> Higher than that hill.
81. Endepan. -> Shorter (familiar).
82. Adi icang endepan. -> My younger sibling is shorter.
83. Endepan teken cai. -> Shorter than you.
84. Bale nika endepan. -> That pavilion is lower.
85. Mudaan. -> Younger (familiar).
86. Adi icang mudaan teken icang. -> My younger sibling is younger than me.
87. Ia mudaan teken beline. -> He is younger than his older brother.
88. Nyai mudaan teken mbok nyai. -> You are younger than your older sister.
89. Maalan. -> More expensive (familiar).
90. Sepatu niki maalan. -> These shoes are more expensive.
91. Maalan teken sandal. -> More expensive than sandals.
92. Baju nika maalan gati! -> That shirt is more expensive!
93. Sugih. -> Rich (familiar).
94. Ida sugih gati. -> He / she (honored) is very rich.
95. Ia sugih, jinahne liu. -> He is rich — he has lots of money.
96. Cai dot dadi sugih? -> Do you want to become rich?
97. Tiwas. -> Poor (familiar).
98. Icang tiwas. -> I am poor.
99. Tiwas nanging liang. -> Poor but happy.
100. Ia tiwas, nanging jemet. -> He is poor, but diligent.
101. Lacur. -> Poor / unlucky (familiar).
102. Lacur gati ia. -> He is so unlucky.
103. Tiwas lan lacur nenten pateh. -> Poor and unlucky are not the same.
104. Icang ngrasa lacur jani. -> I feel unlucky now.
105. Kuat. -> Strong. [recycled from Lesson 15 — fresh sentences]
106. Angga icang kuat jani. -> My body is strong now.
107. Tentara nika kuat gati. -> That soldier is very strong.
108. Ia kuat ngaba batu. -> He is strong (enough) to carry the stone.
109. Kuatan. -> Stronger (familiar).
110. Bapa kuatan teken icang. -> Father is stronger than me.
111. Kuatan sira? -> Who is stronger?
112. Jaran nika kuatan teken sampi. -> The horse is stronger than the cow.
113. Enggalan. -> Faster (familiar).
114. Montor enggalan teken sepeda. -> The motorbike is faster than the bicycle.
115. Ia malaib enggalan teken icang. -> He runs faster than me.
116. Pesawat enggalan gati. -> The airplane is much faster.
117. Adengan. -> Slower (familiar).
118. Dokar adengan teken mobil. -> The horse cart is slower than the car.
119. Adengan, icang kenyel. -> Slower — I'm tired.
120. Sampi nika adengan gati. -> That cow is so slow.
121. Bagusan. -> More handsome (familiar).
122. Beli icang bagusan. -> My older brother is more handsome.
123. Bagusan teken nyen? -> More handsome than whom?
124. Cai bagusan jani! -> You're more handsome now!
