### Lesson 40: Experiences

**Focus:** life experiences — ever/never/already, being used to, happenstance, since when, just now, like this/like that, matters and topics, meeting after a long time.
**Grammar note:** `kene` (like this), `keto` (like that, familiar), `kenten` (like that, refined) refer to manner; `unduk` (familiar) / `indik`/`prakara` (refined) = "about / the matter of". `suba makelo!` = "long time no see!"
**New in this lesson (teaching order):** taen (recycled), naenin (recycled), sing taen (recycled), nenten naenin (recycled), suba taen (recycled), sampun naenin (recycled), biasa (recycled), tusing biasa, katuju, sejak, uling (recycled), saking (recycled), uling pidan, saking pidan, mara (recycled), wau (recycled), bakat (recycled), kene, keto, kenten, kengken, prakara, unduk, indik, bakat tepuk, kacunduk (recycled), suba makelo, sampun suwe, tumben (recycled), taen kema, suba pragat (recycled)

1. Taen. -> Ever / to have done. [recycled from Lesson 31 — fresh sentences]
2. Cai taen mabalih ogoh-ogoh? -> Have you ever watched the ogoh-ogoh?
3. Ia taen dadi guru? -> Has he ever been a teacher?
4. Nyai taen maen di pantai? -> Have you ever played at the beach?
5. Naenin. -> Ever (refined). [recycled — fresh sentences]
6. Ragane naenin mabalih nari ring pura? -> Have you ever watched a dance at the temple?
7. Ipun naenin makarya ditu? -> Has he / she ever worked there?
8. Naenin ida melali ka Jawa? -> Has he / she (honored) ever traveled to Java?
9. Sing taen. -> Never (familiar). [recycled — fresh sentences]
10. Icang sing taen naek pesawat. -> I've never ridden a plane.
11. Cai sing taen nyasar? -> Have you never gotten lost?
12. Icang sing taen ka bandara. -> I've never been to the airport.
13. Nenten naenin. -> Never (refined). [recycled — fresh sentences]
14. Tiang nenten naenin naek kapal. -> I have never ridden a ship.
15. Ipun nenten naenin nyasar. -> He / she has never gotten lost.
16. Ragane nenten naenin ka griya? -> Have you never been to the griya?
17. Suba taen. -> Have already / have ever (familiar). [recycled — fresh sentences]
18. Icang suba taen mabalih ngigel. -> I have watched a dance before.
19. Cai suba taen takon ka polisi? -> Have you ever asked the police?
20. Ia suba taen ngayah ring pura. -> He has done temple service before.
21. Sampun naenin. -> Have already (refined). [recycled — fresh sentences]
22. Tiang sampun naenin ngayah. -> I have done temple service before.
23. Ragane sampun naenin muspa ring griya? -> Have you ever prayed at the griya?
24. Ipun sampun naenin nari ring pura. -> He / she has danced at the temple before.
25. Biasa. -> Used to / usually. [recycled from Lesson 34 — fresh sentences]
26. Icang biasa bangun semeng. -> I usually wake up in the morning.
27. Biasa, ia megawe di kota. -> As usual, he works in the city.
28. Cai biasa madaar napi? -> What do you usually eat?
29. Tusing biasa. -> Not used to (phrase).
30. Icang tusing biasa madaar lalah. -> I'm not used to spicy food.
31. Cai tusing biasa ngoyong jumah? -> Aren't you used to staying home?
32. Ia tusing biasa majalan joh. -> He's not used to walking far.
33. Katuju. -> Happened to (familiar).
34. Icang katuju tepuk timpal di peken. -> I happened to find a friend at the market.
35. Katuju ujan, icang belus. -> It happened to rain — I got wet.
36. Ia katuju driki jani. -> He happens to be here now.
37. Sejak. -> Since (neutral).
38. Sejak dibi icang sakit. -> Since yesterday I've been sick.
39. Sejak pidan cai nongos dini? -> Since when do you live here?
40. Sejak bulan lalu, ia megawe di kota. -> Since last month he works in the city.
41. Uling. -> Since / from (familiar). [recycled — fresh sentences]
42. Icang malajah uling bulan lalu. -> I've been learning since last month.
43. Uling semeng icang sibuk. -> Since morning I've been busy.
44. Uling dibi ia sakit. -> Since yesterday he's been sick.
45. Saking. -> Since / from (refined). [recycled — fresh sentences]
46. Tiang malajah Basa Bali saking tiban lalu. -> I've been learning Balinese since last year.
47. Saking iwau ida ring driki. -> Since earlier he / she (honored) is here.
48. Ipun sungkan saking dibi. -> He / she has been ill since yesterday.
49. Uling pidan. -> Since when (familiar phrase).
50. Uling pidan cai uning ia? -> Since when do you know him?
51. Uling pidan cai malajah Basa Bali? -> Since when do you study Balinese?
52. Uling pidan, icang nongos dini. -> Since long ago, I live here.
53. Saking pidan. -> Since when (refined phrase).
54. Saking pidan ragane uning ida? -> Since when do you know him / her (honored)?
55. Saking pidan ipun linggih driki? -> Since when does he / she reside here?
56. Saking pidan, tiang malajah. -> Since long ago, I have been learning.
57. Mara. -> Just / only (familiar). [recycled — fresh sentences]
58. Ia mara teka uling kota. -> He just came from the city.
59. Icang mara ngae kopi. -> I just made coffee.
60. Mara jani tiang uning. -> Only now do I know.
61. Wau. -> Just (refined). [recycled — fresh sentences]
62. Ida wau mantuk saking pura. -> He / she (honored) just returned from the temple.
63. Ipun wau puput ngayah. -> He / she just finished temple service.
64. Tiang wau mireng gatra nika. -> I just heard that news.
65. Bakat. -> Happened to experience (familiar). [recycled from Lesson 31 — fresh sentences]
66. Icang bakat panas ibak. -> I got caught by the heat earlier.
67. Cai bakat nyasar dibi? -> Did you happen to get lost yesterday?
68. Bakat linuh, umah nika rusak. -> Struck by the earthquake, that house was damaged.
69. Kene. -> Like this (familiar).
70. Kene icang ngae kopi. -> This is how I make coffee.
71. Kene, nggih! -> Like this, okay!
72. Cai ngae kene? -> Do you make it like this?
73. Keto. -> Like that (familiar).
74. Keto icang ngomong. -> That's what I said.
75. Keto, nggih. -> Like that, yes.
76. Ngudiang cai keto? -> Why are you like that?
77. Kenten. -> Like that (refined).
78. Kenten tiang mabaos. -> That is how I spoke.
79. Kenten, nggih. -> Like that, yes.
80. Ipun nenten kenten. -> He / she is not like that.
81. Kengken. -> How (familiar variant).
82. Kengken icang ka bandara? -> How do I get to the airport?
83. Kengken kabare jani? -> How are things now?
84. Kengken cai ngae nika? -> How do you make that?
85. Prakara. -> Matter / case (refined).
86. Prakara nika napi? -> What is that matter?
87. Tiang uning prakara nika. -> I know about that matter.
88. Prakara ipun sampun puput. -> His / her matter is finished.
89. Unduk. -> Matter / thing (familiar).
90. Unduk nika apa? -> What is that matter?
91. Icang sing uning unduk nika. -> I don't know about that matter.
92. Unduk cai, icang nawang. -> As for you — I know.
93. Indik. -> About / regarding (refined).
94. Indik napi nika? -> About what is that?
95. Tiang mabaos indik Basa Bali. -> I speak about the Balinese language.
96. Ipun mireng indik upacara nika. -> He / she heard about that ceremony.
97. Bakat tepuk. -> Happened to find / meet (phrase).
98. Icang bakat tepuk pis di jalan. -> I happened to find money on the road.
99. Cai bakat tepuk nyen ditu? -> Whom did you happen to meet there?
100. Icang bakat tepuk ia di terminal. -> I happened to meet him at the terminal.
101. Kacunduk. -> To meet (refined). [recycled from Lesson 24 — fresh sentences]
102. Tiang kacunduk pandita ring griya. -> I met the priest at the griya.
103. Ragane kacunduk sira dibi? -> Whom did you meet yesterday?
104. Ipun kacunduk tiang ring pura. -> He / she met me at the temple.
105. Suba makelo. -> It's been a long time (familiar phrase).
106. Suba makelo icang tusing kacunduk cai! -> Long time no see!
107. Suba makelo ia nongos ditu. -> He has lived there a long time.
108. Suba makelo icang ngoyong dini. -> I have stayed here a long time.
109. Sampun suwe. -> It's been a long time (refined phrase).
110. Sampun suwe tiang nenten kacunduk ida. -> It's been long since I met him / her (honored).
111. Sampun suwe ipun linggih driki. -> He / she has resided here a long time.
112. Sampun suwe, nggih! -> It's been a while, yes!
113. Tumben. -> First time / rare. [recycled from Lesson 36 — fresh sentences]
114. Tumben icang naek dokar! -> First time I've ridden a horse cart!
115. Tumben cai teka semeng! -> For once you've come in the morning!
116. Tumben ida rauh driki. -> For once, he / she (honored) came here.
117. Taen kema. -> Have been there (phrase).
118. Cai taen kema? -> Have you been there?
119. Icang suba taen kema. -> I have already been there.
120. Ia sing taen kema. -> He has never been there.
121. Suba pragat. -> Already finished (familiar phrase). [recycled — fresh sentences]
122. Pagawean icang suba pragat. -> My work is finished.
123. Suba pragat, icang mareren. -> Finished — I rest.
124. Upacara nika suba pragat. -> The ceremony is finished.

### Lesson 41: Numbers 3

**Focus:** the rest of the number system — 700, 900, 3,000–4,000, 100,000 up to millions, rupiah, discounts, receipts, and paid-off debts.
**Grammar note:** thousands combine number + `tali` (a bundle of 1,000): `satus tali` = 100,000; `a yuta` = one million. At counters and shops: `Aji kuda niki?` → `Seket tali, lunas!`
**New in this lesson (teaching order):** satus (recycled), satak (recycled), telung atus (recycled), samas (recycled), limang atus (recycled), enam atus (recycled), pitung atus, domas (recycled), sangang atus, siu (recycled), duang tali (recycled), telung tali, patang tali, limang tali (recycled), adasa tali (recycled), selawe tali (recycled), seket tali (recycled), satus tali, satak tali, limang atus tali, a yuta, yuta, miliar, rupiah, aji kuda niki (recycled), susuk (recycled), potongan, medagang, mayar (recycled), bon, lunas
*(Note: `duang tali` and `duang tali` are both "2,000" — this lesson practices the `duang` form listed here.)*

1. Satus. -> One hundred. [recycled from Lesson 22 — fresh sentences]
2. Satus buah icang meli. -> I buy a hundred pieces of fruit.
3. Krama satus rauh ka odalan. -> A hundred villagers came to the odalan.
4. Satus nika angka. -> A hundred is a number.
5. Satak. -> Two hundred. [recycled — fresh sentences]
6. Satak taluh ada di warung. -> Two hundred eggs are at the stall.
7. Satak nika angka napi? -> What number is two hundred?
8. Krama satak ngayah ring pura. -> Two hundred villagers serve at the temple.
9. Telung atus. -> Three hundred. [recycled — fresh sentences]
10. Telung atus krama rauh ka odalan. -> Three hundred villagers came to the odalan.
11. Telung atus nika angka. -> Three hundred is a number.
12. Tiang ngae telung atus canang. -> I make three hundred canang.
13. Samas. -> Four hundred. [recycled — fresh sentences]
14. Samas tamiu rauh ka pura. -> Four hundred guests came to the temple.
15. Samas nika angka. -> Four hundred is a number.
16. Samas canang meme ngae. -> Mother makes four hundred canang.
17. Limang atus. -> Five hundred. [recycled — fresh sentences]
18. Limang atus krama ngayah. -> Five hundred villagers serve.
19. Limang atus nika angka. -> Five hundred is a number.
20. Tiang numbas limang atus canang. -> I buy five hundred canang.
21. Enam atus. -> Six hundred. [recycled — fresh sentences]
22. Enam atus tamiu rauh ka odalan. -> Six hundred guests came to the odalan.
23. Enam atus nika angka. -> Six hundred is a number.
24. Enam atus canang ada di merajan. -> Six hundred canang are at the family temple.
25. Pitung atus. -> Seven hundred.
26. Pitung atus krama ring desa nika. -> There are seven hundred villagers in that village.
27. Pitung atus nika angka. -> Seven hundred is a number.
28. Pitung atus canang meme ngae. -> Mother makes seven hundred canang.
29. Domas. -> Eight hundred. [recycled — fresh sentences]
30. Domas tamiu rauh ka pura. -> Eight hundred guests came to the temple.
31. Domas nika angka. -> Eight hundred is a number.
32. Domas piring ada di warung nika. -> Eight hundred plates are at that stall.
33. Sangang atus. -> Nine hundred.
34. Sangang atus krama ngayah. -> Nine hundred villagers serve.
35. Sangang atus nika angka. -> Nine hundred is a number.
36. Sangang atus taluh ada di peken. -> Nine hundred eggs are at the market.
37. Siu. -> One thousand. [recycled — fresh sentences]
38. Siu krama rauh ka odalan. -> A thousand villagers came to the odalan.
39. Siu canang ada di pura. -> A thousand canang are at the temple.
40. Siu nika angka gede. -> A thousand is a big number.
41. Duang tali. -> Two thousand. [recycled — fresh sentences]
42. Aji teh nika duang tali. -> The price of the tea is 2,000.
43. Duang tali, nggih, suksma. -> 2,000 — yes, thanks.
44. Aji jukut nika duang tali. -> The price of the vegetables is 2,000.
45. Telung tali. -> Three thousand.
46. Aji buah nika telung tali. -> The price of the fruit is 3,000.
47. Telung tali, dadi kuang? -> 3,000 — can you make it less?
48. Icang mayar telung tali. -> I pay 3,000.
49. Patang tali. -> Four thousand.
50. Aji nasi nika patang tali? -> Is the price of the rice 4,000?
51. Patang tali, nggih. -> 4,000, yes.
52. Cai mayar patang tali? -> Do you pay 4,000?
53. Limang tali. -> Five thousand. [recycled — fresh sentences]
54. Aji taluh nika limang tali. -> The price of the eggs is 5,000.
55. Icang ngaba limang tali. -> I'm carrying 5,000.
56. Limang tali, nggih, suksma. -> 5,000 — yes, thanks.
57. Adasa tali. -> Ten thousand. [recycled — fresh sentences]
58. Aji celana nika adasa tali. -> The price of those pants is 10,000.
59. Adasa tali, icang bayar. -> 10,000 — I'll pay.
60. Regane adasa tali, nggih. -> The price is 10,000, yes.
61. Selawe tali. -> Twenty-five thousand. [recycled — fresh sentences]
62. Aji sepatu nika selawe tali. -> The price of those shoes is 25,000.
63. Tiang mayar selawe tali dibi. -> I paid 25,000 yesterday.
64. Selawe tali baang baju nika? -> 25,000 for that shirt?
65. Seket tali. -> Fifty thousand. [recycled — fresh sentences]
66. Aji wastra nika seket tali. -> The price of that cloth is 50,000.
67. Seket tali, tiang lakar numbas. -> 50,000 — I will buy it.
68. Cai ngaba seket tali? -> Are you carrying 50,000?
69. Satus tali. -> One hundred thousand.
70. Aji ali-ali nika satus tali. -> The price of that ring is 100,000.
71. Satus tali, mahal gati! -> 100,000 — so expensive!
72. Tiang nenten ngaba jinah satus tali. -> I'm not carrying 100,000.
73. Satak tali. -> Two hundred thousand.
74. Aji hp nika satak tali? -> Is the price of that phone 200,000?
75. Cai mayar satak tali? -> Do you pay 200,000?
76. Satak tali nika mahal baang icang. -> 200,000 is expensive for me.
77. Limang atus tali. -> Five hundred thousand.
78. Aji sepeda motor nika limang atus tali? -> Is the price of that motorbike 500,000?
79. Limang atus tali, mahal gati! -> 500,000 — so expensive!
80. Tiang lakar mayar limang atus tali. -> I will pay 500,000.
81. A yuta. -> One million (phrase).
82. Aji mas nika a yuta. -> The price of that gold is one million.
83. A yuta, mahal gati! -> One million — so expensive!
84. Cai ngaba jinah a yuta? -> Are you carrying one million?
85. Yuta. -> Million.
86. Jinah ida liu, ada yuta. -> He / she (honored) has lots of money — millions.
87. Aji nika kuda yuta? -> How many million is that price?
88. Kuda yuta aji mobil nika? -> How many million is that car?
89. Miliar. -> Billion.
90. Miliar nika angka gede gati. -> A billion is a very big number.
91. Kuda miliar? -> How many billion?
92. Sugih gati, jinahne miliar. -> So rich — his money is in the billions.
93. Rupiah. -> Rupiah.
94. Aji nika kuda rupiah? -> How many rupiah is that price?
95. Icang ngaba satus tali rupiah. -> I'm carrying 100,000 rupiah.
96. Kuda rupiah caine ngaba? -> How many rupiah are you carrying?
97. Aji kuda niki? -> How much is this? [recycled from Lesson 21 — fresh sentences]
98. Baju niki, aji kuda niki? -> This shirt — how much is it?
99. Aji kuda niki jani? -> How much is this now?
100. Kamben niki, aji kuda niki? -> This sarong — how much is this?
101. Susuk. -> Change (money). [recycled from Lesson 21 — fresh sentences]
102. Susuk icang kuda? -> How much is my change?
103. Nggih, susuk caine limang tali. -> Yes, your change is 5,000.
104. Susuk nika baang icang. -> The change is for me / give me the change.
105. Potongan. -> Discount / deduction.
106. Ada potongan jani! -> There's a discount now!
107. Potongan nika kuda? -> How much is the discount?
108. Potongan limang tali, dadi mudah! -> A 5,000 discount — it becomes cheap!
109. Medagang. -> To sell / trade (familiar).
110. Meme medagang di peken. -> Mother trades at the market.
111. Cai medagang apa? -> What do you sell?
112. Ia medagang buah lan jukut. -> He sells fruit and vegetables.
113. Mayar. -> To pay. [recycled from Lesson 21 — fresh sentences]
114. Icang mayar jani, suksma. -> I'll pay now, thanks.
115. Cai mayar kuda rupiah? -> How many rupiah do you pay?
116. Tiang mayar ring toko. -> I pay at the store.
117. Bon. -> Receipt.
118. Bon nika dija? -> Where is the receipt?
119. Cai ngaba bon? -> Do you have the receipt?
120. Bon nika baang icang. -> Give me the receipt.
121. Lunas. -> Paid off / settled.
122. Lunas, suksma! -> Paid in full, thanks!
123. Utang icang suba lunas. -> My debt is paid off.
124. Cai suba mayar? Lunas? -> Have you paid? All settled?

---

### Lesson 42: Emergencies

**Focus:** calling for help, accidents, thieves and pickpockets, injuries and bleeding, medicine and healers, fire, floods, earthquakes, evacuation, warnings.
**New in this lesson (teaching order):** tulung (recycled), pitulung, tulungin tiang, kepaling, nyasar (recycled), sengkala, baya, maling, corah, kecopetan, polisi (recycled), sakit (recycled), sungkan (recycled), berdarah, pesu getih, luka, tatuan, rumah sakit (recycled), klinik, obat, tamba, dokter, balian, api, puun, blabar (recycled), linuh (recycled), ngungsi, awas, enggalang
*(Notes: `maling` (familiar) / `corah` (refined-literary) = thief. `obat` (neutral) / `tamba` (refined) = medicine. A `balian` is a traditional healer — many Balinese consult both `dokter` and `balian`. `enggalang!` = hurry up!)*

1. Tulung. -> Help! [recycled from Lesson 3 — fresh sentences]
2. Tulung! Icang nyasar! -> Help! I'm lost!
3. Tulung tiang, icang sakit! -> Help me, I'm sick!
4. Nyen dadi tulung icang? -> Who can help me?
5. Pitulung. -> Help / assistance (refined noun).
6. Tiang nunas pitulung. -> I ask for help.
7. Pitulung ragane becik gati. -> Your help is very good.
8. Tiang suksma ring pitulung ragane. -> Thank you for your help.
9. Tulungin tiang. -> Help me (phrase).
10. Tulungin tiang, icang paling! -> Help me, I'm lost!
11. Cai, tulungin tiang! -> You — help me!
12. Tulungin tiang ka rumah sakit. -> Help me get to the hospital.
13. Paling. -> Lost / confused. [recycled from Lesson 16 — fresh sentences]
14. Icang paling di alas, tulung! -> I'm lost in the forest — help!
15. Cai paling? Ngoyong dini! -> Are you lost? Stay here!
16. Tiang paling, tiang ngrereh margi. -> I'm lost — I'm looking for the road.
17. Kepaling. -> To get lost (familiar).
18. Icang kepaling di kota. -> I got lost in the city.
19. Cai kepaling? -> Did you get lost?
20. Ia kepaling, dadi ia jejeh. -> He got lost, so he's scared.
21. Nyasar. -> To get lost (neutral). [recycled from Lesson 38 — fresh sentences]
22. Toris nika nyasar ring alas. -> The tourist got lost in the forest.
23. Icang nyasar, icang takon ka polisi. -> I got lost — I asked the police.
24. Nyai nyasar? Tiang nulungin! -> Are you lost? I'll help!
25. Sengkala. -> Accident / disaster (refined).
26. Ada sengkala di jalan. -> There was an accident on the road.
27. Sengkala nika gede? -> Was the accident big?
28. Icang ningeh sengkala dibi. -> I heard about the accident yesterday.
29. Baya. -> Danger (neutral).
30. Ada baya ditu! -> There's danger there!
31. Baya nika gede. -> The danger is big.
32. Tiang ajrih ring baya nika. -> I'm afraid of that danger.
33. Maling. -> Thief (familiar).
34. Ada maling di jumah! -> There's a thief in the house!
35. Maling nika ngaba hp icang! -> The thief took my phone!
36. Polisi ngalih maling. -> The police look for the thief.
37. Corah. -> Thief (refined).
38. Ada corah ring peken! -> There's a thief at the market!
39. Corah nika ngaba jinah ida. -> The thief took his / her (honored) money.
40. Polisi ngrereh corah nika. -> The police search for the thief.
41. Kecopetan. -> To be pickpocketed.
42. Icang kecopetan di peken! -> I got pickpocketed at the market!
43. Cai kecopetan? Jinah caine ilang? -> Were you pickpocketed? Is your money gone?
44. Kecopetan dibi, pis icang ilang. -> Pickpocketed yesterday — my money is gone.
45. Polisi. -> Police. [recycled from Lesson 33 — fresh sentences]
46. Polisi rauh jani! -> The police are coming now!
47. Icang takon ka polisi. -> I ask the police.
48. Polisi nulungin tiang. -> The police help me.
49. Sakit. -> Sick / in pain. [recycled from Lesson 16 — fresh sentences]
50. Icang sakit gati, ka rumah sakit! -> I'm very sick — to the hospital!
51. Cai sakit apa? -> What's wrong with you?
52. Ia sakit, ia nenten dadi megawe. -> He's sick — he can't work.
53. Sungkan. -> Ill (refined). [recycled — fresh sentences]
54. Ida sungkan gati. -> He / she (honored) is very ill.
55. Sungkan ida, krama ngwantu. -> He / she (honored) is ill — the people help.
56. Berdarah. -> Bleeding.
57. Batis icang berdarah! -> My foot is bleeding!
58. Cai berdarah, ka rumah sakit! -> You're bleeding — to the hospital!
59. Berdarah gati, icang jejeh! -> It's bleeding badly — I'm scared!
60. Pesu getih. -> To bleed (phrase).
61. Liman icang pesu getih. -> My hand is bleeding.
62. Pesu getih, tulungin tiang! -> Bleeding — help me!
63. Batisne pesu getih. -> His / her foot is bleeding.
64. Luka. -> Injured / wound.
65. Lima icang luka. -> My hand is injured.
66. Cai luka? Sakit? -> Are you hurt? Does it hurt?
67. Ia luka, krama nulungin. -> He's injured — the people help.
68. Tatuan. -> Wounded (familiar).
69. Ia tatuan di batis. -> He is wounded in the foot.
70. Cai tatuan? -> Are you wounded?
71. Tatuan gati, ka dokter! -> Badly wounded — to the doctor!
72. Rumah sakit. -> Hospital. [recycled from Lesson 12 — fresh sentences]
73. Ka rumah sakit jani! -> To the hospital now!
74. Ia jani ring rumah sakit. -> He is at the hospital now.
75. Rumah sakit nika nampek? -> Is the hospital near?
76. Klinik. -> Clinic.
77. Ada klinik ditu. -> There is a clinic there.
78. Klinik nika dija? -> Where is the clinic?
79. Icang ka klinik, nenten ka rumah sakit. -> I go to the clinic, not the hospital.
80. Obat. -> Medicine (neutral).
81. Icang meli obat. -> I buy medicine.
82. Obat nika dija? -> Where is the medicine?
83. Cai suba nginum obat? -> Have you taken medicine?
84. Tamba. -> Medicine (refined).
85. Tiang nunas tamba ring dokter. -> I ask the doctor for medicine.
86. Tamba nika pait. -> The medicine is bitter.
87. Ipun sampun nginem tamba? -> Has he / she taken the medicine?
88. Dokter. -> Doctor.
89. Dokter nika ring klinik. -> The doctor is at the clinic.
90. Tiang ka dokter benjang. -> I go to the doctor tomorrow.
91. Dokter suba ngicen tamba. -> The doctor has given medicine.
92. Balian. -> Traditional healer.
93. Balian nika ring dija? -> Where is the healer?
94. Ia ka balian, nenten ka dokter. -> He goes to the healer, not the doctor.
95. Balian nika nulungin krama. -> The healer helps the people.
96. Api. -> Fire.
97. Ada api ditu! -> There's a fire there!
98. Api nika gede gati! -> The fire is so big!
99. Api! Api! Icang jejeh! -> Fire! Fire! I'm scared!
100. Puun. -> Burned (familiar).
101. Umah nika puun! -> That house burned down!
102. Toko nika puun dibi. -> That store burned yesterday.
103. Ia sedih, umahne puun. -> He is sad — his house burned.
104. Blabar. -> Flood. [recycled from Lesson 23 — fresh sentences]
105. Blabar gede di desa nika! -> A big flood in that village!
106. Blabar jani, jalan rusak. -> Flood now — the road is damaged.
107. Cai bakat blabar dibi? -> Were you caught by the flood yesterday?
108. Linuh. -> Earthquake. [recycled — fresh sentences]
109. Ada linuh ibak! -> There was an earthquake earlier!
110. Linuh gede, icang jejeh gati! -> A big earthquake — I was so scared!
111. Linuh, umah icang rusak. -> Earthquake — my house is damaged.
112. Ngungsi. -> To evacuate / seek refuge.
113. Krama ngungsi ka sekolah. -> The people evacuate to the school.
114. Cai ngungsi dija? -> Where do you evacuate?
115. Ngungsi jani, ada baya! -> Evacuate now — there's danger!
116. Awas. -> Watch out!
117. Awas! Ada maling! -> Watch out! There's a thief!
118. Awas, api ditu! -> Watch out — fire there!
119. Awas, cai kepaling! -> Careful — you'll get lost!
120. Enggalang. -> Hurry up!
121. Enggalang, ka rumah sakit! -> Hurry — to the hospital!
122. Enggalang, polisi rauh! -> Hurry, the police are coming!
123. Enggalang cai, baya ditu! -> Hurry up — danger there!
124. Enggalang, ngungsi jani! -> Hurry — evacuate now!

---

### Lesson 43: Relationships

**Focus:** friends and enemies, neighbors, courtship and marriage, break-ups, love, like, hate, missing someone, loyalty, jealousy.
**Grammar note:** affection takes `ajak` (familiar) or `ring` (refined): `Icang sayang cai.` / `Tiang tresna ring ragane.` The famous line `Tiang tresna ajak adi.` = "I love you" (to someone younger/dear).
**New in this lesson (teaching order):** timpal (recycled), sawitra (recycled), musuh, satru, pisaga, banjaran, tunangan, kurenan (recycled), rabin (recycled), nyama (recycled), semeton (recycled), jomblo, truna truni, makurenan, nganten, marabian, pegat, cerai, sayang, tresna, tiang tresna ajak adi, demen (recycled), seneng (recycled), benci, gedeg (recycled), kangen, rindu, satya, cemburu, selingkuh, baikan

1. Timpal. -> Friend (familiar). [recycled from Lesson 4 — fresh sentences]
2. Timpal icang liu gati. -> I have so many friends.
3. Cai timpal icang paling luung. -> You are my best friend.
4. Timpal icang rauh jani. -> My friend is coming now.
5. Sawitra. -> Friend (refined). [recycled — fresh sentences]
6. Sawitra tiang becik gati. -> My friend is very kind.
7. Ipun sawitra tiang saking sekolah. -> He / she is my friend from school.
8. Sawitra ida liu. -> He / she (honored) has many friends.
9. Musuh. -> Enemy (familiar).
10. Ia musuh icang. -> He is my enemy.
11. Cai musuh icang? -> Are you my enemy?
12. Musuh nenten luung. -> Having enemies is not good.
13. Satru. -> Enemy (refined).
14. Satru tiang nyen? -> Who is my enemy?
15. Ipun nenten satru tiang. -> He / she is not my enemy.
16. Satru lan sawitra nenten pateh. -> Enemy and friend are not the same.
17. Pisaga. -> Neighbor (neutral).
18. Pisaga icang becik. -> My neighbor is kind.
19. Cai pisaga icang? -> Are you my neighbor?
20. Pisaga icang nongos di samping umah. -> My neighbor lives beside the house.
21. Banjaran. -> Neighbor (familiar).
22. Banjaran icang liu. -> I have many neighbors.
23. Banjaran caine nyen? -> Who is your neighbor?
24. Banjaran icang jemet ngayah. -> My neighbor is diligent in temple service.
25. Tunangan. -> Fiancé(e) / boyfriend / girlfriend.
26. Tunangan icang jegeg. -> My fiancée is beautiful.
27. Tunangan nyai nyen? -> Who is your boyfriend?
28. Tunangan icang lakar dadi kurenan. -> My fiancée will become my spouse.
29. Kurenan. -> Spouse (familiar). [recycled from Lesson 28 — fresh sentences]
30. Kurenan icang luung gati. -> My spouse is very good.
31. Kurenan icang megawe di kota. -> My spouse works in the city.
32. Kurenan nyai dija? -> Where is your spouse?
33. Rabin. -> Spouse (refined). [recycled — fresh sentences]
34. Rabin tiang becik gati. -> My spouse is very kind.
35. Rabin ida ring dija? -> Where is his / her (honored) spouse?
36. Rabin ipun makarya ring sekolah. -> His / her spouse works at the school.
37. Nyama. -> Sibling / relative. [recycled from Lesson 9 — fresh sentences]
38. Nyama icang dadi pisaga icang. -> My sibling became my neighbor.
39. Cai nyama icang, nenten timpal biasa. -> You are family to me, not just a friend.
40. Nyama lan timpal rauh jani. -> Relatives and friends are coming now.
41. Semeton. -> Relatives (refined). [recycled — fresh sentences]
42. Semeton tiang rauh ka jero. -> My relatives come to the house.
43. Ipun semeton tiang, nenten sawitra biasa. -> He / she is my relative, not just a friend.
44. Semeton ida liu ring desa nika. -> He / she has many relatives in that village.
45. Jomblo. -> Single (neutral).
46. Icang jomblo jani. -> I'm single now.
47. Cai jomblo? -> Are you single?
48. Jomblo nenten sebet! -> Being single isn't sad!
49. Truna truni. -> Unmarried young men and women (phrase).
50. Truna truni ngigel ring banjar. -> The young people dance at the banjar.
51. Truna truni desa nika jemet. -> The youth of that village are diligent.
52. Ada truna truni di bale. -> There are young people at the pavilion.
53. Makurenan. -> To be married (familiar).
54. Beli icang suba makurenan. -> My older brother is married.
55. Cai lakar makurenan pidan? -> When will you get married?
56. Ia makurenan ajak timpal icang. -> He married my friend.
57. Nganten. -> Married couple / wedding (familiar).
58. Nganten nika jegeg lan bagus. -> The bride is beautiful and the groom handsome.
59. Ada nganten di desa nika. -> There is a wedding in that village.
60. Nganten nika suba makurenan. -> That couple is now married.
61. Marabian. -> To marry (refined).
62. Ipun marabian benjang. -> He / she marries tomorrow.
63. Ragane lakar marabian pidan? -> When will you marry?
64. Ipun marabian ring pura. -> He / she married at the temple.
65. Pegat. -> Broken up / separated (familiar).
66. Ia suba pegat. -> He is separated now.
67. Cai pegat lan tunangan caine? -> Did you break up with your fiancée?
68. Pegat nenten luung, nanging dadi. -> Breaking up isn't good, but it happens.
69. Cerai. -> Divorced (neutral).
70. Ia suba cerai. -> He is divorced.
71. Cerai lan pegat patuh? -> Are cerai and pegat the same?
72. Ia cerai, ia sedih. -> He's divorced — he's sad.
73. Sayang. -> Love / affection (neutral).
74. Icang sayang cai. -> I love you.
75. Ia sayang gati ajak adine. -> He loves his younger sibling so much.
76. Meme sayang ajak panakne. -> Mother loves her child.
77. Tresna. -> Love (refined).
78. Tiang tresna ring Basa Bali. -> I love the Balinese language.
79. Tresna ida ring rabin ida. -> He / she (honored) loves his wife.
80. Tresna nika rasa becik. -> Love is a good feeling.
81. Tiang tresna ajak adi. -> I love you (set phrase, to someone dear).
82. Tiang tresna ajak adi, seken! -> I love you, truly!
83. Adi, tiang tresna ajak adi. -> Dear — I love you.
84. Demen. -> To like / love (familiar). [recycled from Lesson 16 — fresh sentences]
85. Icang demen cai. -> I like you.
86. Cai demen nyai? -> Do you like her?
87. Ia demen gati ajak tunanganne. -> He likes his fiancée very much.
88. Seneng. -> To like (refined). [recycled — fresh sentences]
89. Tiang seneng ring ragane. -> I like you.
90. Ipun seneng gati ring rabin ipun. -> He / she loves his wife very much.
91. Seneng lan tresna pateh? -> Are seneng and tresna the same?
92. Benci. -> To hate (neutral).
93. Icang benci maling! -> I hate thieves!
94. Cai benci nyen? -> Whom do you hate?
95. Benci nenten luung, sayang luungan. -> Hate is not good — love is better.
96. Gedeg. -> Angry / to detest (familiar). [recycled from Lesson 16 — fresh sentences]
97. Icang gedeg ajak maling nika! -> I am so angry at that thief!
98. Cai gedeg ajak icang? -> Are you angry with me?
99. Gedeg, nanging icang sabar. -> Angry — but I am patient.
100. Kangen. -> To miss someone (neutral).
101. Icang kangen cai! -> I miss you!
102. Cai kangen nyai? -> Do you miss her?
103. Kangen gati icang ring nyama icang. -> I miss my sibling so much.
104. Rindu. -> To miss / long for (neutral variant).
105. Icang rindu jumah. -> I miss home.
106. Rindu gati ia ring desane. -> He misses his village so much.
107. Cai rindu nyen? -> Whom do you miss?
108. Satya. -> Loyal / faithful.
109. Kurenan icang satya. -> My spouse is faithful.
110. Satya gati cicing nika! -> That dog is so loyal!
111. Cai satya ring timpal caine? -> Are you loyal to your friend?
112. Cemburu. -> Jealous (romantic).
113. Icang cemburu! -> I'm jealous!
114. Cai cemburu kenapa? -> Why are you jealous?
115. Cemburu nenten luung. -> Jealousy is not good.
116. Selingkuh. -> To cheat (in love).
117. Ia selingkuh, kurenane sedih. -> He cheated — his spouse is sad.
118. Cai selingkuh? Jelek ento! -> Did you cheat? That's bad!
119. Selingkuh nika nenten satya. -> Cheating is not loyalty.
120. Baikan. -> To make up (after a quarrel).
121. Icang lan cai baikan jani. -> You and I make up now.
122. Suba baikan? -> Have you made up yet?
123. Ia baikan lan timpalne. -> He made up with his friend.
124. Baikan, nggih, icang ten gedeg. -> Make up, okay — I'm not angry.

---

### Lesson 44: Communication

**Focus:** talking, chatting, listening, writing and reading (recycled with fresh sentences), letters and messages, phone calls, sending, answering, greeting, words and thoughts, silence and secrets.
**New in this lesson (teaching order):** ngomong (recycled), mabaos (recycled), ngorta, ningeh (recycled), mireng (recycled), dingehang, nulis (recycled), nyurat (recycled), maca (recycled), ngwacen (recycled), surat, pesan, nelpon, hp (recycled), ngirim, ngaturang (recycled), jawab, saur, nyambatang, maosang (recycled), takon (recycled), nakenang (recycled), sapa, omong, baos, raos, keneh, manah (recycled), nengil, meneng, rahasia

1. Ngomong. -> To speak / talk (familiar). [recycled from Lesson 37 — fresh sentences]
2. Cai ngomong Basa Bali luung gati! -> You speak Balinese so well!
3. Icang ngomong ajak pisaga icang. -> I talk with my neighbor.
4. Ia ngomong indik tunanganne. -> He talks about his fiancée.
5. Mabaos. -> To speak (refined). [recycled — fresh sentences]
6. Ida mabaos alus gati. -> He / she (honored) speaks very gently.
7. Tiang mabaos sareng kelian. -> I speak with the banjar head.
8. Ipun mabaos indik upacara. -> He / she speaks about the ceremony.
9. Ngorta. -> To chat (familiar).
10. Meme ngorta ajak pisagane. -> Mother chats with the neighbor.
11. Cai ngorta lan nyen? -> Whom are you chatting with?
12. Ngorta di bale liang gati. -> Chatting at the pavilion is so pleasant.
13. Ningeh. -> To hear / listen (familiar). [recycled from Lesson 24 — fresh sentences]
14. Icang ningeh ia ngomong. -> I hear him talking.
15. Cai ningeh kulkul dibi? -> Did you hear the kulkul yesterday?
16. Ia nenten ningeh, ia pules. -> He doesn't hear — he is asleep.
17. Mireng. -> To hear / listen (refined). [recycled — fresh sentences]
18. Tiang mireng ida mabaos. -> I hear him / her (honored) speaking.
19. Ragane mireng gatra becik? -> Did you hear the good news?
20. Ipun mireng krebek iwau. -> He / she heard thunder earlier.
21. Dingehang. -> Listen (to this)! (familiar).
22. Dingehang icang! -> Listen to me!
23. Dingehang gatra niki! -> Listen to this news!
24. Cai dingehang icang, nggih. -> You listen to me, okay.
25. Nulis. -> To write (familiar). [recycled — fresh sentences]
26. Icang nulis adan icang di buku. -> I write my name in the book.
27. Nyai nulis napi? -> What are you writing?
28. Ia nulis liu gati. -> He writes so much.
29. Nyurat. -> To write (refined). [recycled — fresh sentences]
30. Tiang nyurat ring buku. -> I write in a book.
31. Ipun nyurat ring griya. -> He / she writes at the griya.
32. Nyurat Basa Bali dangan? -> Is writing Balinese easy?
33. Maca. -> To read (familiar). [recycled — fresh sentences]
34. Icang maca ring bale. -> I read at the pavilion.
35. Nyai maca buku nyai? -> Are you reading your book?
36. Maca Basa Bali dadi? -> Can you read Balinese?
37. Ngwacen. -> To read (refined). [recycled — fresh sentences]
38. Tiang ngwacen ring jero. -> I read at home.
39. Ragane ngwacen napi? -> What are you reading?
40. Ipun ngwacen buku kuna. -> He / she reads an old book.
41. Surat. -> Letter.
42. Ada surat baang icang. -> There is a letter for me.
43. Surat nika saking nyen? -> From whom is that letter?
44. Cai nulis surat baang nyen? -> To whom do you write a letter?
45. Pesan. -> Message.
46. Ada pesan baang cai. -> There is a message for you.
47. Pesan nika saking timpal icang. -> The message is from my friend.
48. Cai ningeh pesan icang? -> Did you hear my message?
49. Nelpon. -> To call (neutral).
50. Icang nelpon cai dibi. -> I called you yesterday.
51. Cai nelpon nyen jani? -> Whom are you calling now?
52. Nelpon dokter, enggalang! -> Call the doctor — hurry!
53. Hp. -> Cellphone. [recycled from Lesson 7 — fresh sentences]
54. Hp icang uug, icang sedih. -> My phone is broken — I'm sad.
55. Hp caine baru? -> Is your phone new?
56. Icang nelpon nganggo hp. -> I call using the phone.
57. Ngirim. -> To send (familiar).
58. Icang ngirim pesan baang cai. -> I send a message for you.
59. Cai ngirim surat? -> Do you send a letter?
60. Ia ngirim jinah baang memene. -> He sends money for his mother.
61. Ngaturang. -> To send / offer upward (refined). [recycled from Lesson 24 — fresh sentences]
62. Tiang ngaturang surat ring ida. -> I send a letter to him / her (honored).
63. Ipun ngaturang banten ring pura. -> He / she offers banten at the temple.
64. Ngaturang suksma ring ida. -> (I) offer thanks to him / her (honored).
65. Jawab. -> Answer (neutral).
66. Cai nenten jawab! -> You don't answer!
67. Jawab pesan icang! -> Answer my message!
68. Ia jawab takon icang. -> He answers my question.
69. Saur. -> Answer (refined).
70. Ipun nenten saur. -> He / she does not answer.
71. Saur pesan tiang, nggih. -> Answer my message, okay.
72. Ida saur alus gati. -> He / she (honored) answers very gently.
73. Nyambatang. -> To say / mention (familiar).
74. Ia nyambatang adan icang. -> He mentions my name.
75. Cai nyambatang napi? -> What are you saying?
76. Nyen nyambatang nika? -> Who said that?
77. Maosang. -> To say (refined). [recycled — fresh sentences]
78. Ida maosang napi ring tiang? -> What does he / she (honored) say to me?
79. Ipun maosang indik odalan. -> He / she speaks about the odalan.
80. Tiang maosang suksma ring krama. -> I say thanks to the people.
81. Takon. -> To ask (familiar). [recycled from Lesson 38 — fresh sentences]
82. Icang takon ka guru. -> I ask the teacher.
83. Cai takon apa jani? -> What are you asking now?
84. Takon ka kelian, ia nawang. -> Ask the banjar head — he knows.
85. Nakenang. -> To ask (refined). [recycled — fresh sentences]
86. Tiang nakenang indik upacara. -> I ask about the ceremony.
87. Ragane nakenang napi ring ida? -> What do you ask him / her (honored)?
88. Ipun nakenang wastan tiang. -> He / she asks my name.
89. Sapa. -> To greet (neutral).
90. Icang sapa pisaga icang. -> I greet my neighbor.
91. Cai sapa nyen dini? -> Whom do you greet here?
92. Sapa ida, nggih! -> Greet him / her (honored), okay!
93. Omong. -> Speech / words (familiar).
94. Omong caine becik. -> Your words are good.
95. Omong nika nenten patut. -> Those words are not right.
96. Omongne manis. -> His words are sweet.
97. Baos. -> Speech / words (refined).
98. Baos ida alus gati. -> His / her (honored) speech is very refined.
99. Baos nika becik. -> That speech is good.
100. Tiang mireng baos ida. -> I hear his / her (honored) words.
101. Raos. -> Speech (refined variant).
102. Raos ipun alus. -> His / her speech is refined.
103. Raos nika patut. -> That statement is right.
104. Tiang mireng raos becik. -> I hear good words.
105. Keneh. -> Thoughts / mind / heart (familiar).
106. Keneh icang liu jani. -> I have many thoughts now.
107. Keneh caine apa? -> What's on your mind?
108. Keneh icang liang dibi. -> My heart was happy yesterday.
109. Manah. -> Thoughts / mind (refined). [recycled from Lesson 34 — fresh sentences]
110. Manah tiang becik ring ida. -> My thoughts toward him / her (honored) are good.
111. Manah ragane kenten? -> Is your mind like that?
112. Manah ida agung. -> His / her (honored) heart is great.
113. Nengil. -> To keep quiet (familiar).
114. Cai nengil, nggih! -> You keep quiet, okay!
115. Ia nengil, nenten ngomong. -> He keeps quiet, doesn't talk.
116. Nengil, guru lakar mabaos! -> Quiet — the teacher will speak!
117. Meneng. -> To keep quiet (refined).
118. Meneng, nggih. -> Quiet, please.
119. Ipun meneng ring griya. -> He / she is quiet at the griya.
120. Ida meneng mireng baos pandita. -> He / she (honored) listens quietly to the priest's words.
121. Rahasia. -> Secret.
122. Nika rahasia icang! -> That's my secret!
123. Rahasia nika nyen nawang? -> Who knows that secret?
124. Rahasia nika baang icang lan cai. -> That secret is for me and you.

### Lesson 45: Materials

**Focus:** what things are made of — wood, bamboo, stone, metals, glass, paper, cloth, rope, leaves (the three kinds used in offerings), brick, cotton, silk, leather, bone.
**Grammar note:** materials follow the noun like adjectives: `tembok bata` "wall (of) brick". "Made of" can also use `nganggen` (using): `Undagi ngaryanang bale nganggen taru.`
**New in this lesson (teaching order):** kayu, taru (recycled — "wood" sense), tiing, pring, batu (recycled), bias (recycled), tanah (recycled), mas (recycled), perak (recycled), slaka, perunggu, besi, wesi, kaca, plastik, kertas, daluang, kain, wastra (recycled), kamben (recycled), tali, don (recycled), busung, slepan, ron, bata, semen, kapuk, sutra, kulit (recycled), tulang (recycled)
*(Cultural note: three leaf materials matter for offerings — `busung` (young coconut leaf, for canang), `slepan` (mature coconut leaf), `ron` (mature palm leaf). `Daluang` is traditional Balinese manuscript paper, still used for sacred texts.)*

1. Kayu. -> Wood (familiar).
2. Bale nika kayu. -> That pavilion is (made of) wood.
3. Meja icang kayu. -> My table is wood.
4. Kayu nika kuat. -> That wood is strong.
5. Taru. -> Tree / wood (refined). [recycled from Lesson 29 — "wood" sense]
6. Taru nika dadi kursi. -> That wood becomes a chair.
7. Undagi ngaryanang bale nganggen taru. -> The master builder makes the pavilion using wood.
8. Taru ring alas akeh. -> There is much wood in the forest.
9. Tiing. -> Bamboo (familiar).
10. Penjor nika tiing. -> That penjor is bamboo.
11. Tiing liu di sisi tukad. -> There is much bamboo beside the river.
12. Cai ngaba tiing? -> Are you carrying bamboo?
13. Pring. -> Bamboo (refined).
14. Pring nika tegeh. -> That bamboo is tall.
15. Bale nika pring. -> That pavilion is bamboo.
16. Pring ring carik ida akeh. -> There is much bamboo in his / her (honored) field.
17. Batu. -> Stone. [recycled from Lesson 29 — fresh sentences]
18. Tembok nika batu. -> That wall is stone.
19. Batu liu di pasih. -> There are many stones at the beach.
20. Batu nika baat gati. -> That stone is very heavy.
21. Bias. -> Sand. [recycled — fresh sentences]
22. Bias di pantai nika putih. -> The sand at that beach is white.
23. Bias lan batu ada ditu. -> Sand and stones are there.
24. Panak icang maen di bias. -> My child plays in the sand.
25. Tanah. -> Earth / soil / clay. [recycled — fresh sentences]
26. Tanah nika barak. -> That clay is red.
27. Ada tanah di carik. -> There is soil in the rice field.
28. Tanah nika becik baang punyan. -> That soil is good for trees.
29. Mas. -> Gold. [recycled from Lesson 19 — fresh sentences]
30. Gelang nika mas. -> That bracelet is gold.
31. Mas mahal gati jani. -> Gold is very expensive now.
32. Kalung biang icang mas. -> My mother's necklace is gold.
33. Perak. -> Silver. [recycled — fresh sentences]
34. Perak nika melah gati. -> That silver is so beautiful.
35. Icang dot kalung perak. -> I want a silver necklace.
36. Perak nika saking kota? -> Is that silver from the city?
37. Slaka. -> Silver (refined).
38. Slaka nika becik. -> That silver is beautiful.
39. Gelang ida slaka. -> His / her (honored) bracelet is silver.
40. Slaka lan perak pateh. -> Slaka and perak are the same.
41. Perunggu. -> Bronze.
42. Perunggu nika aji kuda? -> How much is that bronze (piece)?
43. Ada perunggu ring griya. -> There is bronze (ware) at the griya.
44. Perunggu nika kuna. -> That bronze (piece) is ancient.
45. Besi. -> Iron (neutral).
46. Pintu nika besi. -> That door is iron.
47. Besi nika kuat gati. -> The iron is very strong.
48. Tiang ngaba besi. -> I carry iron.
49. Wesi. -> Iron (refined).
50. Wesi nika aji kuda? -> How much is that iron?
51. Kursi nika wesi. -> That chair is iron.
52. Wesi lan kayu pateh kuat? -> Are iron and wood equally strong?
53. Kaca. -> Glass (material).
54. Jendela nika kaca. -> That window is glass.
55. Kaca nika uug! -> The glass is broken!
56. Gelas nika kaca? -> Is that cup glass?
57. Plastik. -> Plastic.
58. Gelas niki plastik. -> This cup is plastic.
59. Plastik nika kotor. -> That plastic is dirty.
60. Cai ngaba plastik? -> Are you carrying plastic?
61. Kertas. -> Paper (neutral).
62. Tiang nulis ring kertas. -> I write on paper.
63. Kertas nika putih. -> The paper is white.
64. Kertas icang ilang. -> My paper is lost.
65. Daluang. -> Traditional bark paper (refined).
66. Daluang nika kuna gati. -> That daluang is very old.
67. Ada surat kuna ring daluang. -> There is an old letter on daluang.
68. Daluang nika tipis. -> The daluang is thin.
69. Kain. -> Cloth (neutral).
70. Kain nika melah. -> That cloth is beautiful.
71. Baju nika kain Bali? -> Is that shirt Balinese cloth?
72. Icang meli kain di peken. -> I buy cloth at the market.
73. Wastra. -> Traditional cloth (refined). [recycled from Lesson 20 — fresh sentences]
74. Wastra Bali becik pisan. -> Balinese cloth is very beautiful.
75. Wastra nika aji seket tali. -> That cloth costs 50,000.
76. Wastra lan kain pateh? -> Are wastra and kain the same?
77. Kamben. -> Sarong / cloth (familiar). [recycled — fresh sentences]
78. Kamben nika kain barak. -> That sarong is red cloth.
79. Kamben icang suba kedas. -> My sarong is clean already.
80. Bapa nganggo kamben ka pura. -> Father wears a sarong to the temple.
81. Tali. -> Rope / string.
82. Tali nika panjang. -> The rope is long.
83. Tali nika kuat? -> Is the rope strong?
84. Cai ngaba tali? -> Are you carrying rope?
85. Don. -> Leaf (familiar). [recycled from Lesson 29 — fresh sentences]
86. Don punyan nika belus. -> The leaves are wet.
87. Don liu di tanah. -> There are many leaves on the ground.
88. Cai ngaba don? -> Are you carrying leaves?
89. Busung. -> Young coconut leaf (for offerings).
90. Busung nika pabuat canang. -> The young leaf is for canang.
91. Meme ngae banten nganggo busung. -> Mother makes offerings using young coconut leaf.
92. Busung niki gadang nguda. -> These young coconut leaves are light green.
93. Slepan. -> Mature coconut leaf.
94. Slepan nika kandel. -> The mature coconut leaf is thick.
95. Cai ngaba slepan? -> Are you carrying slepan?
96. Slepan lan busung nenten pateh. -> Slepan and busung are not the same.
97. Ron. -> Mature palm leaf (refined).
98. Ron nika gede. -> The palm leaf is big.
99. Ron lan slepan pateh? -> Are ron and slepan the same?
100. Tiang ngaba ron ka pura. -> I carry palm leaves to the temple.
101. Bata. -> Brick.
102. Tembok nika bata. -> That wall is brick.
103. Ada bata liu ditu. -> There are many bricks there.
104. Bata nika barak. -> The brick is red.
105. Semen. -> Cement.
106. Tiang meli semen. -> I buy cement.
107. Semen lan bata ngae tembok. -> Cement and brick make a wall.
108. Semen nika aji kuda? -> How much is the cement?
109. Kapuk. -> Cotton.
110. Bantal nika kapuk. -> The pillow is cotton.
111. Kapuk nika alus. -> The cotton is soft.
112. Kasur icang kapuk. -> My mattress is cotton.
113. Sutra. -> Silk.
114. Kain nika sutra. -> That cloth is silk.
115. Sutra mahal gati. -> Silk is very expensive.
116. Wastra sutra becik pisan. -> Silk cloth is very beautiful.
117. Kulit. -> Skin / leather. [recycled from Lesson 18 — fresh sentences]
118. Sabuk nika kulit. -> That belt is leather.
119. Kulit sampi nika kandel. -> The cowhide is thick.
120. Sepatu icang kulit. -> My shoes are leather.
121. Tulang. -> Bone. [recycled — fresh sentences]
122. Tulang nika putih. -> The bone is white.
123. Tulang iwak liu. -> The fish has many bones.
124. Tulang nika kuat, nenten dadi uug. -> The bone is strong — it can't break.

### Lesson 46: Verbs 4 — Creation

**Focus:** making and building, carving and painting, drawing, weaving, sewing, piercing, wrapping, burning, lighting, installing, preparing, tying, cutting, mixing, nailing, gluing, arranging, choosing.
**New in this lesson (teaching order):** ngae (recycled), ngaryanang (recycled), mbangun, wangun, ngukir, mulas, nggambar, ngulat, nenun, mait, nebek, ngaput, makaput, meborbor, nunjel, masang, mekarya, ngedap, nyiapang, nyait, ngiket, negul, mutus, ngetep, nebas, nyampur, ngadon, macek, ngempel, nyusun, milih
*(Notes: `ngulat` = weave bamboo/leaves; `nenun` = weave cloth. `mait` here = carve wood (familiar); `nyait` = sew. `mutus` covers both "cut" and "decide". `mekarya` (create/work, refined) is distinct from `makarya` (work, Lesson 11).)*

1. Ngae. -> To make (familiar). [recycled from Lesson 24 — fresh sentences]
2. Icang ngae jukung. -> I make a boat.
3. Nyai ngae banten? -> Do you make offerings?
4. Ia ngae meja kayu. -> He makes a wooden table.
5. Ngaryanang. -> To make (refined). [recycled — fresh sentences]
6. Undagi ngaryanang bale. -> The master builder makes a pavilion.
7. Ipun ngaryanang canang sari. -> He / she makes canang sari.
8. Ipun ngaryanang gebogan. -> He / she makes a gebogan offering.
9. Mbangun. -> To build (familiar).
10. Bapa mbangun umah. -> Father builds a house.
11. Cai lakar mbangun apa? -> What will you build?
12. Ia mbangun tembok bata. -> He builds a brick wall.
13. Wangun. -> To build (refined).
14. Ipun wangun jero alit. -> He / she builds a small house.
15. Tiang wangun paon anyar. -> I build a new kitchen.
16. Wangun lan mbangun pateh? -> Are wangun and mbangun the same?
17. Ngukir. -> To carve.
18. Undagi nika ngukir kayu. -> The master builder carves wood.
19. Cai bisa ngukir? -> Can you carve?
20. Ia ngukir bunga ring kayu. -> He carves flowers into the wood.
21. Mulas. -> To paint.
22. Icang mulas tembok. -> I paint the wall.
23. Cai mulas nganggo warna apa? -> What color do you paint with?
24. Ia mulas jelanan. -> He paints the gate.
25. Nggambar. -> To draw.
26. Panak icang nggambar gunung. -> My child draws a mountain.
27. Cai nggambar apa? -> What are you drawing?
28. Nggambar punyan dangan? -> Is drawing a tree easy?
29. Ngulat. -> To weave (bamboo / leaves).
30. Dadong ngulat busung. -> Grandmother weaves young coconut leaf.
31. Cai bisa ngulat? -> Can you weave?
32. Ngulat slepan nika keweh. -> Weaving slepan is hard.
33. Nenun. -> To weave (cloth).
34. Istri nika nenun kain. -> The woman weaves cloth.
35. Nyai bisa nenun? -> Can you weave cloth?
36. Ia nenun wastra becik. -> She weaves beautiful cloth.
37. Mait. -> To carve (wood) (familiar).
38. Bapa mait kayu. -> Father carves wood.
39. Cai mait apa? -> What are you carving?
40. Mait lan ngukir pateh? -> Are mait and ngukir the same?
41. Nebek. -> To pierce.
42. Icang nebek kayu. -> I pierce the wood.
43. Awas, nebek lima! -> Careful — (it) pierces the hand!
44. Ia nebek tiing. -> He pierces the bamboo.
45. Ngaput. -> To wrap.
46. Icang ngaput nasi. -> I wrap rice.
47. Meme ngaput jukut nganggo don. -> Mother wraps vegetables in leaves.
48. Ngaput nika dangan. -> Wrapping is easy.
49. Makaput. -> To wrap (food) (familiar).
50. Meme makaput nasi. -> Mother wraps rice.
51. Makaput lan ngaput patuh? -> Are makaput and ngaput the same?
52. Ia makaput be baang icang. -> He wraps fish for me.
53. Meborbor. -> To burn (familiar).
54. Ia meborbor kayu. -> He burns wood.
55. Cai meborbor don tuh. -> You burn dry leaves.
56. Meborbor, api gede! -> It burns — a big fire!
57. Nunjel. -> To burn / light (a fire) (familiar).
58. Icang nunjel dupa. -> I light incense.
59. Cai nunjel api? -> Do you light a fire?
60. Nunjel kayu tuh dangan. -> Lighting dry wood is easy.
61. Masang. -> To install / put up.
62. Bapa masang lampu. -> Father installs a lamp.
63. Cai masang penjor? -> Do you put up a penjor?
64. Masang pintu nika keweh. -> Installing that door is hard.
65. Mekarya. -> To create / work (refined).
66. Seniman nika mekarya ring bale. -> The artist creates at the pavilion.
67. Ida mekarya napi? -> What does he / she (honored) create?
68. Mekarya nika dangan? -> Is creating easy?
69. Ngedap. -> To prepare (familiar).
70. Icang ngedap banten. -> I prepare the offerings.
71. Cai ngedap apa jani? -> What are you preparing now?
72. Ngedap dedaaran baang tamiu. -> Prepare food for the guest.
73. Nyiapang. -> To prepare (refined).
74. Tiang nyiapang ajengan ring ida. -> I prepare food for him / her (honored).
75. Ipun nyiapang upacara. -> He / she prepares the ceremony.
76. Nyiapang napi ragane? -> What are you preparing?
77. Nyait. -> To sew.
78. Meme nyait baju icang. -> Mother sews my shirt.
79. Nyai nyait kamen? -> Do you sew sarongs?
80. Nyait nika keweh baang icang. -> Sewing is hard for me.
81. Ngiket. -> To tie (familiar).
82. Icang ngiket tali ring punyan. -> I tie the rope to the tree.
83. Cai ngiket sampi? -> Do you tie the cow?
84. Ngiket bungkusan nganggo tali. -> Tie the package with rope.
85. Negul. -> To tie (variant).
86. Bapa negul jaran. -> Father ties the horse.
87. Negul lan ngiket pateh? -> Are negul and ngikat the same?
88. Ia negul sampi ring punyan. -> He ties the cow to the tree.
89. Mutus. -> To cut / to decide.
90. Icang mutus tali. -> I cut the rope.
91. Cai mutus apa jani? -> What are you cutting / deciding now?
92. Ia mutus, ia lakar mulih. -> He decided — he will go home.
93. Ngetep. -> To cut (familiar).
94. Icang ngetep tiing. -> I cut bamboo.
95. Cai ngetep nganggo apa? -> What do you cut with?
96. Ngetep don baang banten. -> Cut leaves for the offerings.
97. Nebas. -> To slash / chop.
98. Bapa nebas tiing di carik. -> Father chops bamboo in the field.
99. Nebas punyan nika keweh. -> Chopping that tree is hard.
100. Cai nebas apa ditu? -> What are you chopping there?
101. Nyampur. -> To mix.
102. Icang nyampur kopi lan susu. -> I mix coffee and milk.
103. Cai nyampur warna apa? -> What colors are you mixing?
104. Nyampur basa lan uyah. -> Mix the spices and salt.
105. Ngadon. -> To mix (dough / spices).
106. Meme ngadon basa. -> Mother mixes the spices.
107. Ngadon nika keweh? -> Is mixing spices hard?
108. Ia ngadon jukut lan be. -> He mixes vegetables and meat.
109. Macek. -> To nail.
110. Bapa macek kayu. -> Father nails the wood.
111. Cai macek pintu? -> Do you nail the door?
112. Macek tembok nika keweh. -> Nailing into that wall is hard.
113. Ngempel. -> To glue.
114. Icang ngempel kertas. -> I glue paper.
115. Cai ngempel apa? -> What are you gluing?
116. Ngempel buku uug nika. -> Glue that broken book.
117. Nyusun. -> To arrange / stack.
118. Icang nyusun buku. -> I arrange the books.
119. Nyai nyusun piring? -> Do you stack the plates?
120. Nyusun canang baang upacara. -> Arrange canang for the ceremony.
121. Milih. -> To choose.
122. Icang milih buku niki. -> I choose this book.
123. Cai milih warna apa? -> What color do you choose?
124. Tiang milih wastra nika. -> I choose that cloth.

### Lesson 47: Celebrations

**Focus:** the great Balinese ceremonies and festivals — Galungan, Kuningan, Nyepi, Saraswati, Siwaratri, otonan, weddings, cremations, tooth-filing — plus sacred chanting, dance, and gamelan.
**Cultural note:** `otonan` is the Balinese birthday (every 210 days); `metatah`/`mepandes`/`potong gigi` all name the tooth-filing rite of passage; `ngaben` is the cremation, `palebon` its royal form; Galungan → Kuningan bookend the ten-day victory of dharma.
**New in this lesson (teaching order):** rerainan (recycled), odalan (recycled), piodalan, Galungan, Kuningan, Nyepi (recycled), Saraswati, Siwaratri, otonan, pawiwahan, nganten (recycled), ngaben, palebon, metatah, mepandes, potong gigi, rame (recycled), mabasan, makidung, tari, igel, megambel, suci (recycled), puput (recycled), rahayu (recycled), canang sari (recycled), mebanten (recycled), pemangku (recycled), tirta (recycled), nunas tirta, mebakti (recycled)

1. Rerainan. -> Holy day / festival (refined). [recycled from Lesson 35 — fresh sentences]
2. Benjang rerainan, krama ka pura. -> Tomorrow is a holy day — the people go to the temple.
3. Jani rerainan napi? -> What holy day is it now?
4. Rerainan pidan benjang? -> What holy day is tomorrow?
5. Odalan. -> Temple anniversary festival. [recycled — fresh sentences]
6. Odalan ring pura desa pidan? -> When is the village temple's odalan?
7. Odalan nika rame gati. -> The odalan is very festive.
8. Krama ngayah ring odalan. -> The people serve at the odalan.
9. Piodalan. -> Temple festival (refined).
10. Piodalan ring pura niki agung. -> The festival at this temple is grand.
11. Piodalan pidan, ragane uning? -> Do you know when the festival is?
12. Tiang lakar rauh ka piodalan. -> I will come to the festival.
13. Galungan. -> Galungan festival.
14. Galungan rauh benjang! -> Galungan comes tomorrow!
15. Galungan nika rerainan gede. -> Galungan is a great holy day.
16. Penjor liu di Galungan. -> There are many penjor at Galungan.
17. Kuningan. -> Kuningan festival.
18. Kuningan rauh sesampun Galungan. -> Kuningan comes after Galungan.
19. Kuningan lan Galungan nenten pateh. -> Kuningan and Galungan are not the same.
20. Ring Kuningan, krama mebakti. -> At Kuningan, the people pray.
21. Nyepi. -> Day of Silence. [recycled from Lesson 35 — fresh sentences]
22. Nyepi nika rerahinan suci. -> Nyepi is a holy day.
23. Ring Nyepi, krama ngoyong jumah. -> On Nyepi, the people stay home.
24. Nyepi benjang, ogoh-ogoh jani! -> Nyepi tomorrow — ogoh-ogoh today!
25. Saraswati. -> Day of Knowledge.
26. Saraswati nika rerahinan buku. -> Saraswati is the holy day of books.
27. Saraswati pidan? -> When is Saraswati?
28. Tiang seneng ring rerahinan Saraswati. -> I love the Saraswati holy day.
29. Siwaratri. -> Night of Siwa.
30. Siwaratri nika wengi suci. -> Siwaratri is a holy night.
31. Ring Siwaratri, krama meyasa. -> On Siwaratri, people practice austerities.
32. Siwaratri rauh ring bulan napi? -> In what month does Siwaratri come?
33. Otonan. -> Balinese birthday ceremony.
34. Otonan icang jani! -> My otonan is now!
35. Otonan caine pidan? -> When is your otonan?
36. Otonan nika upacara baang panak. -> Otonan is a ceremony for children.
37. Pawiwahan. -> Wedding (refined).
38. Pawiwahan ida agung gati. -> His / her (honored) wedding is very grand.
39. Pawiwahan nika pidan? -> When is the wedding?
40. Tiang rauh ka pawiwahan sawitra icang. -> I attend my friend's wedding.
41. Nganten. -> Newlyweds / wedding (familiar). [recycled from Lesson 43 — fresh sentences]
42. Ada nganten ring desa icang. -> There are newlyweds in my village.
43. Nganten nika liang gati. -> The bridal couple is so happy.
44. Icang ningal nganten di banjar. -> I see the bridal couple at the banjar.
45. Ngaben. -> Cremation ceremony.
46. Ngaben nika upacara gede. -> Ngaben is a great ceremony.
47. Ada ngaben di desa nika. -> There is a cremation in that village.
48. Ngaben pidan, ragane uning? -> When is the cremation — do you know?
49. Palebon. -> Royal cremation (refined).
50. Palebon nika agung gati. -> The royal cremation is very grand.
51. Palebon lan ngaben nenten pateh. -> Palebon and ngaben are not the same.
52. Krama rauh ka palebon. -> The people come to the royal cremation.
53. Metatah. -> Tooth-filing ceremony (familiar).
54. Metatah nika upacara baang truna truni. -> Metatah is a ceremony for the young people.
55. Cai suba metatah? -> Have you had your tooth filing?
56. Adi icang lakar metatah benjang. -> My younger sibling will have metatah tomorrow.
57. Mepandes. -> Tooth-filing ceremony (refined).
58. Mepandes nika upacara suci. -> Mepandes is a holy ceremony.
59. Ipun lakar mepandes. -> He / she will have mepandes.
60. Mepandes lan metatah pateh. -> Mepandes and metatah are the same.
61. Potong gigi. -> Tooth filing (phrase).
62. Potong gigi nika pidan? -> When is the tooth filing?
63. Cai suba potong gigi? -> Have you had your tooth filing?
64. Upacara potong gigi gede. -> The tooth-filing ceremony is big.
65. Rame. -> Festive / crowded. [recycled from Lesson 36 — fresh sentences]
66. Pura rame jani, ada odalan. -> The temple is crowded now — there's an odalan.
67. Rame gati Galungan! -> Galungan is so festive!
68. Cai demen tongos rame? -> Do you like crowded places?
69. Mabasan. -> To read/chant sacred texts.
70. Krama mabasan ring pura. -> The people chant sacred texts at the temple.
71. Cai bisa mabasan? -> Can you chant sacred texts?
72. Mabasan nika keweh? -> Is chanting sacred texts hard?
73. Makidung. -> To sing sacred songs.
74. Dadong makidung ring merajan. -> Grandmother sings sacred songs at the family shrine.
75. Makidung nika alus gati. -> Sacred singing is very refined.
76. Cai taen makidung? -> Have you ever sung sacred songs?
77. Tari. -> Dance (refined noun).
78. Tari Bali becik pisan. -> Balinese dance is very beautiful.
79. Tiang seneng ningal tari. -> I love watching dance.
80. Tari nika pidan? -> When is the dance?
81. Igel. -> Dance (familiar noun).
82. Igel Bali melah gati. -> Balinese dance is so beautiful.
83. Icang dot uning igel Bali. -> I want to know Balinese dance.
84. Igel nika pidan, cai uning? -> When is the dance — do you know?
85. Megambel. -> To play gamelan.
86. Truna desa nika megambel. -> The village youth play gamelan.
87. Cai bisa megambel? -> Can you play gamelan?
88. Megambel ring odalan rame gati. -> Playing gamelan at the odalan is so lively.
89. Suci. -> Sacred / holy. [recycled from Lesson 35 — fresh sentences]
90. Tirta nika suci. -> The holy water is sacred.
91. Pura nika genah suci gati. -> That temple is a very sacred place.
92. Nyepi nika rahina suci. -> Nyepi is a sacred day.
93. Puput. -> Completed / finished (refined). [recycled from Lesson 8 — fresh sentences]
94. Upacara sampun puput. -> The ceremony is completed.
95. Puput odalan, krama mantuk. -> The odalan is finished — the people go home.
96. Upacarane puput, nggih. -> The ceremony is done, yes.
97. Rahayu. -> Blessings / safe. [recycled from Lesson 2 — fresh sentences]
98. Dumogi krama rahayu. -> May the people be blessed.
99. Rahayu, upacara puput. -> Blessings — the ceremony is complete.
100. Rahayu baang ragane. -> Blessings for you.
101. Canang sari. -> Daily flower offering. [recycled from Lesson 35 — fresh sentences]
102. Meme ngae canang sari semeng-semeng. -> Mother makes canang sari every morning.
103. Canang sari ring ajeng jelanan. -> A canang sari is at the front of the gate.
104. Canang sari nika pabuat napi? -> What is that canang sari for?
105. Mebanten. -> To make offerings. [recycled — fresh sentences]
106. Krama mebanten ring piodalan. -> The people make offerings at the festival.
107. Nyai mebanten pidan? -> When do you make offerings?
108. Mebanten lan ngayah pateh? -> Are mebanten and ngayah the same?
109. Pemangku. -> Temple priest. [recycled from Lesson 28 — fresh sentences]
110. Pemangku muspa ring pura. -> The priest prays at the temple.
111. Pemangku ngicen tirta ring krama. -> The priest gives holy water to the people.
112. Pemangku nika jemet ngayah. -> The priest is diligent in service.
113. Tirta. -> Holy water. [recycled from Lesson 35 — fresh sentences]
114. Tirta saking pura nika suci. -> The holy water from that temple is sacred.
115. Tiang ngaba tirta mulih. -> I bring holy water home.
116. Tirta nika baang nyen? -> For whom is the holy water?
117. Nunas tirta. -> To ask for holy water (phrase).
118. Tiang nunas tirta ring pemangku. -> I ask the priest for holy water.
119. Krama nunas tirta ring pura. -> The people ask for holy water at the temple.
120. Ragane nunas tirta? -> Do you ask for holy water?
121. Mebakti. -> To pray. [recycled — fresh sentences]
122. Krama mebakti ring piodalan. -> The people pray at the festival.
123. Mebakti semeng, mebakti sanja. -> Pray in the morning, pray in the evening.
124. Tiang mebakti sareng semeton. -> I pray with my relatives.

### Lesson 48: Cause & Effect

**Focus:** the full causal toolkit — because, because of, so, that's why, the reason, until, although, but, or, and, with — plus results (`hasil`, `phala`), right/wrong, and sequence (`lantas`, `uli ento`). This lesson is a **review powerhouse**: almost every connector from Lesson 30 returns with fresh complex sentences.
**New in this lesson (teaching order):** krana (recycled), sawireh (recycled), sangkan (recycled), dadi (recycled), dados (recycled), ento krana (recycled), punika mawinan (recycled), awinan, kanti (recycled), ngantos (recycled), yadiastun (recycled), yadiapin (recycled), tapi (recycled), kewanten (recycled), nanging (recycled), utawi (recycled), wiadin (recycled), tur (recycled), miwah (recycled), ajak (recycled), sareng (recycled), lan (recycled), hasil, phala, pelih (recycled), iwang (recycled), beneh (recycled), patut (recycled), dadi ne, uli ento, lantas

1. Krana. -> Because (familiar). [recycled from Lesson 30 — fresh sentences]
2. Icang pules krana kenyel gati. -> I sleep because I'm very tired.
3. Krana odalan, desa rame. -> Because of the odalan, the village is lively.
4. Ia sebet krana tunanganne pegat. -> He is sad because his fiancée broke up (with him).
5. Sawireh. -> Because (familiar). [recycled — fresh sentences]
6. Ia ngoyong jumah sawireh sungkan. -> He stays home because he is ill.
7. Sawireh apa cai gedeg? -> Why are you angry?
8. Sawireh ujan bales, blabar ditu. -> Because of the heavy rain, there's a flood there.
9. Sangkan. -> Because of (refined). [recycled — fresh sentences]
10. Sangkan linuh, jero ida rusak. -> Because of the earthquake, his / her home is damaged.
11. Sangkan napi ida sungkan? -> Why is he / she (honored) ill?
12. Sangkan tiang lali, tiang iwang. -> Because I forgot, I was wrong.
13. Dadi. -> So / become (familiar). [recycled from Lesson 7 — fresh sentences]
14. Ujan bales, dadi icang ngoyong. -> Heavy rain, so I stay put.
15. Ia jemet, dadi ia bisa. -> He is diligent, so he can.
16. Kopi icang telah, dadi icang nginem teh. -> My coffee is gone, so I drink tea.
17. Dados. -> So / become (refined). [recycled — fresh sentences]
18. Sabeh bales, dados tiang nenten mrika. -> Heavy rain, so I do not go there.
19. Ipun jemet, dados ipun uning liu. -> He / she is diligent, so he knows a lot.
20. Ida sungkan, dados ida ngoyong ring jero. -> He / she (honored) is ill, so he stays home.
21. Ento krana. -> That's why (familiar). [recycled — fresh sentences]
22. Icang kenyel, ento krana icang mareren. -> I'm tired — that's why I rest.
23. Montor icang uug, ento krana icang majalan. -> My motorbike is broken — that's why I walk.
24. Ia sakit, ento krana ia jumah. -> He's sick — that's why he's home.
25. Punika mawinan. -> That's why (refined). [recycled — fresh sentences]
26. Tiang kimud, punika mawinan tiang meneng. -> I am shy — that's why I am quiet.
27. Margine rusak, punika mawinan tiang nenten mrika. -> The road is damaged — that's why I don't go there.
28. Ipun jemet, punika mawinan pakaryanne becik. -> He / she is diligent — that's why the work is good.
29. Awinan. -> Reason / cause (refined).
30. Awinan napi ida krodha? -> What is the reason he / she (honored) is angry?
31. Tiang ten uning awinan nika. -> I don't know the reason for that.
32. Awinan ipun sungkan napi? -> What is the reason he / she is ill?
33. Kanti. -> Until (familiar). [recycled — fresh sentences]
34. Krama ngayah kanti sanja. -> The people serve until evening.
35. Icang megawe kanti peteng. -> I work until night.
36. Kanti pidan cai dini? -> Until when are you here?
37. Ngantos. -> Until / to wait for (refined). [recycled — fresh sentences]
38. Tiang ngantos ida ring pura. -> I wait for him / her (honored) at the temple.
39. Ipun makarya ngantos wengi. -> He / she works until night.
40. Ngantos pidan ragane driki? -> Until when are you here?
41. Yadiastun. -> Although (refined). [recycled — fresh sentences]
42. Yadiastun keweh, tiang jemet malajah. -> Although it's hard, I study diligently.
43. Yadiastun ida lingsir, ida kuat. -> Although he / she (honored) is old, he is strong.
44. Yadiastun tiwas, ia liang. -> Although poor, he is happy.
45. Yadiapin. -> Although (familiar). [recycled — fresh sentences]
46. Yadiapin kenyel, icang megawe. -> Even though tired, I work.
47. Yadiapin joh, ia rauh. -> Even though it's far, he comes.
48. Yadiapin cai jomblo, cai liang! -> Even though you're single, you're happy!
49. Tapi. -> But (neutral). [recycled — fresh sentences]
50. Kopi niki jaen, tapi panas gati. -> This coffee is delicious, but very hot.
51. Icang dot mrika, tapi joh. -> I want to go there, but it's far.
52. Ia sugih, tapi nenten liang. -> He is rich, but not happy.
53. Kewanten. -> But (refined). [recycled — fresh sentences]
54. Ajengan nika jaen, kewanten lalah gati. -> The food is tasty, but very spicy.
55. Genah nika melah, kewanten cupit. -> The place is nice, but narrow.
56. Ipun alit, kewanten kuat. -> He / she is small, but strong.
57. Utawi. -> Or (refined). [recycled — fresh sentences]
58. Ragane numbas wastra utawi kain? -> Do you buy wastra or kain?
59. Ipun dokter utawi balian? -> Is he a doctor or a healer?
60. Mani utawi benjang, tiang rauh. -> Tomorrow either way — I will come.
61. Wiadin. -> Or (familiar). [recycled — fresh sentences]
62. Cai dot jukut wiadin buah? -> Do you want vegetables or fruit?
63. Cai ngaba montor wiadin sepeda? -> Do you take the motorbike or the bicycle?
64. Ia dokter wiadin guru? -> Is he a doctor or a teacher?
65. Tur. -> And / and then (refined). [recycled from Lesson 26 — fresh sentences]
66. Ida rauh tur muspa ring pura. -> He / she (honored) arrives and prays at the temple.
67. Ipun mabasan tur makidung. -> He / she chants and sings sacred songs.
68. Tiang malajah tur ngwacen buku. -> I study and read books.
69. Miwah. -> And (refined). [recycled from Lesson 1 — fresh sentences]
70. Krama miwah pemangku wénten ring pura. -> The people and the priest are at the temple.
71. Tiang miwah semeton tiang rauh. -> My relatives and I arrive.
72. Wastra miwah kamben ida becik. -> His / her (honored) cloth and sarong are fine.
73. Ajak. -> With (familiar). [recycled from Lesson 26 — fresh sentences]
74. Icang ka odalan ajak timpal. -> I go to the odalan with a friend.
75. Cai megambel ajak nyen? -> Whom do you play gamelan with?
76. Ia metatah ajak adine. -> He has his tooth filing together with his sibling.
77. Sareng. -> With (refined). [recycled — fresh sentences]
78. Tiang ka pura sareng semeton. -> I go to the temple with relatives.
79. Ida mebakti sareng krama. -> He / she (honored) prays with the people.
80. Ragane sareng sira mrika? -> With whom do you go there?
81. Lan. -> And. [recycled from Lesson 1 — fresh sentences]
82. Galungan lan Kuningan rauh. -> Galungan and Kuningan come.
83. Icang lan cai ngayah ring pura. -> You and I serve at the temple.
84. Tari lan igel pateh? -> Are tari and igel the same?
85. Hasil. -> Result / yield (neutral).
86. Hasil carik icang liu. -> My rice field's yield is plentiful.
87. Hasil pagawean caine melah? -> Is the result of your work good?
88. Hasil upacara nika rahayu. -> The ceremony's result is blessing.
89. Phala. -> Fruit (of deeds) / result (refined).
90. Phala pakaryan becik nika becik. -> The fruit of good work is good.
91. Phala nika napi? -> What is the result?
92. Phala pakaryan ipun becik. -> The fruit of his / her work is good.
93. Pelih. -> Wrong / mistake. [recycled from Lesson 3 — fresh sentences]
94. Nika pelih tiang, ampura. -> That was my mistake — sorry.
95. Yen cai pelih, ngidih pelih. -> If you're wrong, apologize.
96. Pelih nika alit, nanging pelih. -> That mistake is small, but a mistake.
97. Beneh. -> Right / correct. [recycled from Lesson 1 — fresh sentences]
98. Manah ragane beneh. -> Your opinion is right.
99. Omong gurune beneh. -> The teacher's words are right.
100. Beneh utawi pelih? -> Right or wrong?
101. Iwang. -> Wrong (refined). [recycled from Lesson 3 — fresh sentences]
102. Tiang iwang mabaos, sinampura. -> I spoke wrongly — apologies.
103. Nika iwang, nenten patut. -> That is wrong, not right.
104. Ipun nenten iwang. -> He / she is not wrong.
105. Patut. -> Right / proper. [recycled from Lesson 1 — fresh sentences]
106. Patut krama ngayah ring pura. -> It is proper for people to serve at the temple.
107. Manah ida patut. -> His / her (honored) opinion is right.
108. Patut utawi iwang? -> Right or wrong?
109. Dadi ne. -> The result is… (familiar phrase).
110. Ia mayus, dadi ne hasil pagawean jelek. -> He is lazy — the result is bad work.
111. Ujan bales, dadi ne blabar. -> Heavy rain — the result is flood.
112. Icang lali, dadi ne icang pelih. -> I forgot — the result is I was wrong.
113. Uli ento. -> From that (familiar phrase).
114. Uli ento, icang jejeh. -> From that, I am scared.
115. Uli ento ia liang. -> From that, he is happy.
116. Uli ento dadi blabar. -> From that came the flood.
117. Lantas. -> Then / after that (neutral).
118. Icang mireng kulkul, lantas icang ka banjar. -> I hear the kulkul, then I go to the banjar.
119. Ia bangun semeng, lantas mandi. -> He wakes in the morning, then bathes.
120. Upacara puput, lantas krama mepamit. -> The ceremony ends, then the people take leave.
121. Lantas, cai kija? -> So then — where are you going?
122. Nanging. -> But (familiar). [recycled from Lesson 1 — fresh sentences]
123. Ia alit, nanging kendel. -> He is small, but cheerful.
124. Kopi nika pait, nanging jaen. -> The coffee is bitter, but delicious.
