### Lesson 49: Reflexive Actions

**Focus:** doing things by oneself/alone, grooming verbs (mirror, comb, haircut, shave, powder, bathe), talking to oneself, and humble/proposal verbs.
**Grammar note:** "myself/alone" = `padidi` (Andap) / `ngraga` (Alus), placed after the subject or verb: `Icang padidi.` "I (am) alone." Grooming verbs use the ma- prefix and need no object: `nyisir` (comb hair), `magunting` (cut hair), `mecukur` (shave), `mebedak` (powder).
**New in this lesson (teaching order):** padidi, ngraga, pedidi, ngaraga, tiang padidi, titiang ngraga, cai padidi, ragane ngraga, awak (recycled), angga (recycled), makaca, mepayas (recycled), mabusana, mandus, masiram (recycled), manjus (recycled), nyisir, magunting, mecukur, mebedak, matangi (recycled), bangun (recycled), ngomong padidi, mabaos ngraga, ngenot, madik, ngebuh, matilesang, sebet (recycled), liang (recycled), keneh padidi

1. Padidi. -> Oneself / alone (familiar).
2. Icang padidi dini. -> I am here alone.
3. Cai lakar kema padidi? -> Will you go there alone?
4. Ia ngae nika padidi. -> He made that himself.
5. Ngraga. -> Oneself (refined).
6. Tiang ngraga mrika. -> I go there myself.
7. Ida nenten ngraga, sareng krama. -> He / she (honored) is not alone — with the people.
8. Ipun ngaryanang ngraga. -> He / she makes it himself.
9. Pedidi. -> Alone (familiar).
10. Icang jumah pedidi. -> I am home alone.
11. Cai pedidi ditu? -> Are you alone there?
12. Pedidi, nanging nenten sebet. -> Alone, but not sad.
13. Ngaraga. -> Alone / oneself (refined).
14. Tiang driki ngaraga. -> I am here alone.
15. Ipun mantuk ngaraga? -> Does he / she go home alone?
16. Ngaraga, tiang ten jejeh. -> Alone, I am not afraid.
17. Tiang padidi. -> I myself (phrase).
18. Tiang padidi ngae jukut nika. -> I myself made that dish.
19. Nggih, tiang padidi rauh. -> Yes, I myself came.
20. Tiang padidi nenten uning. -> I myself don't know.
21. Titiang ngraga. -> I myself (refined phrase).
22. Titiang ngraga ngaturang suksma. -> I myself offer thanks.
23. Nggih, titiang ngraga rauh. -> Yes, I myself came.
24. Titiang ngraga nenten uning. -> I myself don't know.
25. Cai padidi. -> You yourself (phrase).
26. Cai padidi ngae nika? -> Did you yourself make that?
27. Cai padidi lakar kema? -> Will you yourself go there?
28. Cai padidi nawang unduk nika. -> You yourself know that matter.
29. Ragane ngraga. -> You yourself (refined phrase).
30. Ragane ngraga rauh? -> Did you yourself come?
31. Ragane ngraga ngaryanang nika? -> Did you yourself make that?
32. Nggih, ragane ngraga uning. -> Yes, you yourself know.
33. Awak. -> Body / oneself (familiar). [recycled from Lesson 18 — fresh sentences]
34. Awak icang sakit dibi, jani luungan. -> My body was sick yesterday — now better.
35. Awakne kedas? -> Is his body clean?
36. Icang ngedasang awak. -> I clean my body.
37. Angga. -> Body / oneself (refined). [recycled — fresh sentences]
38. Angga ipun lesu jani. -> His / her body is weak now.
39. Angga ida becik jani. -> His / her (honored) body is well now.
40. Tiang ngrasa angga tiang kuat. -> I feel my body is strong.
41. Makaca. -> To look in the mirror.
42. Nyai makaca liu gati! -> You look in the mirror so much!
43. Icang makaca semeng. -> I look in the mirror in the morning.
44. Ia makaca, ia mepayas. -> She looks in the mirror, she dresses up.
45. Mepayas. -> To dress up / adorn oneself. [recycled from Lesson 20 — fresh sentences]
46. Nyai mepayas lakar ka pura? -> Are you dressing up to go to the temple?
47. Mepayas liu, jegeg gati! -> All dressed up — so beautiful!
48. Ia mepayas padidi. -> She dresses up by herself.
49. Mabusana. -> To dress (traditionally) (refined).
50. Tiang mabusana lakar ka odalan. -> I dress traditionally for the odalan.
51. Ida mabusana becik gati. -> He / she (honored) is beautifully dressed.
52. Ipun mabusana ring kamar. -> He / she dresses in the room.
53. Mandus. -> To bathe oneself (familiar).
54. Icang mandus semeng. -> I bathe in the morning.
55. Cai suba mandus? -> Have you bathed?
56. Ia mandus di tukad. -> He bathes in the river.
57. Masiram. -> To bathe (refined). [recycled from Lesson 11 — fresh sentences]
58. Ida masiram jani. -> He / she (honored) is bathing now.
59. Ipun sampun masiram? -> Has he / she bathed?
60. Masiram semeng, mabusana raris ka pura. -> Bathe in the morning, dress, then go to the temple.
61. Manjus. -> To bathe (familiar). [recycled from Lesson 11 — fresh sentences]
62. Icang manjus di tukad jani. -> I bathe in the river now.
63. Cai manjus semeng wiadin tengai? -> Do you bathe in the morning or at midday?
64. Manjus lan mandus pateh? -> Are manjus and mandus the same?
65. Nyisir. -> To comb (one's hair).
66. Nyai nyisir bok. -> You comb your hair.
67. Icang nyisir bok semeng. -> I comb my hair in the morning.
68. Panak icang nyisir padidi. -> My child combs (her hair) by herself.
69. Magunting. -> To cut (one's) hair.
70. Icang lakar magunting. -> I'm going to get a haircut.
71. Cai magunting pidan? -> When will you cut your hair?
72. Ia magunting bokne padidi. -> He cuts his own hair.
73. Mecukur. -> To shave.
74. Bapa mecukur semeng. -> Father shaves in the morning.
75. Cai mecukur dija? -> Where do you shave?
76. Ia mecukur padidi. -> He shaves by himself.
77. Mebedak. -> To put on powder.
78. Mbok icang mebedak. -> My older sister puts on powder.
79. Ia mebedak padidi. -> She puts on powder by herself.
80. Nyai mebedak jani? -> Are you putting on powder now?
81. Matangi. -> To wake up (refined). [recycled from Lesson 11 — fresh sentences]
82. Ida matangi galang. -> He / she (honored) wakes at dawn.
83. Ipun sampun matangi. -> He / she is already awake.
84. Ragane matangi jam kuda? -> What time do you wake up?
85. Bangun. -> To wake up (familiar). [recycled — fresh sentences]
86. Icang bangun jam limang semeng. -> I wake up at five in the morning.
87. Cai bangun dibi tengai? -> Did you wake late yesterday?
88. Bangun, lantas manjus. -> Wake up, then bathe.
89. Ngomong padidi. -> To talk to oneself (phrase).
90. Icang ngomong padidi. -> I talk to myself.
91. Cai ngomong padidi? -> Are you talking to yourself?
92. Ia ngomong padidi, icang jejeh. -> He talks to himself — I'm scared.
93. Mabaos ngraga. -> To talk to oneself (refined phrase).
94. Tiang mabaos ngraga. -> I talk to myself.
95. Ipun mabaos ngraga ring jero. -> He / she talks to himself at home.
96. Mabaos ngraga, nenten jelek. -> Talking to oneself is not bad.
97. Ngenot. -> To look at oneself.
98. Ia ngenot ring kaca. -> He looks at himself in the mirror.
99. Cai ngenot padidi? -> Do you look at yourself?
100. Nyai ngenot liu gati! -> You look at yourself so much!
101. Madik. -> To propose marriage (familiar).
102. Truna nika lakar madik. -> That young man will propose.
103. Madik nika unduk apa? -> Proposing — what kind of matter is that?
104. Beli icang lakar madik bajang nika. -> My older brother will propose to that young woman.
105. Ngebuh. -> To show off (familiar).
106. Cai ngebuh gati! -> You show off so much!
107. Ngebuh nenten becik. -> Showing off is not good.
108. Ia ngebuh montor barune. -> He shows off his new motorbike.
109. Matilesang. -> To humble oneself (refined).
110. Tiang matilesang ring ajeng ida. -> I humble myself before him / her (honored).
111. Matilesang nika becik. -> Humbling oneself is good.
112. Ipun matilesang, nenten ngebuh. -> He / she is humble, doesn't show off.
113. Sebet. -> Sad. [recycled from Lesson 16 — fresh sentences]
114. Tiang sebet ngoyong padidi. -> I am sad staying alone.
115. Sebet nika dadi uli keneh padidi? -> Does sadness come from one's own mind?
116. Ida sebet? Nenten dados kenten. -> He / she (honored) is sad? That must not be.
117. Liang. -> Happy. [recycled — fresh sentences]
118. Icang liang ngoyong padidi. -> I am happy staying alone.
119. Liang padidi, nenten sebet. -> Happy alone, not sad.
120. Cai liang pedidi dini? -> Are you happy alone here?
121. Keneh padidi. -> One's own mind / will (phrase).
122. Nika keneh padidi caine. -> That is your own will.
123. Keneh padidi tiang lakar malajah. -> By my own will, I want to study.
124. Uli keneh padidi, icang jemet. -> From my own will, I am diligent.

### Lesson 50: Environment

**Focus:** the environment — nature, earth, trash and cleanliness, planting, trees, rivers and beaches, floods and landslides, protection and pollution.
**Grammar note:** purpose/slogans use `mangda` (so that) + adjective: `Krama ngedasang alas mangda lestari.` "The people care for the forest so it endures."
**New in this lesson (teaching order):** mis,, lingkungan, alam, gumi (recycled), jagat (recycled), luu, sampah, nyampat (recycled), bersih (recycled), kedas (recycled), daki (recycled), kotor (recycled), ngentungang luu, nanem, mula, pohon, punyan (recycled), taru (recycled), pasih (recycled), segara (recycled), tukad (recycled), toya (recycled), yeh (recycled), banjir (recycled), blabar (recycled), tanah embid, asri, lestari, alas (recycled), lindungi, polusi

1. Lingkungan. -> Environment / surroundings.
2. Lingkungan icang kedas. -> My surroundings are clean.
3. Cai ngedasang lingkungan caine? -> Do you clean your surroundings?
4. Lingkungan niki melah. -> This environment is good.
5. Alam. -> Nature.
6. Alam Bali melah gati. -> Balinese nature is so beautiful.
7. Tiang demen alam niki. -> I love this nature.
8. Alam rusak, dadi banjir. -> Nature is damaged — the result is floods.
9. Gumi. -> Earth / land (familiar). [recycled from Lesson 4 — fresh sentences]
10. Gumi Bali suci. -> The land of Bali is sacred.
11. Krama ngedasang gumi. -> The people clean the land.
12. Gumi niki asri. -> This land is lush and beautiful.
13. Jagat. -> Earth / world (refined). [recycled — fresh sentences]
14. Jagat niki agung gati. -> This world is so vast.
15. Jagat niki becik, nanging kotor ditu-ditu. -> The world is good, but dirty here and there.
16. Dumogi jagat niki rahayu. -> May this world be blessed.
17. Luu. -> Trash (familiar).
18. Luu liu di jalan. -> There is much trash on the road.
19. Luu nika kotor gati. -> That trash is very dirty.
20. Luu plastik liu di pasih. -> There is much plastic trash on the beach.
22. Wénten mis ring margi. -> There is trash on the road.
23. Mis ditu liu. -> There is much trash there.
24. Ngedasang sampah nika pabuat jagat. -> Cleaning the trash is for the world.
25. Sampah. -> Trash (neutral).
26. Sampah liu di peken. -> There is much trash at the market.
27. Sampah plastik nenten melah baang tanah. -> Plastic trash is not good for the soil.
28. Sampah ring desa nika bedik. -> There is little trash in that village.
29. Nyampat. -> To sweep. [recycled from Lesson 27 — fresh sentences]
30. Krama nyampat jalan desa. -> The people sweep the village road.
31. Nyampat semeng-semeng dadi biasa. -> Sweeping every morning becomes a habit.
32. Nyai nyampat dija? -> Where do you sweep?
33. Bersih. -> Clean. [recycled from Lesson 20 — fresh sentences]
34. Desa nika bersih gati. -> That village is very clean.
35. Tukad niki bersih, nenten daki. -> This river is clean, not dirty.
36. Cai demen genah bersih? -> Do you like clean places?
37. Kedas. -> Clean (familiar). [recycled — fresh sentences]
38. Natah icang kedas jani. -> My courtyard is clean now.
39. Kedas gati peken nika jani! -> That market is so clean now!
40. Paon caine kedas? -> Is your kitchen clean?
41. Daki. -> Dirty. [recycled — fresh sentences]
42. Sisi tukad nika daki. -> The riverbank is dirty.
43. Ditu daki, liu luu. -> It's dirty there — lots of trash.
44. Baju caine daki, numbah! -> Your clothes are dirty — wash them!
45. Kotor. -> Dirty (neutral). [recycled — fresh sentences]
46. Jalan nika kotor gati. -> That road is so dirty.
47. Pasih kotor sangkan luu. -> The beach is dirty because of trash.
48. Kotor nenten melah baang krama. -> Dirt is not good for the people.
49. Ngentungang luu. -> To throw away trash (phrase).
50. Cai ngentungang luu dija? -> Where do you throw trash?
51. Ngentungang luu di tukad nenten patut! -> Throwing trash in the river is not right!
52. Krama ngentungang luu ring tongos sampah. -> People throw trash at the garbage place.
53. Nanem. -> To plant (familiar).
54. Icang nanem punyan di natah. -> I plant a tree in the courtyard.
55. Cai nanem apa? -> What are you planting?
56. Krama nanem bunga ring sisi jalan. -> People plant flowers by the roadside.
57. Mula. -> To plant (familiar variant).
58. Bapa mula jukut di carik. -> Father plants vegetables in the field.
59. Cai mula punyan apa? -> What tree are you planting?
60. Mula lan nanem pateh? -> Are mula and nanem the same?
61. Pohon. -> Tree (neutral).
62. Pohon nika tegeh gati. -> That tree is so tall.
63. Ada pohon ijo di sisi umah. -> There is a green tree beside the house.
64. Pohon nika kuna, nenten dadi nebas. -> That tree is old — it must not be cut.
65. Punyan. -> Tree (familiar). [recycled from Lesson 29 — fresh sentences]
66. Punyan icang suba gede. -> My tree is already big.
67. Punyan di natah icang akeh. -> There are many trees in my courtyard.
68. Bojog nongos di punyan. -> The monkey lives in the tree.
69. Taru. -> Tree / wood (refined). [recycled — fresh sentences]
70. Taru agung nika wénten ring kaja. -> That great tree stands to the north.
71. Taru kuna nika suci. -> That old tree is sacred.
72. Krama nanem taru ring sisi margi. -> People plant trees along the road.
73. Pasih. -> Beach. [recycled from Lesson 12 — fresh sentences]
74. Pasih nika kotor, krama ngedasang. -> The beach is dirty — the people clean it.
75. Pasih nika asri gati. -> The beach is so beautiful.
76. Cai ngentungang luu di pasih? -> Do you throw trash on the beach?
77. Segara. -> Sea. [recycled — fresh sentences]
78. Segara nika kotor sangkan sampah. -> The sea is dirty because of trash.
79. Tiang demen segara bersih. -> I love a clean sea.
80. Segara lan pasih pateh? -> Are segara and pasih the same?
81. Tukad. -> River. [recycled — fresh sentences]
82. Luu liu di tukad, dadi kotor. -> Much trash in the river — it becomes dirty.
83. Tukad bersih, toya bersih. -> A clean river (means) clean water.
84. Cai manjus di tukad kotor? -> Do you bathe in a dirty river?
85. Toya. -> Water (refined). [recycled from Lesson 5 — fresh sentences]
86. Toya tukad nika bersih? -> Is the river water clean?
87. Toya nika daki, nenten dados nginem. -> That water is dirty — one must not drink it.
88. Toya bersih penting gati. -> Clean water is very important.
89. Yeh. -> Water (familiar). [recycled — fresh sentences]
90. Yeh tukad nika daki jani. -> The river water is dirty now.
91. Yeh bersih, icang demen. -> Clean water — I like it.
92. Yeh banjir nika kotor gati. -> The floodwater is so dirty.
93. Banjir. -> Flood (neutral). [recycled from Lesson 23 — fresh sentences]
94. Banjir sangkan luu liu. -> Flood because of much trash.
95. Banjir nika rusak desa. -> The flood damaged the village.
96. Yen ujan bales, dadi banjir. -> If it rains heavily, there is a flood.
97. Blabar. -> Flood (familiar). [recycled — fresh sentences]
98. Blabar jani sangkan tukad kotor. -> Flood now because the river is dirty.
99. Blabar nika gede, krama ngungsi. -> The flood is big — people evacuate.
100. Yen krama ngedasang tukad, nenten blabar. -> If people clean the river, there is no flood.
101. Tanah embid. -> Landslide (phrase).
102. Ada tanah embid di kaja. -> There is a landslide up-island.
103. Tanah embid sangkan ujan bales. -> Landslide because of heavy rain.
104. Tanah embid nika ngae jalan rusak. -> The landslide damaged the road.
105. Asri. -> Beautiful and green / lush.
106. Desa nika asri gati. -> That village is so lush.
107. Carik asri, tiang demen. -> Lush rice fields — I love them.
108. Alam asri pabuat krama. -> Lush nature is for the people.
109. Lestari. -> Preserved / enduring.
110. Alam niki lestari, gumi rahayu. -> This nature preserved, the land blessed.
111. Krama ngedasang alas mangda lestari. -> People care for the forest so it endures.
112. Lestari nika penting baang gumi. -> Preservation is important for the land.
113. Alas. -> Forest. [recycled from Lesson 12 — fresh sentences]
114. Alas nika asri, nenten dadi nebas taru. -> The forest is lush — trees must not be cut.
115. Alas kotor sangkan sampah? -> Is the forest dirty because of trash?
116. Alas nika genah sato. -> The forest is a home for animals.
117. Lindungi. -> To protect (neutral).
118. Lindungi alam niki! -> Protect this nature!
119. Krama lindungi segara. -> The people protect the sea.
120. Tiang lindungi gumi icang. -> I protect my land.
121. Polusi. -> Pollution.
122. Polusi liu di kota. -> There is much pollution in the city.
123. Polusi liu, krama sungkan. -> Much pollution — people get sick.
124. Polusi ring kota, nenten ring desa. -> Pollution is in the city, not in the village.

---

125. Mis ditu liu. -> There is much trash there.
126. Ngedasang mis nika pabuat jagat. -> Cleaning up trash is for the world.

### Lesson 51: Imperatives

**Focus:** commands, invitations, and prohibitions — go/come, take, make, don't!, please, let's go, hurry/slow down, stay, be quiet, listen, look, give, stop, finish.
**Grammar note:** prohibitions: `da`/`eda` (familiar) and `sampunang` (refined) + verb: `Da kema!` / `Sampunang mrika!` Polite commands pair with `durus`/`raris`/`silakan`. Reduplicated `adeng-adeng`/`alon-alon` = "slowly, slow down!".
**New in this lesson (teaching order):** kema (recycled), mrika (recycled), mai (recycled), mriki (recycled), jemak (recycled), ambil (recycled), gaenang, karyanin, da, sampunang, eda, durus (recycled), raris (recycled), silakan (recycled), tulung (recycled), jalan (recycled), ngiring (recycled), enggalang (recycled), gelisang, adeng-adeng (recycled), alon-alon, oyong, meneng (recycled), nengil (recycled), dingehang (recycled), pirengang, tolih, cingak, baang (recycled), icen, suud (recycled), puputang

1. Kema. -> Go there! [recycled from Lesson 12 — fresh commands]
2. Kema jani, suba sanja! -> Go now, it's already evening!
3. Kema ka peken, meli jukut! -> Go to the market, buy vegetables!
4. Kema, icang ngantos dini. -> Go — I'll wait here.
5. Mrika. -> Go there! (polite). [recycled — fresh commands]
6. Mrika, ida sampun ngantos. -> Go there — he / she (honored) is already waiting.
7. Durus mrika, ragane. -> Please go there.
8. Mrika, dumogi rahayu. -> Go there — may you be blessed.
9. Mai. -> Come here! [recycled — fresh commands]
10. Mai, icang baang kopi! -> Come here — I'll give you coffee!
11. Mai enggal! -> Come quickly!
12. Nyai mai, icang nulungin. -> Come here — I'll help you.
13. Mriki. -> Come here! (polite). [recycled — fresh commands]
14. Mriki, tiang ngicen toya. -> Come here — I'll give you water.
15. Ragane mriki, durus. -> Please come here.
16. Mriki jani, nggih. -> Come here now, please.
17. Jemak. -> Take (it)! [recycled from Lesson 24 — fresh commands]
18. Jemak buku nika! -> Take that book!
19. Jemak sapunne, nyampat jani! -> Take the broom — sweep now!
20. Jemak nika, baang icang. -> Take that and give it to me.
21. Ambil. -> Take (it)! (polite). [recycled — fresh commands]
22. Ambil ajengan niki. -> Please take this food.
23. Ambil toya tirta nika. -> Take that holy water.
24. Durus ambil buku nika. -> Please take that book.
25. Gaenang. -> Make it! (familiar).
26. Gaenang icang kopi! -> Make me coffee!
27. Gaenang bungkusan baang icang. -> Make a package for me.
28. Gaenang nika enggal! -> Make that quickly!
29. Karyanin. -> Make it! (polite).
30. Karyanin canang sari, nggih. -> Please make canang sari.
31. Karyanin ajengan baang ida. -> Make food for him / her (honored).
32. Karyanin nika raris. -> Please make that.
33. Da. -> Don't! (familiar).
34. Da majalan kema, joh! -> Don't walk there — it's far!
35. Da ngentungang luu di tukad! -> Don't throw trash in the river!
36. Da jejeh, icang dini. -> Don't be scared — I'm here.
37. Sampunang. -> Don't! (polite).
38. Sampunang mrika, adoh. -> Don't go there — it's far.
39. Sampunang sebet, nggih. -> Don't be sad, okay.
40. Sampunang ngentungang luu ring segara! -> Don't throw trash into the sea!
41. Eda. -> Don't! (familiar variant).
42. Eda kema, ada baya! -> Don't go there — there's danger!
43. Eda ngebuh, nenten becik. -> Don't show off — it's not good.
44. Eda gedeg, sabar! -> Don't be angry — be patient!
45. Durus. -> Please (do). [recycled from Lesson 3 — fresh commands]
46. Durus malinggih. -> Please sit.
47. Durus ngajeng. -> Please eat.
48. Durus malajah ring driki. -> Please study here.
49. Raris. -> Please go ahead / then (refined). [recycled — fresh commands]
50. Raris malinggih, nggih. -> Please sit, yes.
51. Raris nginem toya niki. -> Please drink this water.
52. Raris malajah Basa Bali. -> Please study Balinese.
53. Silakan. -> Please (neutral). [recycled — fresh commands]
54. Silakan madaar! -> Please eat!
55. Silakan nginem kopi! -> Please drink coffee!
56. Silakan ka pura, nggih. -> Please go to the temple, yes.
57. Tulung. -> Please help. [recycled — fresh commands]
58. Tulung icang ngaba nika! -> Help me carry that!
59. Tulung icang nyampat! -> Help me sweep!
60. Tulung, enggalang! -> Help — hurry!
61. Jalan. -> Let's go. [recycled from Lesson 3 — fresh commands]
62. Jalan ka pantai jani! -> Let's go to the beach now!
63. Jalan melali ka bukit! -> Let's go for an outing to the hill!
64. Jalan mulih, suba peteng. -> Let's go home — it's already night.
65. Ngiring. -> Let's go (polite). [recycled — fresh commands]
66. Ngiring ka pura! -> Let's go to the temple!
67. Ngiring mebakti jani. -> Let's pray now.
68. Ngiring malajah Basa Bali! -> Let's learn Balinese!
69. Enggalang. -> Hurry up! [recycled from Lesson 42 — fresh commands]
70. Enggalang, suba tengai! -> Hurry — it's already afternoon!
71. Enggalang mai, cai! -> Come here quickly!
72. Enggalang, bemo lakar kema! -> Hurry — the bemo is leaving!
73. Gelisang. -> Hurry up! (polite).
74. Gelisang, ida sampun ngantos. -> Hurry — he / she (honored) is already waiting.
75. Gelisang mrika, nggih. -> Please hurry there.
76. Gelisang memargi, sampun sanja. -> Hurry along — it's already evening.
77. Adeng-adeng. -> Slow down! [recycled from Lesson 25 — fresh commands]
78. Adeng-adeng majalan di rurung! -> Walk slowly in the alley!
79. Adeng-adeng, jalan nika rusak! -> Slow down — the road is damaged!
80. Adeng-adeng, nggih. -> Slowly, please.
81. Alon-alon. -> Slow down! (polite).
82. Alon-alon memargi. -> Walk slowly.
83. Alon-alon, sampun enggalang. -> Slowly — no need to hurry.
84. Alon-alon nginem toya panas. -> Drink the hot water slowly.
85. Oyong. -> Stay put! (familiar).
86. Oyong dini, icang lakar mai! -> Stay here — I'll be back!
87. Oyong, da kema! -> Stay — don't go there!
88. Oyong jumah, ujan bales! -> Stay home — it's raining hard!
89. Meneng. -> Keep quiet! (refined). [recycled from Lesson 44 — fresh commands]
90. Meneng, upacarane suci. -> Be quiet — the ceremony is sacred.
91. Meneng, ida mabaos. -> Quiet — he / she (honored) is speaking.
92. Meneng, nggih. -> Quiet, please.
93. Nengil. -> Keep quiet! (familiar). [recycled — fresh commands]
94. Nengil, guru lakar ngomong. -> Quiet — the teacher will talk.
95. Nengil, icang ningeh gatra. -> Quiet — I'm listening to the news.
96. Cai nengil, nggih! -> You keep quiet, okay!
97. Dingehang. -> Listen! (familiar). [recycled — fresh commands]
98. Dingehang kulkul nika! -> Listen to that kulkul!
99. Dingehang gatra melah niki! -> Listen to this good news!
100. Dingehang baos pandita! -> Listen to the priest's words!
101. Pirengang. -> Listen! (polite).
102. Pirengang baos ida. -> Listen to his / her (honored) words.
103. Pirengang gatra becik niki. -> Listen to this good news.
104. Pirengang, nggih. -> Listen, please.
105. Tolih. -> Look! (familiar).
106. Tolih kaja, gunung nika! -> Look north — that mountain!
107. Tolih ogoh-ogoh nika! -> Look at that ogoh-ogoh!
108. Tolih, ada bintang nyentak! -> Look — a bright star!
109. Cingak. -> Look! (polite).
110. Cingak tari nika! -> Look at that dance!
111. Cingak penjor nika! -> Look at that penjor!
112. Cingak, becik pisan! -> Look — very beautiful!
113. Baang. -> Give (it)! (familiar). [recycled from Lesson 7 — fresh commands]
114. Baang icang toya! -> Give me water!
115. Baang ia nasi, ia luwe. -> Give him rice — he's hungry.
116. Baang nika mai! -> Hand that over here!
117. Icen. -> Give (it)! (polite).
118. Icen tiang toya, nggih. -> Give me water, please.
119. Icen ida ajengan niki. -> Give him / her (honored) this food.
120. Icen tiang galah akidik. -> Give me a little time.
121. Suud. -> Stop / finish! (familiar). [recycled from Lesson 30 — fresh commands]
122. Suud ngomong, dingehang! -> Stop talking — listen!
123. Suud jani, suba peteng! -> Stop now — it's already night!
124. Suud, icang lakar pules. -> Stop — I'm going to sleep.
125. Puputang. -> Finish it! (polite).
126. Puputang pagawean nika! -> Finish that work!
127. Puputang ajengan ragane. -> Finish your food.
128. Puputang nika jani, nggih. -> Finish that now, please.

### Lesson 52: Storytelling

**Focus:** the story frame — "once upon a time", long ago, then, after that, over time, suddenly, shouts, endings — plus story characters (giants, gods, heroes) and winning/losing.
**Grammar note:** stories open with `Ada kone…` (familiar) or `Weten kocap…` (refined) = "once upon a time"; sequence runs `lantas`/`raris` (then) → `suba keto`/`sampun kenten` (after that) → `pamragat`/`pamuput` (the ending).
**New in this lesson (teaching order):** satua, carita, dongeng, kocap (recycled), ada kone, weten kocap, pidan (recycled — "in the past"), dumun, ilu, lantas (recycled), raris (recycled), suba keto, sampun kenten, pamragat, pamuput, kasuen-suen, makelo-kelo, kacarita, kaget, prajani, suryak, paling duri, puput (recycled), pragat (recycled), tokoh, raksasa, dewa, pahlawan, kesaktian, menang, kalah

1. Satua. -> Story (familiar).
2. Satua nika melah gati. -> That story is very good.
3. Cai uning satua Bali? -> Do you know Balinese stories?
4. Dadong icang uning satua liu. -> My grandmother knows many stories.
5. Carita. -> Story (refined).
6. Carita nika becik pisan. -> That story is very good.
7. Tiang seneng mireng carita. -> I love listening to stories.
8. Carita nika panjang gati. -> That story is very long.
9. Dongeng. -> Fairy tale.
10. Dongeng nika baang panak. -> That fairy tale is for children.
11. Panak icang demen dongeng. -> My child loves fairy tales.
12. Dongeng lan satua pateh? -> Are dongeng and satua the same?
13. Kocap. -> It is said. [recycled from Lesson 32 — narrative sense]
14. Kocap, pura nika kuna gati. -> It is said that temple is very ancient.
15. Kocap, danu nika suci. -> It is said that lake is sacred.
16. Kocap, ia pahlawan. -> It is said he was a hero.
17. Ada kone. -> Once upon a time (familiar phrase).
18. Ada kone raksasa di alas. -> Once upon a time there was a giant in the forest.
19. Ada kone dewa ring wukir. -> Once upon a time there was a god on the mountain.
20. Ada kone pahlawan kuat. -> Once upon a time there was a strong hero.
21. Weten kocap. -> Once upon a time (refined phrase).
22. Weten kocap, ada raksasa kaon. -> Once upon a time, there was an evil giant.
23. Weten kocap, dewa rauh ring desa. -> Once upon a time, a god came to the village.
24. Weten kocap, pahlawan nika menang. -> Once upon a time, that hero won.
25. Pidan. -> In the past / when. [recycled from Lesson 10 — "in the past" sense]
26. Pidan, ada raksasa di Bali. -> Long ago, there were giants in Bali.
27. Pidan nika, dewa malinggih ring wukir. -> In those old days, gods resided on the mountain.
28. Carita nika uli pidan. -> That story is from the past.
29. Dumun. -> In the past (refined).
30. Dumun, jagat niki sepi. -> Long ago, this world was quiet.
31. Dumun wénten raksasa ring alas. -> Long ago there were giants in the forest.
32. Satua dumun becik gati. -> Stories of old are very good.
33. Ilu. -> In the old days (familiar).
34. Ilu, desa nika sepi gati. -> In the old days, that village was very quiet.
35. Ada liu satua ilu. -> There were many stories in the old days.
36. Ilu, alas nika linggah. -> Long ago, that forest was wide.
37. Lantas. -> Then (neutral). [recycled from Lesson 48 — fresh sentences]
38. Raksasa rauh, lantas krama jejeh. -> The giant came — then the people were afraid.
39. Lantas ia malaib ka alas. -> Then he ran to the forest.
40. Dewa mireng, lantas rauh. -> The god heard — then came.
41. Raris. -> Then (refined). [recycled from Lesson 3 — fresh sentences]
42. Ida mireng, raris mrika. -> He / she (honored) heard, then went there.
43. Raris raksasa nika kalah. -> Then the giant was defeated.
44. Krama muspa, raris dewa ngicen pitulung. -> The people prayed — then the god gave help.
45. Suba keto. -> After that (familiar phrase).
46. Suba keto, raksasa nika ilang. -> After that, the giant disappeared.
47. Suba keto, desa rahayu. -> After that, the village was blessed.
48. Suba keto, icang pules. -> After that, I slept.
49. Sampun kenten. -> After that (refined phrase).
50. Sampun kenten, raksasa nika ical. -> After that, the giant vanished.
51. Sampun kenten, krama liang. -> After that, the people were happy.
52. Sampun kenten, jagat rahayu. -> After that, the world was blessed.
53. Pamragat. -> Ending (familiar).
54. Pamragat satua nika melah. -> The story's ending is good.
55. Cai demen pamragat nika? -> Do you like that ending?
56. Pamragat carita nika sedih? -> Is the story's ending sad?
57. Pamuput. -> Ending / finally (refined).
58. Pamuput carita nika becik. -> The story's ending is good.
59. Pamuput, raksasa kalah. -> Finally, the giant lost.
60. Pamuput nika melah, nggih. -> The ending is good, yes.
61. Kasuen-suen. -> After a long time (refined).
62. Kasuen-suen, pahlawan nika rauh. -> After a long time, the hero came.
63. Kasuen-suen, desa nika gede. -> Over time, the village grew big.
64. Kasuen-suen raksasa nika kalah. -> Eventually the giant was defeated.
65. Makelo-kelo. -> After a long time (familiar).
66. Makelo-kelo, ia dadi kuat. -> Over time, he became strong.
67. Makelo-kelo, satua nika puput. -> Eventually, the story ended.
68. Makelo-kelo raksasa nika ilang. -> Over time, the giant vanished.
69. Kacarita. -> The story goes (refined).
70. Kacarita, ada dewa ring wukir. -> The story goes: there was a god on the mountain.
71. Kacarita, pahlawan nika alit. -> As the story goes, the hero was small.
72. Kacarita, raksasa nika kaon gati. -> As the story goes, the giant was very evil.
73. Kaget. -> Suddenly / startled (neutral).
74. Kaget, ada kulkul! -> Suddenly — the kulkul!
75. Kaget raksasa rauh! -> Suddenly the giant came!
76. Icang kaget ningeh krebek. -> I was startled hearing thunder.
77. Prajani. -> Suddenly (familiar).
78. Prajani ujan bales! -> Suddenly it rained hard!
79. Prajani raksasa nika suryak! -> Suddenly the giant shouted!
80. Prajani, dewa rauh. -> Suddenly, the god came.
81. Suryak. -> Shout / cheer (familiar).
82. Krama suryak liang! -> The people cheered joyfully!
83. Suryak nika gede gati. -> The shout was very loud.
84. Raksasa suryak, icang jejeh. -> The giant shouted — I was scared.
85. Paling duri. -> The last one / lastly (phrase).
86. Paling duri, pahlawan nika rauh. -> Last of all, the hero came.
87. Paling duri, satua nika puput. -> Lastly, the story ended.
88. Paling duri, krama menang. -> In the end, the people won.
89. Puput. -> Finished. [recycled from Lesson 8 — fresh sentences]
90. Satua nika puput. -> The story is finished.
91. Puput caritane, icang pules. -> The story ended — I slept.
92. Carita nika durung puput. -> That story isn't finished yet.
93. Pragat. -> Finished (familiar). [recycled — fresh sentences]
94. Satua nika pragat. -> The story is done.
95. Pragat, suba keto krama mulih. -> Done — after that the people went home.
96. Dongeng nika pragat, panak icang pules. -> The fairy tale is done — my child sleeps.
97. Tokoh. -> Character.
98. Tokoh satua nika nyen? -> Who is the story's character?
99. Tokoh nika raksasa kaon. -> That character is an evil giant.
100. Tokoh nika dewa becik. -> That character is a good god.
101. Raksasa. -> Giant / monster.
102. Raksasa nika gede tur kaon. -> The giant is big and evil.
103. Raksasa nika kalah ring pahlawan. -> The giant lost to the hero.
104. Ada raksasa ring satua Bali. -> There are giants in Balinese stories.
105. Dewa. -> God / deity.
106. Dewa nika becik gati. -> The god is very good.
107. Dewa ngicen pitulung ring krama. -> The god gives help to the people.
108. Krama muspa ring dewa. -> The people pray to the god.
109. Pahlawan. -> Hero.
110. Pahlawan nika kuat gati. -> The hero is very strong.
111. Pahlawan nika menang ring raksasa. -> The hero wins over the giant.
112. Cai dot dadi pahlawan? -> Do you want to be a hero?
113. Kesaktian. -> Magical power.
114. Kesaktian raksasa nika gede. -> The giant's magical power is great.
115. Pahlawan nika uning kesaktian. -> The hero knows magic.
116. Kesaktian nika wénten ring satua. -> Magical powers exist in stories.
117. Menang. -> To win.
118. Pahlawan nika menang! -> The hero wins!
119. Sira menang ring satua nika? -> Who wins in that story?
120. Krama menang, raksasa kalah. -> The people win, the giant loses.
121. Kalah. -> To lose.
122. Raksasa nika kalah. -> The giant loses.
123. Kalah nenten luung, nanging biasa. -> Losing isn't nice, but it happens.
124. Sira kalah ring satua nika? -> Who loses in that story?

### Lesson 53: Arts & Entertainment

**Focus:** the Balinese arts world — gamelan instruments, wayang and its puppeteer, dance and dancers, sacred songs and poems, painting and carving, stages, shows, and the magical `taksu`.
**Cultural note:** `taksu` is the spiritual charisma that makes a performance alive — it cannot be bought, only cultivated. A `sekaa` is the arts troupe/club of the banjar; a `pragina` is a (refined word for a) dancer.
**New in this lesson (teaching order):** seni, gambelan, gong, wayang, dalang, igel (recycled), tari (recycled), topeng, kidung, kekawin, lukisan, ukiran, suling, kendang, cengceng, rindik, panggung, balih-balihan, nonton, mabalih (recycled), miarsayang (recycled), megending (recycled), menabuh, seniman (recycled), pragina, sekaa, latihan, melajah igel, taksu (recycled from Lesson 53 list itself), kalang, sorak

1. Seni. -> Art.
2. Seni Bali becik pisan. -> Balinese art is very beautiful.
3. Cai demen seni apa? -> What art do you like?
4. Tiang malajah seni Bali. -> I study Balinese art.
5. Gambelan. -> Traditional orchestra (gamelan).
6. Ada gambelan di banjar. -> There is a gamelan at the banjar.
7. Truna truni megambel nganggo gambelan banjar. -> The youth play the banjar's gamelan.
8. Gambelan nika kuna. -> That gamelan is old.
9. Gong. -> Gong / gamelan orchestra.
10. Gong nika gede gati. -> The gong is very big.
11. Icang ningeh gong di banjar. -> I hear the gong at the banjar.
12. Gong lan gambelan pateh? -> Are gong and gambelan the same?
13. Wayang. -> Shadow puppet (play).
14. Ada wayang ring bale. -> There are wayang puppets at the pavilion.
15. Icang mabalih wayang! -> I watch the wayang!
16. Wayang nika satua apa? -> What story is that wayang?
17. Dalang. -> Puppeteer.
18. Dalang nika nyen? -> Who is that puppeteer?
19. Dalang nika uning satua liu. -> The puppeteer knows many stories.
20. Dalang nika becik gati. -> The puppeteer is very good.
21. Igel. -> Dance (familiar). [recycled from Lesson 47 — fresh sentences]
22. Igel nika jegeg gati. -> The dance is so beautiful.
23. Nyai lakar melajah igel? -> Will you learn dance?
24. Ia ngigel, icang mabalih. -> He dances, I watch.
25. Tari. -> Dance (refined). [recycled — fresh sentences]
26. Tari nika becik pisan. -> The dance is very beautiful.
27. Tiang miarsayang tari Bali. -> I watch Balinese dance.
28. Tari lan igel pateh? -> Are tari and igel the same?
29. Topeng. -> Mask.
30. Topeng nika barak lan ireng. -> The mask is red and black.
31. Ada topeng ring bale. -> There is a mask at the pavilion.
32. Cai mabalih tari topeng? -> Do you watch mask dance?
33. Kidung. -> Sacred song.
34. Dadong megending kidung. -> Grandmother sings kidung.
35. Kidung nika alus gati. -> The kidung is very refined.
36. Cai taen mireng kidung? -> Have you ever heard kidung?
37. Kekawin. -> Classical poem.
38. Kekawin nika basa Jawa kuna? -> Is that kekawin in Old Javanese?
39. Pandita mabasan kekawin. -> The priest chants kekawin.
40. Kekawin nika panjang. -> The kekawin is long.
41. Lukisan. -> Painting.
42. Lukisan nika melah gati. -> The painting is so beautiful.
43. Ada lukisan gunung ring kamar icang. -> There is a mountain painting in my room.
44. Seniman nika ngae lukisan. -> The artist makes paintings.
45. Ukiran. -> Carving.
46. Ukiran Bali becik pisan. -> Balinese carving is very beautiful.
47. Ukiran nika ring pintu jero. -> The carving is on the house door.
48. Undagi ngae ukiran alus. -> The master craftsman makes fine carvings.
49. Suling. -> Bamboo flute.
50. Icang ningeh suling. -> I hear the flute.
51. Cai bisa nganggen suling? -> Can you play the flute?
52. Suling nika tiing. -> The flute is bamboo.
53. Kendang. -> Drum.
54. Kendang nika gede. -> The drum is big.
55. Ada kendang ring gambelan. -> There is a drum in the gamelan.
56. Kendang nika kulit sampi. -> The drum is cowhide.
57. Cengceng. -> Cymbals.
58. Cengceng nika cenik. -> The cymbals are small.
59. Cengceng ada ring gambelan. -> Cymbals are in the gamelan.
60. Cai ningeh cengceng? -> Do you hear the cymbals?
61. Rindik. -> Bamboo xylophone.
62. Rindik nika tiing. -> The rindik is bamboo.
63. Icang ningeh rindik di warung. -> I hear rindik at the stall.
64. Rindik nika melah. -> The rindik is lovely.
65. Panggung. -> Stage.
66. Tari nika ring panggung. -> The dance is on the stage.
67. Panggung nika gede. -> The stage is big.
68. Ada panggung di banjar jani. -> There is a stage at the banjar now.
69. Balih-balihan. -> Entertainment / show.
70. Balih-balihan nika pidan? -> When is the show?
71. Balih-balihan ring pura rame. -> The show at the temple is crowded.
72. Cai dot mabalih balih-balihan? -> Do you want to watch the show?
73. Nonton. -> To watch (neutral).
74. Icang nonton wayang. -> I watch the wayang.
75. Cai lakar nonton apa? -> What will you watch?
76. Nonton balih-balihan ring banjar. -> Watch the show at the banjar.
77. Mabalih. -> To watch (a performance). [recycled from Lesson 37 — fresh sentences]
78. Icang mabalih wayang di bale. -> I watch wayang at the pavilion.
79. Krama mabalih balih-balihan. -> The people watch the show.
80. Cai mabalih igel utawi wayang? -> Do you watch dance or wayang?
81. Miarsayang. -> To watch / listen (refined). [recycled — fresh sentences]
82. Tiang miarsayang tari ring pura. -> I watch dance at the temple.
83. Ida miarsayang gambelan. -> He / she (honored) enjoys the gamelan.
84. Ipun miarsayang balih-balihan. -> He / she watches the show.
85. Megending. -> To sing. [recycled — fresh sentences]
86. Nyai megending kidung? -> Do you sing kidung?
87. Ia megending ring panggung. -> He sings on the stage.
88. Megending nika dangan? -> Is singing easy?
89. Menabuh. -> To play (gamelan instruments).
90. Truna nika menabuh gong. -> The young man plays the gong.
91. Cai bisa menabuh kendang? -> Can you play the drum?
92. Menabuh gambelan keweh dumun. -> Playing gamelan is hard at first.
93. Seniman. -> Artist. [recycled from Lesson 33 — fresh sentences]
94. Seniman nika ngae ukiran. -> The artist makes carvings.
95. Seniman Bali liu ring desa nika. -> There are many Balinese artists in that village.
96. Seniman nika nggambar lan ngukir. -> The artist draws and carves.
97. Pragina. -> Dancer (refined).
98. Pragina nika jegeg gati. -> The dancer is so beautiful.
99. Pragina nari ring panggung. -> The dancer dances on the stage.
100. Ipun dadi pragina. -> She became a dancer.
101. Sekaa. -> (Arts) group / troupe.
102. Ada sekaa gong di banjar. -> There is a gong group at the banjar.
103. Sekaa tari nika melah. -> The dance troupe is good.
104. Sekaa icang megambel ring odalan. -> My group plays gamelan at the odalan.
105. Latihan. -> Practice / rehearsal.
106. Sekaa gong latihan jani. -> The gong group practices now.
107. Latihan nika dija? -> Where is the practice?
108. Cai lakar latihan pidan? -> When will you practice?
109. Melajah igel. -> To learn dance (phrase).
110. Nyai melajah igel? -> Do you learn dance?
111. Icang lakar melajah igel. -> I will learn dance.
112. Melajah igel keweh, nanging liang. -> Learning dance is hard, but fun.
113. Taksu. -> Spiritual charisma (in art).
114. Taksu pragina nika gede. -> The dancer's taksu is great.
115. Taksu nika saking dija? -> Where does taksu come from?
116. Seniman nika taksu. -> That artist has taksu.
117. Kalang. -> Arena / stage ground.
118. Kalang nika linggah. -> The arena is wide.
119. Ada wayang ring kalang. -> There is a wayang (show) in the arena.
120. Kalang nika di banjar. -> The arena is at the banjar.
121. Sorak. -> Applause / cheer.
122. Sorak krama gede gati! -> The people's applause is so loud!
123. Sorak sesampun tari puput. -> Applause after the dance ends.
124. Krama sorak liang. -> The people cheer joyfully.

### Lesson 54: Abstract Nouns

**Focus:** the great abstractions — love, happiness, sadness, peace, fear, mind, life and death, fate and karma, energy, goodness, dharma, purity, character, sin, and merit.
**Grammar note:** abstract nouns behave like regular nouns: `Bagia nika napi?` "What is happiness?" Possession and predication work as always: `Manah ida damai.` "His mind (is) peaceful."
**New in this lesson (teaching order):** tresna (recycled), sayang (recycled), bagia, liang (recycled), sebet (recycled), sedih (recycled), damai, shanti, jejeh (recycled), ajrih (recycled), kendel (recycled), keneh (recycled), manah (recycled), urip, idup, mati, padem, nasib, karma, phala (recycled), taksu (recycled), tenaga, bayu, kebaikan, dharma, adharma, suci (recycled), cemer (recycled), budi, dosa, pahala
*(Note: `mati` (familiar) vs `padem` (refined) = death; for respected persons, higher forms exist later in the course. `phala` and `pahala` both mean the fruit/merit of deeds.)*

1. Tresna. -> Love (refined noun). [recycled from Lesson 43 — fresh sentences]
2. Tresna nika rasa paling kuat. -> Love is the strongest feeling.
3. Tresna ida ring rabin ida agung. -> His love for his wife is great.
4. Tresna lan benci nenten pateh. -> Love and hate are not the same.
5. Sayang. -> Love / affection. [recycled — fresh sentences]
6. Sayang meme ring panakne gede gati. -> Mother's love for her child is very great.
7. Sayang nika melah. -> Affection is good.
8. Sayang icang ring cai nenten kirang. -> My affection for you does not lessen.
9. Bagia. -> Happiness.
10. Bagia nika napi? -> What is happiness?
11. Tiang ngrasa bagia. -> I feel happiness.
12. Bagia nika lebih penting teken jinah. -> Happiness is more important than money.
13. Liang. -> Joy / happiness. [recycled from Lesson 16 — fresh sentences]
14. Liang nika bagia. -> Joy is happiness.
15. Keneh liang nika melah baang awak. -> A joyful heart is good for the body.
16. Cai liang, icang liang. -> You're happy, I'm happy.
17. Sebet. -> Sadness. [recycled — fresh sentences]
18. Sebet nika nenten suwe, pasti ilang. -> Sadness doesn't last — it surely fades.
19. Sebet lan liang nenten pateh. -> Sadness and joy are not the same.
20. Yen cai sebet, icang nulungin. -> If you are sad, I help.
21. Sedih. -> Sadness (neutral). [recycled — fresh sentences]
22. Sedih nika rasa biasa. -> Sadness is a normal feeling.
23. Ia sedih krana cicingne ilang. -> He is sad because his dog is lost.
24. Sedih lan sebet pateh? -> Are sedih and sebet the same?
25. Damai. -> Peace.
26. Damai nika melah gati. -> Peace is very good.
27. Desa nika damai. -> The village is peaceful.
28. Tiang dot damai ring jagat. -> I want peace in the world.
29. Shanti. -> (Spiritual) peace.
30. Shanti nika damai ring manah. -> Shanti is peace in the heart.
31. Muspa ngrereh shanti. -> Praying to seek peace.
32. Shanti ring jagat. -> Peace in the world.
33. Jejeh. -> Fear. [recycled from Lesson 16 — fresh sentences]
34. Jejeh nika nenten luung. -> Fear is not good.
35. Icang jejeh ring peteng. -> I am afraid of the dark.
36. Yen ada jejeh, muspa. -> If there is fear, pray.
37. Ajrih. -> Fear / awe (refined). [recycled — fresh sentences]
38. Ajrih tiang ring sasuhunan. -> I stand in awe of the revered deity.
39. Ajrih lan jejeh pateh? -> Are ajrih and jejeh the same?
40. Tiang ajrih ring bhatara. -> I stand in awe before the deity.
41. Kendel. -> Gladness / bravery. [recycled — fresh sentences]
42. Kendel tiang jani. -> I feel glad/brave now.
43. Kendel nenten jejeh. -> Brave is not afraid.
44. Pahlawan nika kendel gati. -> The hero is very brave.
45. Keneh. -> Mind / heart (familiar). [recycled from Lesson 44 — fresh sentences]
46. Keneh icang damai jani. -> My heart is peaceful now.
47. Keneh lan manah pateh? -> Are keneh and manah the same?
48. Keneh becik nika penting. -> A good heart is important.
49. Manah. -> Mind / heart (refined). [recycled from Lesson 34 — fresh sentences]
50. Manah ida damai gati. -> His / her (honored) heart is very peaceful.
51. Manah becik, baos becik. -> A good heart (makes) good speech.
52. Manah lan keneh nenten pateh? -> Are manah and keneh not the same?
53. Urip. -> Life (refined).
54. Urip nika suci. -> Life is sacred.
55. Urip icang liang. -> My life is joyful.
56. Dumogi urip ragane rahayu. -> May your life be blessed.
57. Idup. -> Life (familiar).
58. Idup icang liang. -> My life is happy.
59. Idup nika melah. -> Life is good.
60. Idup lan urip pateh? -> Are idup and urip the same?
61. Mati. -> Death (familiar).
62. Mati nika pasti. -> Death is certain.
63. Cicing icang suba mati. -> My dog has died.
64. Mati lan urip nenten pateh. -> Death and life are not the same.
65. Padem. -> Death / to pass away (refined).
66. Ida sampun padem. -> He / she (honored) has passed away.
67. Padem lan mati pateh? -> Are padem and mati the same?
68. Sesampun padem, wénten ngaben. -> After death, there is the cremation.
69. Nasib. -> Fate.
70. Nasib icang becik. -> My fate is good.
71. Nasib nika saking dija? -> Where does fate come from?
72. Nasib icang nenten kaon. -> My fate is not bad.
73. Karma. -> Karma / deeds.
74. Karma becik, phala becik. -> Good deeds (bring) good fruit.
75. Tiang uning indik karma. -> I know about karma.
76. Karma kaon ngae phala kaon. -> Bad deeds make bad results.
77. Phala. -> Fruit (of deeds). [recycled from Lesson 48 — fresh sentences]
78. Phala karma becik nika liang. -> The fruit of good deeds is joy.
79. Phala nika pidan rauh? -> When does the fruit come?
80. Phala pakaryan ipun becik. -> The fruit of his / her work is good.
81. Taksu. -> Spiritual charisma. [recycled from Lesson 53 — fresh sentences]
82. Taksu nika saking manah suci. -> Taksu comes from a pure heart.
83. Taksu nika nenten dadi meli. -> Taksu cannot be bought.
84. Taksu ida gede gati. -> His / her (honored) taksu is very great.
85. Tenaga. -> Energy / strength.
86. Tenaga icang kirang jani. -> My energy is low now.
87. Tenaga nika saking ajengan. -> Energy comes from food.
88. Madaar baang tenaga. -> Eating is for energy.
89. Bayu. -> Energy / wind (refined).
90. Bayu nika tenaga ring angga. -> Bayu is the energy in the body.
91. Bayu icang kirang, icang lesu. -> My energy is low — I'm tired.
92. Bayu lan tenaga pateh? -> Are bayu and tenaga the same?
93. Kebaikan. -> Goodness (neutral).
94. Kebaikan nika penting. -> Goodness is important.
95. Kebaikan ida gede. -> His / her (honored) goodness is great.
96. Kebaikan ngae damai. -> Goodness makes peace.
97. Dharma. -> Truth / duty / righteousness.
98. Dharma nika patut. -> Dharma is right.
99. Galungan nika dharma menang. -> Galungan is (when) dharma wins.
100. Tiang malajah dharma. -> I learn dharma.
101. Adharma. -> Untruth / evil.
102. Adharma nika kaon. -> Adharma is evil.
103. Dharma lan adharma nenten pateh. -> Dharma and adharma are not the same.
104. Adharma kalah, dharma menang. -> Evil loses, dharma wins.
105. Suci. -> Purity / holy. [recycled from Lesson 35 — fresh sentences]
106. Manah suci nika melah. -> A pure heart is good.
107. Toya tirta suci. -> The holy water is sacred.
108. Suci lan cemer nenten pateh. -> Pure and impure are not the same.
109. Cemer. -> Impurity. [recycled from Lesson 19 — fresh sentences]
110. Cemer nika nenten suci. -> Impurity is not purity.
111. Ring genah cemer, nenten dados mebakti. -> In an impure place, one may not pray.
112. Angga cemer, manjus mangda suci. -> (When) the body is impure, bathe to be pure.
113. Budi. -> Character / mind.
114. Budi ida becik gati. -> His / her (honored) character is very good.
115. Budi lan manah pateh? -> Are budi and manah the same?
116. Budi becik nika penting gati. -> Good character is very important.
117. Dosa. -> Sin.
118. Dosa nika kaon. -> Sin is bad.
119. Cai ngae dosa? -> Do you commit sin?
120. Dosa lan dharma nenten pateh. -> Sin and dharma are not the same.
121. Pahala. -> Reward / merit.
122. Pahala pakaryan becik nika liu. -> The merit of good work is much.
123. Pahala lan phala pateh? -> Are pahala and phala the same?
124. Dosa ngae pahala kirang. -> Sin makes merit less.

### Lesson 55: Indirect Speech

**Focus:** reported speech and hearsay — "they say", "I heard", "it was said", gossip and slander, truth and lies.
**Grammar note:** hearsay particles: `kone`/`jare` (familiar), `kocap` (neutral/literary) attach to the reported clause: `Kone ia suba mulih.` "They say he's home." Passive report: `orahanga`/`nikayanga` = "it was said". `Ida ngandika` = the honored one speaks (high-to-low).
**New in this lesson (teaching order):** kone, orahanga, nikayanga, dingeh tiang, pireng titiang, ia ngorahang, ipun nikayang, ida ngandika, kene kone, kocap (recycled), unduk (recycled), indik (recycled), ada gatra, wénten orti, gatra (recycled), orti, jare, crita, sambat, baosang, nyambat (recycled), maosang (recycled), nengil (recycled), rahasia (recycled), pingit, pisuna, ngorta (recycled), mitnah, leres, saja (recycled), bogbog

1. Kone. -> They say / he said (familiar).
2. Kone ia suba mulih. -> They say he's already home.
3. Kone satua nika seken. -> They say the story is true.
4. Ia lakar rauh kone. -> He will come, they say.
5. Orahanga. -> It was said / he was told (familiar).
6. Orahanga, ia lakar teka. -> It was said he would come.
7. Orahanga, odalan benjang. -> It was said the odalan is tomorrow.
8. Orahanga nika seken? -> Was that said to be true?
9. Nikayanga. -> It was said (refined).
10. Nikayanga, ida lakar rauh. -> It was said he / she (honored) would come.
11. Nikayanga, upacara benjang. -> It was said the ceremony is tomorrow.
12. Nikayanga ipun sampun mantuk. -> It was said he / she had already returned.
13. Dingeh tiang. -> I heard (familiar phrase).
14. Dingeh tiang, ia suba makurenan. -> I heard he is married already.
15. Dingeh tiang gatra nika dibi. -> I heard that news yesterday.
16. Dingeh tiang, odalan benjang rame. -> I heard tomorrow's odalan is festive.
17. Pireng titiang. -> I heard (refined phrase).
18. Pireng titiang, ida sampun rauh. -> I heard he / she (honored) has arrived.
19. Pireng titiang gatra becik nika. -> I heard that good news.
20. Pireng titiang, piodalan agung benjang. -> I heard the great festival is tomorrow.
21. Ia ngorahang. -> He says/said (familiar phrase).
22. Ia ngorahang ia lakar melali. -> He says he will travel.
23. Ia ngorahang cai jemet. -> He says you are diligent.
24. Ia ngorahang nika seken. -> He says that is true.
25. Ipun nikayang. -> He/she says/said (refined phrase).
26. Ipun nikayang ipun lakar mrika. -> He / she says he will go there.
27. Ipun nikayang nika patut. -> He / she says that is right.
28. Ipun nikayang ida lakar rauh. -> He / she says he / she (honored) will come.
29. Ida ngandika. -> He/she (honored) speaks (phrase).
30. Ida ngandika napi? -> What does he / she (honored) say?
31. Ida ngandika, krama mangda rauh. -> He / she (honored) says the people should come.
32. Ida ngandika alus gati. -> He / she (honored) speaks very gently.
33. Kene kone. -> It's like this, they say (phrase).
34. Kene kone satua nika. -> The story goes like this, they say.
35. Kene kone, ada kone raksasa. -> Like this, they say: once there was a giant.
36. Kene kone unduk nika. -> That's how the matter is, they say.
37. Kocap. -> It is said. [recycled from Lesson 32 — fresh sentences]
38. Kocap, ia jemet gati. -> It is said he is very diligent.
39. Kocap nika seken? -> Is it said to be true?
40. Kocap, odalan benjang rame. -> It is said tomorrow's odalan is festive.
41. Unduk. -> About / matter (familiar). [recycled from Lesson 40 — fresh sentences]
42. Unduk apa kone nika? -> What is that about, they say?
43. Icang ningeh unduk cai. -> I heard about you.
44. Unduk nika rahasia. -> That matter is a secret.
45. Indik. -> About / regarding (refined). [recycled — fresh sentences]
46. Indik napi nika, nggih? -> About what is that, please?
47. Tiang mireng indik ida. -> I heard about him / her (honored).
48. Indik odalan, krama sampun uning. -> About the odalan, the people already know.
49. Ada gatra. -> There is news (familiar phrase).
50. Ada gatra melah jani! -> There's good news now!
51. Ada gatra unduk cai? -> Is there news about you?
52. Ada gatra, icang ningeh. -> There's news — I heard (it).
53. Wénten orti. -> There is news (refined phrase).
54. Wénten orti becik. -> There is good news.
55. Wénten orti indik piodalan? -> Is there news about the festival?
56. Wénten orti napi jani? -> What news is there now?
57. Gatra. -> News. [recycled from Lesson 2 — fresh sentences]
58. Gatra nika seken? -> Is that news true?
59. Gatra napi jani? -> What's the news now?
60. Icang ningeh gatra kaon. -> I heard bad news.
61. Orti. -> News (refined).
62. Orti nika becik. -> The news is good.
63. Orti napi wénten? -> What news is there?
64. Tiang mireng orti nika iwau. -> I heard that news earlier.
65. Jare. -> They say (familiar colloquial).
66. Jare ia suba mulih. -> They say he's already home.
67. Jare odalan benjang rame gati. -> They say tomorrow's odalan is very festive.
68. Jare cai lakar melali? -> They say you will travel?
69. Crita. -> It is said / the story goes (neutral).
70. Crita nika seken? -> Is that story true?
71. Critane, ia pahlawan. -> The story goes: he's a hero.
72. Crita nika kocap dumun. -> That story is told from the past.
73. Sambat. -> Mentioned (familiar).
74. Adan icang sambat? -> Was my name mentioned?
75. Sambat napi nika? -> What was mentioned?
76. Nenten sambat adan cai. -> Your name wasn't mentioned.
77. Baosang. -> Mentioned (refined).
78. Wastan ida baosang ring pura. -> His / her (honored) name was mentioned at the temple.
79. Baosang napi dibi? -> What was said yesterday?
80. Nenten baosang unduk nika. -> That matter wasn't mentioned.
81. Nyambat. -> To mention / say. [recycled from Lesson 24 — fresh sentences]
82. Ia nyambat adan cai. -> He mentions your name.
83. Nyen nyambat unduk nika? -> Who mentioned that matter?
84. Tiang nenten nyambat napi. -> I didn't mention anything.
85. Maosang. -> To say / tell (refined). [recycled — fresh sentences]
86. Ipun maosang orti becik. -> He / she tells good news.
87. Ida maosang napi ring tiang? -> What does he / she (honored) say to me?
88. Ipun maosang nika patut. -> He / she says that is right.
89. Nengil. -> To keep quiet (about). [recycled from Lesson 44 — fresh sentences]
90. Icang nengil unduk nika. -> I keep quiet about that matter.
91. Cai nengil, nanging cai uning. -> You keep quiet, but you know.
92. Nengil indik rahasia nika. -> Keep quiet about that secret.
93. Rahasia. -> Secret. [recycled — fresh sentences]
94. Rahasia nika nyen uning? -> Who knows that secret?
95. Rahasia icang lan cai. -> A secret between me and you.
96. Rahasia nika nenten dados baosang. -> That secret must not be told.
97. Pingit. -> Secret / sacred-hidden (refined).
98. Genah nika pingit. -> That place is sacred and hidden.
99. Unduk pingit nika napi? -> What is that hidden matter?
100. Satua pingit wénten ring desa. -> Hidden stories exist in the village.
101. Pisuna. -> Slander / gossip (familiar).
102. Pisuna nika kaon gati. -> Slander is very bad.
103. Da ngae pisuna! -> Don't make slander!
104. Pisuna ngae nyama gedeg. -> Slander makes siblings angry.
105. Ngorta. -> To chat / gossip. [recycled from Lesson 44 — fresh sentences]
106. Krama ngorta indik odalan. -> The people chat about the odalan.
107. Cai ngorta pisuna? -> Do you gossip slander?
108. Ngorta nenten luung yen pisuna. -> Chatting isn't good if it's slander.
109. Mitnah. -> To slander.
110. Da mitnah timpal! -> Don't slander a friend!
111. Ia mitnah icang? -> Did he slander me?
112. Mitnah nika dosa. -> Slander is a sin.
113. Leres. -> True (fact) (refined).
114. Nika leres. -> That is true.
115. Leres napi gatra nika? -> Is that news true?
116. Nggih, leres. -> Yes, that's true.
117. Saja. -> True (fact) (familiar). [recycled from Lesson 1 — fresh sentences]
118. Saja nika? -> Is that true?
119. Nggih, saja, icang nawang. -> Yes, truly — I know.
120. Saja utawi bogbog? -> True or a lie?
121. Bogbog. -> Lie.
122. Nika bogbog! -> That's a lie!
123. Da ngomong bogbog! -> Don't tell lies!
124. Bogbog nika dosa. -> Lying is a sin.

### Lesson 56: Conditionals

**Focus:** the full "if" system, examples, possibility, certainty, "as long as", "only/just", waiting — and the standalone refined future marker `jagi`.
**Grammar note:** `yening`/`yen` (if) + condition, then result: `Yen cai luwe, madaar!` "If so" = `yening kenten` (refined) / `yening keto` / `yen keto` (familiar). "Only" = `dogen` (familiar) / `kemanten` (refined) — the intensifiers `tuah`/`wantah` come in Lesson 60. `Be` is the little particle marking an accomplished condition: `Cai be madaar?` "Have you eaten already?"
**New in this lesson (teaching order):** yening (recycled), yen (recycled), yadiastun (recycled), yadiapin (recycled), umpami, upami, bisa-bisa, minab (recycled), mirib (recycled), yening keto, yening kenten, yen keto, sinah, pasti (recycled), janten (recycled), konden tawang, durung uning, asal, kewanten (recycled — "only/but"), dogen, kemanten, kel (recycled), jagi (recycled — standalone unlocked), lakar (recycled), apang (recycled), mangda (recycled), kanti (recycled), antos, yening ten, yen sing, be

1. Yening. -> If (refined). [recycled from Lesson 30 — fresh sentences]
2. Yening ida rauh, tiang liang. -> If he / she (honored) comes, I am happy.
3. Yening sabeh, sampunang mrika. -> If it rains, don't go there.
4. Yening ragane uning, baosang ring tiang. -> If you know, tell me.
5. Yen. -> If (familiar). [recycled — fresh sentences]
6. Yen cai luwe, madaar jani! -> If you're hungry, eat now!
7. Yen icang sugih, icang lakar meli jero. -> If I were rich, I would buy a house.
8. Yen seken, icang liang! -> If it's true, I'm happy!
9. Yadiastun. -> Even if (refined). [recycled — fresh sentences]
10. Yadiastun suwe, tiang lakar ngantos. -> Even if it takes long, I will wait.
11. Yadiastun ida lingsir, ida mekarya. -> Even though elderly, he / she works.
12. Yadiastun keweh, mangda puput. -> Even if hard, may it be finished.
13. Yadiapin. -> Even if (familiar). [recycled — fresh sentences]
14. Yadiapin icang kenyel, icang lakar megae. -> Even if tired, I will work.
15. Yadiapin joh, icang lakar kema. -> Even if far, I will go there.
16. Yadiapin tiwas, keneh icang bagia. -> Even if poor, my heart is happy.
17. Umpami. -> For example (refined).
18. Umpami, seni Bali: tari, igel, lan gambelan. -> For example, Balinese arts: dance, dance, and gamelan.
19. Umpami cai lakar kema, lakar ngaba apa? -> For example if you go there, what will you bring?
20. Umpami, odalan benjang rame. -> For example, the odalan tomorrow is festive.
21. Upami. -> For example (familiar).
22. Upami, icang lakar melali ka pantai. -> For example, I would travel to the beach.
23. Upami cai sugih, cai lakar ngae apa? -> For example if you were rich, what would you make?
24. Upami nika seken? -> For example — is that true?
25. Bisa-bisa. -> It might happen (phrase).
26. Bisa-bisa ujan mani. -> It might rain tomorrow.
27. Bisa-bisa ia lakar rauh. -> He might come.
28. Bisa-bisa blabar yen ujan bales. -> There might be a flood if it rains heavily.
29. Minab. -> Maybe (refined). [recycled from Lesson 32 — fresh sentences]
30. Minab ida sampun mantuk. -> Maybe he / she (honored) has already returned.
31. Minab nika bogbog. -> Maybe that is a lie.
32. Minab ujan, minab nenten. -> Maybe rain, maybe not.
33. Mirib. -> Seems like / maybe (familiar). [recycled — fresh sentences]
34. Mirib ia suba mulih. -> He seems to have gone home.
35. Mirib ujan benjang. -> Looks like rain tomorrow.
36. Mirib nika leres. -> That seems true.
37. Yening keto. -> If so (refined-familiar phrase).
38. Yening keto, icang lakar kema. -> If so, I'll go there.
39. Yening keto, nggih, jalan! -> If so — okay, let's go!
40. Yening keto, cai suba uning. -> If so, you already know.
41. Yening kenten. -> If so (refined phrase).
42. Yening kenten, tiang lakar mrika. -> If so, I will go there.
43. Yening kenten, nggih, ngiring! -> If so — okay, let's go!
44. Yening kenten, sampunang sebet. -> If so, don't be sad.
45. Yen keto. -> If so (familiar phrase).
46. Yen keto, icang setuju. -> If so, I agree.
47. Yen keto, jalan jani! -> If so, let's go now!
48. Yen keto, nggih! -> If so, okay!
49. Sinah. -> Surely / clearly (familiar).
50. Sinah ia lakar teka. -> Surely he will come.
51. Sinah ujan mani, langit gulem. -> Clearly it will rain tomorrow — the sky is cloudy.
52. Sinah cai uning! -> Surely you know!
53. Pasti. -> Definitely. [recycled from Lesson 32 — fresh sentences]
54. Pasti ia rauh, dong! -> He'll definitely come, of course!
55. Nika pasti leres. -> That is definitely true.
56. Pasti icang lakar mulih mani. -> I will definitely go home tomorrow.
57. Janten. -> Certainly. [recycled — fresh sentences]
58. Ida janten rauh, nggih. -> He / she (honored) will certainly come, yes.
59. Janten nika leres. -> That is certainly true.
60. Janten tiang lakar mrika. -> I will certainly go there.
61. Konden tawang. -> Not yet known (phrase).
62. Konden tawang sira lakar rauh. -> Not yet known who will come.
63. Unduk nika konden tawang. -> That matter is not yet known.
64. Konden tawang, icang ngantos. -> Not yet known — I wait.
65. Durung uning. -> Don't know yet (phrase).
66. Tiang durung uning unduk nika. -> I don't know about that matter yet.
67. Ipun durung uning sira rauh. -> He / she doesn't know yet who is coming.
68. Durung uning, minab benjang. -> Not known yet — maybe tomorrow.
69. Asal. -> As long as (neutral).
70. Asal cai jemet, cai bisa. -> As long as you're diligent, you can.
71. Asal nenten ujan, icang lakar mrika. -> As long as it doesn't rain, I'll go there.
72. Asal damai, icang liang. -> As long as there's peace, I'm happy.
73. Kewanten. -> But / only however (refined). [recycled from Lesson 30 — fresh sentences]
74. Tiang lakar rauh, kewanten benjang. -> I will come, but only tomorrow.
75. Tiang numbas, kewanten jinah tiang bedik. -> I would buy, but my money is little.
76. Ipun lakar mrika, kewanten nyanan. -> He / she will go there, but later.
77. Dogen. -> Only / just (familiar).
78. Icang dogen nongos dini. -> Only I live here.
79. Nika dogen cai uning? -> Is that all you know?
80. A bedik dogen, suksma. -> Just a little, thanks.
81. Kemanten. -> Only / just (refined).
82. Tiang kemanten rauh. -> I alone came.
83. Nika kemanten, nggih. -> Just that, yes.
84. Akidik kemanten, suksma. -> Just a little, thank you.
85. Kel. -> Will (short form). [recycled from Lesson 32 — fresh sentences]
86. Icang kel mrika jani. -> I'll go there now.
87. Cai kel ngudiang mani? -> What will you do tomorrow?
88. Kel ia teka, icang ngantos. -> He'll come — I'm waiting.
89. Jagi. -> Will (refined). [standalone sense now unlocked]
90. Ida jagi rauh benjang. -> He / she (honored) will come tomorrow.
91. Tiang jagi mrika. -> I will go there.
92. Ipun jagi makarya napi? -> What will he / she work on?
93. Lakar. -> Will (familiar). [recycled — fresh sentences]
94. Icang lakar melali mani. -> I'll travel tomorrow.
95. Cai lakar dadi apa? -> What will you become?
96. Lakar ujan? Langite gulem. -> Will it rain? The sky is cloudy.
97. Apang. -> So that (familiar). [recycled from Lesson 30 — fresh sentences]
98. Icang jemet malajah apang bisa Basa Bali. -> I study hard so I can speak Balinese.
99. Madaar jani apang ten luwe nyanan. -> Eat now so you won't be hungry later.
100. Ngoyong jumah apang ten bakat ujan. -> Stay home so you don't get caught in the rain.
101. Mangda. -> So that (refined). [recycled — fresh sentences]
102. Tiang malajah mangda uning liu. -> I study so that I know much.
103. Muspa mangda manah damai. -> Pray so the heart is peaceful.
104. Krama ngedasang desa mangda kedas. -> The people clean the village so it is clean.
105. Kanti. -> Until. [recycled — fresh sentences]
106. Icang ngantos kanti sanja. -> I wait until evening.
107. Kanti jani ia durung rauh. -> Until now he hasn't come.
108. Megawe kanti peteng, dadi kenyel. -> Working until night — (one) gets tired.
109. Antos. -> To wait (familiar).
110. Antos icang dini! -> Wait for me here!
111. Cai antos nyen? -> Whom are you waiting for?
112. Antos, ia lakar teka jani. -> Wait — he'll come now.
113. Yening ten. -> If not (refined phrase).
114. Yening ten ujan, tiang lakar mrika. -> If it doesn't rain, I'll go there.
115. Yening ten cai rauh, tiang sebet. -> If you don't come, I'll be sad.
116. Yening ten jani, nyanan? -> If not now, later?
117. Yen sing. -> If not (familiar phrase).
118. Yen sing ujan, icang lakar melali. -> If it doesn't rain, I'll travel.
119. Yen sing cai teka, icang sebet. -> If you don't come, I'm sad.
120. Yen sing jani, nyanan. -> If not now, later.
121. Be. -> Already (condition-met particle) (familiar).
122. Cai be madaar? -> Have you eaten already?
123. Cai be uning? -> Do you know already?
124. Ia be teka dini. -> He has already come here.
