### Lesson 57: Verbs 5 — Cognition

**Focus:** the inner verbs — know, forget, remember, understand, think, ponder, believe, agree, guess, suspect, hope, daydream, realize — plus certainty and desire (recycled).
**New in this lesson (teaching order):** nawang (recycled), uning (recycled), engsap (recycled), lali (recycled), inget, eling, ngerti, resep, pineh (recycled), manah (recycled), mapineh, mamanah, menehang, ngega, percaya, ngidep, narka, ngira, cager, ngarep, bengong (recycled), bingung (recycled), seken (recycled), janten (recycled), dot (recycled), kayun (recycled), pelih (recycled), iwang (recycled), ngelah keneh, maduwe manah, sadar
*(Note: standalone `mapineh`/`mamanah` (thinking) are unlocked here — before this they appeared only inside the set phrases `tiang mapineh` / `titiang mamanah` from Lesson 34. `maduwe` exists only inside the phrase `maduwe manah`.)*

1. Nawang. -> To know (familiar). [recycled from Lesson 4 — fresh sentences]
2. Icang nawang unduk nika. -> I know about that matter.
3. Cai nawang satua nika? -> Do you know that story?
4. Ia nawang liu indik Bali. -> He knows a lot about Bali.
5. Uning. -> To know (refined). [recycled — fresh sentences]
6. Tiang uning indik odalan. -> I know about the odalan.
7. Ragane uning sira ida? -> Do you know who he / she (honored) is?
8. Ipun uning Basa Bali alus. -> He / she knows refined Balinese.
9. Engsap. -> To forget (familiar). [recycled from Lesson 31 — fresh sentences]
10. Icang engsap adan timpal icang! -> I forgot my friend's name!
11. Cai engsap ngaba buku? -> Did you forget to bring the book?
12. Engsap, ampura — icang sibuk gati. -> Forgot — sorry, I'm very busy.
13. Lali. -> To forget (refined). [recycled — fresh sentences]
14. Tiang lali wastan ipun. -> I forgot his / her name.
15. Sampunang lali mebakti. -> Don't forget to pray.
16. Ragane lali napi jani? -> What did you forget now?
17. Inget. -> To remember (familiar).
18. Icang inget cai! -> I remember you!
19. Cai inget adan icang? -> Do you remember my name?
20. Inget, da engsap! -> Remember — don't forget!
21. Eling. -> To remember (refined).
22. Tiang eling ring ida. -> I remember him / her (honored).
23. Ragane eling wastan tiang? -> Do you remember my name?
24. Eling lan inget pateh? -> Are eling and inget the same?
25. Ngerti. -> To understand (neutral).
26. Icang ngerti Basa Bali akidik. -> I understand a little Balinese.
27. Cai ngerti satua nika? -> Do you understand that story?
28. Ngerti, nggih, suksma! -> Understood, yes, thanks!
29. Resep. -> To understand (familiar).
30. Icang resep unduk nika. -> I understand that matter.
31. Cai resep Basa Bali? -> Do you understand Balinese?
32. Ia resep gati. -> He understands very well.
33. Pineh. -> To think (familiar). [recycled from Lesson 34 — fresh sentences]
34. Icang pineh nika beneh. -> I think that is right.
35. Cai pineh apa jani? -> What do you think now?
36. Pineh icang, ia lakar teka. -> I think he will come.
37. Manah. -> To think / mind (refined). [recycled — fresh sentences]
38. Tiang manah nika patut. -> I think that is proper.
39. Manah ragane napi indik nika? -> What do you think about that?
40. Manah ipun becik. -> His / her thinking is good.
41. Mapineh. -> Thinking (familiar).
42. Icang mapineh indik cai. -> I'm thinking about you.
43. Cai mapineh apa? -> What are you thinking?
44. Ia mapineh nika pelih. -> He thinks that is wrong.
45. Mamanah. -> Thinking (refined).
46. Tiang mamanah indik odalan. -> I'm thinking about the odalan.
47. Ipun mamanah napi? -> What is he / she thinking?
48. Mamanah dumun, raris baosang. -> Think first, then say it.
49. Menehang. -> To think over / ponder (familiar).
50. Menehang unduk nika dumun. -> Think over that matter first.
51. Cai menehang apa? -> What are you pondering?
52. Menehang nika melah baang keneh. -> Pondering is good for the mind.
53. Ngega. -> To believe (familiar).
54. Icang ngega cai. -> I believe you.
55. Cai ngega satua nika? -> Do you believe that story?
56. Ngega icang, nika seken! -> Believe me — it's true!
57. Percaya. -> To believe (neutral).
58. Icang percaya cai. -> I believe you.
59. Percaya nika penting. -> Trust is important.
60. Cai percaya kone? -> Do you believe what they say?
61. Ngidep. -> To agree / understand (familiar).
62. Icang ngidep. -> I agree / understand.
63. Cai ngidep unduk nika? -> Do you understand that matter?
64. Ngidep lan setuju pateh? -> Are ngidep and setuju the same?
65. Narka. -> To suspect / guess (familiar).
66. Icang narka ia lakar teka. -> I suspect he will come.
67. Narka cai nyen nika? -> Whom do you guess that is?
68. Narka icang nika bogbog. -> I guess that's a lie.
69. Ngira. -> To guess / reckon (familiar).
70. Icang ngira ujan mani. -> I reckon it'll rain tomorrow.
71. Cai ngira ia nyen? -> Who do you think he is?
72. Ngira icang nika leres. -> I reckon that is true.
73. Cager. -> To hope / rely on (familiar).
74. Icang cager ring cai. -> I rely on you.
75. Cager cai lakar teka. -> I hope you will come.
76. Icang cager ia jemet. -> I hope he is diligent.
77. Ngarep. -> To hope (familiar).
78. Icang ngarep cai teka mani. -> I hope you come tomorrow.
79. Ngarep apa cai? -> What do you hope for?
80. Ngarep lan cager pateh? -> Are ngarep and cager the same?
81. Bengong. -> To daydream / stare blankly. [recycled from Lesson 36 — fresh sentences]
82. Cai bengong jani, ngudiang? -> You're spacing out now — why?
83. Ia bengong ningal pragina. -> He stares mesmerized at the dancer.
84. Bengong, inget malajah! -> Stop daydreaming — remember to study!
85. Bingung. -> Confused. [recycled from Lesson 16 — fresh sentences]
86. Icang bingung indik satua nika. -> I'm confused about that story.
87. Cai bingung kenapa jani? -> Why are you confused now?
88. Bingung, nanging nyanan ngerti. -> Confused — but later (I'll) understand.
89. Seken. -> Sure / certain. [recycled — fresh sentences]
90. Icang seken nawang ia. -> I know him for sure.
91. Seken nika leres? -> Is that certainly true?
92. Tiang seken, nika patut. -> I'm sure — that is right.
93. Janten. -> Certainly. [recycled — fresh sentences]
94. Janten tiang rauh mani. -> I will certainly come tomorrow.
95. Janten ida uning unduk nika. -> He / she (honored) certainly knows that matter.
96. Nika janten dados. -> That will certainly be possible.
97. Dot. -> To want. [recycled from Lesson 10 — fresh sentences]
98. Icang dot ngerti Basa Bali. -> I want to understand Balinese.
99. Cai dot uning apa? -> What do you want to know?
100. Ia dot percaya cai. -> He wants to believe you.
101. Kayun. -> To want (highest refined). [recycled — fresh sentences]
102. Ida kayun uning indik cai. -> He / she (honored) wants to know about you.
103. Kayun ida napi jani? -> What does he / she (honored) want now?
104. Ida kayun mireng satua. -> He / she (honored) wants to hear the story.
105. Pelih. -> Wrong / to misunderstand. [recycled from Lesson 3 — fresh sentences]
106. Tiang pelih ngerti, ampura. -> I misunderstood — sorry.
107. Cai pelih mireng gatra nika. -> You misheard that news.
108. Ia pelih, nanging ia sadar. -> He was wrong, but he realized it.
109. Iwang. -> Wrong / mistaken (refined). [recycled — fresh sentences]
110. Tiang iwang mireng, sinampura. -> I misheard — apologies.
111. Ipun iwang ngerti unduk nika. -> He / she misunderstood that matter.
112. Nenten iwang, nika leres. -> Not wrong — that is true.
113. Ngelah keneh. -> To have an idea (phrase).
114. Icang ngelah keneh melah! -> I have a good idea!
115. Cai ngelah keneh apa? -> What idea do you have?
116. Ngelah keneh, baosang ring icang! -> Have an idea — tell me!
117. Maduwe manah. -> To have an idea / intention (refined phrase).
118. Tiang maduwe manah malajah igel. -> I intend to learn dance.
119. Ida maduwe manah napi? -> What intention does he / she (honored) have?
120. Ipun maduwe manah becik. -> He / she has good intentions.
121. Sadar. -> To realize.
122. Icang sadar jani. -> I realize it now.
123. Cai sadar pelih caine? -> Do you realize your mistake?
124. Sadar nika penting baang keneh. -> Awareness is important for the mind.

---

### Lesson 58: Polite Register (Alus) — A Deeper Dive

**Focus:** the crown of the speech-level system — the humble "I" (`titiang`), honored-subject verbs (`rauh`, `mantuk`, `makolem`, `seda`, `ngandika`), humble verbs (`ngambil`, `makta`, `ngaksi`, `nyingak`), and the respected dwellings (`jero`, `puri`, `griya`).
**Grammar note — sor-singgih logic:** humble words lower the SPEAKER (`titiang`, `ngambil`, `mapamit`); honoring words raise the OTHER (`ida`, `rauh`, `makta`, `ngandika`, `seda`). `Titiang` is now the default polite "I" in refined sentences.
**New in this lesson (teaching order):** titiang (unlocked!), ida (recycled), ngambil, mapamit, puput (recycled), suksma (recycled), suksma mewali (recycled), nunas lugra, nunas pica (recycled), mapesengan, malinggih (recycled), rauh (recycled), kairing, makta (recycled), ngaksi, nyingak, ngandika (unlocked), nyuryanin, ngaturang (recycled), pakaryan (recycled), jagi (recycled), ledang (recycled), ngicalang, mantuk (recycled), seda, padem (recycled), makolem, wau (recycled), jero (recycled), puri (recycled), griya (recycled), ngrayunang

1. Titiang. -> I (humble, refined).
2. Titiang uning Basa Bali akidik. -> I know a little Balinese.
3. Titiang nunas ampura. -> I ask for forgiveness.
4. Titiang jagi mantuk. -> I will go home.
5. Ida. -> He / she (honored). [recycled from Lesson 1 — fresh sentences]
6. Ida linggih ring puri. -> He / she (honored) resides at the palace.
7. Ida sira wastanne? -> Who is he / she (honored)?
8. Ida sampun rauh? -> Has he / she (honored) arrived?
9. Ngambil. -> To take (refined, humble action).
10. Titiang ngambil buku nika. -> I take that book.
11. Ragane ngambil buku nika? -> Are you taking that book?
12. Titiang ngambil ajengan. -> I take the food.
13. Mapamit. -> To excuse oneself (refined).
14. Titiang mapamit dumun. -> I take my leave first.
15. Titiang mapamit, dumogi rahayu. -> I take my leave — may you be blessed.
16. Ipun mapamit ring ida. -> He / she takes leave of him / her (honored).
17. Puput. -> Finished (refined). [recycled from Lesson 8 — fresh sentences]
18. Upacarane puput wau. -> The ceremony just finished.
19. Puput, tiang mapamit. -> Finished — I take my leave.
20. Puput napi durung? -> Finished or not yet?
21. Suksma. -> Thank you. [recycled from Lesson 3 — fresh sentences]
22. Suksma gati baang ragane. -> Thank you very much (for you).
23. Suksma, titiang sampun neda. -> Thanks — I have eaten.
24. Suksma mewali. -> You're welcome (refined). [recycled — fresh sentences]
25. Suksma mewali, nggih. -> You're welcome, yes.
26. Suksma mewali, dumogi rahayu. -> You're welcome — may you be blessed.
27. Suksma, suksma mewali! -> Thanks — you're welcome!
28. Nunas lugra. -> To ask for permission (phrase).
29. Titiang nunas lugra mapamit. -> I ask permission to take my leave.
30. Nunas lugra, titiang jagi mrika. -> With permission, I will go there.
31. Titiang nunas lugra ngambil nika. -> I ask permission to take that.
32. Nunas pica. -> To ask for a blessing. [recycled from Lesson 35 — fresh sentences]
33. Titiang nunas pica ring ida. -> I ask him / her (honored) for a blessing.
34. Krama nunas pica ring pandita. -> The people ask the priest for blessings.
35. Nunas pica, dumogi rahayu. -> Asking for blessing — may (we) be safe.
36. Mapesengan. -> To be named (refined humble).
37. Titiang mapesengan Wayan. -> My name is Wayan.
38. Ipun mapesengan Made. -> He / she is named Made.
39. Mapesengan napi ragane? -> What is your name?
40. Malinggih. -> To sit / reside (refined). [recycled from Lesson 11 — fresh sentences]
41. Ida malinggih ring puri. -> He / she (honored) resides at the palace.
42. Durus malinggih driki. -> Please sit here.
43. Ipun malinggih nampek ring pura. -> He / she resides near the temple.
44. Rauh. -> To arrive (refined). [recycled from Lesson 2 — fresh sentences]
45. Ida wau rauh saking Jawa. -> He / she (honored) just arrived from Java.
46. Rahajeng rauh ring jero titiang! -> Welcome to my house!
47. Benjang ida rauh? -> Does he / she (honored) come tomorrow?
48. Kairing. -> Accompanied by (refined).
49. Ida kairing krama. -> He / she (honored) is accompanied by the people.
50. Ipun kairing sawitra ipun. -> He / she is accompanied by friends.
51. Kairing nyen ida? -> Who accompanies him / she (honored)?
52. Makta. -> To bring (refined). [recycled from Lesson 7 — fresh sentences]
53. Titiang makta banten ring pura. -> I bring offerings to the temple.
54. Ragane makta ajengan? -> Are you bringing food?
55. Makta nika mrika, nggih. -> Bring that there, okay.
56. Ngaksi. -> To see (refined, humble).
57. Titiang ngaksi ida ring pura. -> I see him / her (honored) at the temple.
58. Ngaksi napi ragane? -> What do you see?
59. Titiang ngaksi tari becik. -> I see a beautiful dance.
60. Nyingak. -> To see (refined).
61. Titiang nyingak gunung Agung. -> I see Mount Agung.
62. Nyingak ida driki, titiang liang. -> Seeing him / her (honored) here, I am happy.
63. Ragane nyingak napi? -> What do you see?
64. Ngandika. -> To speak (honored, high-to-low). [standalone sense unlocked]
65. Ida ngandika ring krama. -> He / she (honored) speaks to the people.
66. Ngandika ida alus gati. -> He / she (honored) speaks very gently.
67. Sampun ngandika ida? -> Has he / she (honored) spoken?
68. Nyuryanin. -> To shine upon / bless (refined).
69. Matan ai nyuryanin jagat. -> The sun shines upon the world.
70. Dumogi Ida Bhatara nyuryanin desa. -> May the deity bless the village.
71. Surya nyuryanin gumi Bali. -> The sun shines upon the land of Bali.
72. Ngaturang. -> To offer / give upward (refined). [recycled from Lesson 24 — fresh sentences]
73. Titiang ngaturang banten ring pura. -> I offer banten at the temple.
74. Titiang ngaturang canang sari. -> I offer canang sari.
75. Ngaturang napi ragane? -> What are you offering?
76. Pakaryan. -> Work (refined noun). [recycled from Lesson 11 — fresh sentences]
77. Pakaryan ida becik gati. -> His / her (honored) work is very good.
78. Pakaryan titiang alit. -> My work is small (humble).
79. Pakaryan napi ring pura? -> What work is there at the temple?
80. Jagi. -> Will (refined). [recycled — fresh sentences]
81. Titiang jagi malajah Basa Bali. -> I will study Balinese.
82. Ida jagi ngandika? -> Will he / she (honored) speak?
83. Jagi rauh benjang, ida? -> Will he / she (honored) come tomorrow?
84. Ledang. -> Pleased / willing (refined). [recycled from Lesson 16 — fresh sentences]
85. Titiang ledang ngayah ring pura. -> I am pleased to serve at the temple.
86. Ida ledang mireng satua. -> He / she (honored) is pleased to hear stories.
87. Ledang ragane nulungin titiang? -> Would you be willing to help me?
88. Ngicalang. -> To lose / eliminate (refined).
89. Titiang ngicalang buku titiang! -> I lost my book!
90. Ngicalang sedih nika keweh. -> Getting rid of that sadness is hard.
91. Ida ngicalang napi? -> What is he / she (honored) removing?
92. Mantuk. -> To return / go home (refined). [recycled from Lesson 11 — fresh sentences]
93. Titiang jagi mantuk dumun. -> I will go home first.
94. Ida sampun mantuk? -> Has he / she (honored) already returned?
95. Mantuk dumun, nggih. -> (I'll) go home first, okay.
96. Seda. -> To pass away (for high persons) (refined).
97. Ida seda wau. -> He / she (honored) just passed away.
98. Seda lan padem nenten pateh. -> Seda and padem are not the same (level).
99. Ida seda, krama sebet. -> He / she (honored) passed away — the people are sad.
100. Padem. -> To pass away (refined). [recycled from Lesson 54 — fresh sentences]
101. Ipun padem dibi. -> He / she passed away yesterday.
102. Padem nika pasti. -> Death is certain.
103. Sesampun padem, krama ngae ngaben. -> After death, the people hold the cremation.
104. Makolem. -> To sleep (for high persons) (refined).
105. Ida makolem ring puri. -> He / she (honored) sleeps at the palace.
106. Sampun makolem ida? -> Has he / she (honored) already slept?
107. Ida makolem, meneng nggih. -> He / she (honored) is sleeping — quiet, okay.
108. Wau. -> Just now (refined). [recycled from Lesson 14 — fresh sentences]
109. Titiang wau rauh. -> I just arrived.
110. Ida wau ngandika ring titiang. -> He / she (honored) just spoke to me.
111. Wau titiang ngaksi ida. -> I just saw him / her (honored).
112. Jero. -> House / home (respectful). [recycled from Lesson 7 — fresh sentences]
113. Jero titiang alit. -> My house is small (humble).
114. Jero ida nampek ring puri. -> His / her (honored) home is near the palace.
115. Jero titiang ring desa. -> My house is in the village.
116. Puri. -> Royal palace (refined). [recycled from Lesson 27 — fresh sentences]
117. Puri nika genah ida. -> The palace is his / her (honored) residence.
118. Titiang jagi ka puri. -> I will go to the palace.
119. Puri lan griya nenten pateh. -> Puri and griya are not the same.
120. Griya. -> Priest's house (refined). [recycled — fresh sentences]
121. Griya ida nampek ring pura. -> His / her (honored) griya is near the temple.
122. Titiang ka griya nunas pica. -> I go to the griya to ask for blessing.
123. Griya lan puri pateh? -> Are griya and puri the same?
124. Wénten pandita ring griya. -> There is a priest at the griya.

---

125. Ngrayunang. -> To eat (highest honour).
126. Ida ngrayunang. -> He / she (honored) eats.
127. Ida sampun ngrayunang. -> He / she (honored) has eaten.

### Lesson 59: Travel & Tourism

**Focus:** the tourism world — guests, foreigners, hotels, tickets, guides, passports, flights, vacations, souvenirs, maps, cameras, swimming, diving, sunbathing, and sightseeing.
**New in this lesson (teaching order):** tamiu (recycled), toris (recycled), bule, hotel, penginapan, tiket (recycled), pandu wisata, pemandu, paspor, bandara (recycled), penerbangan, liburan, melali (recycled), plesir, oleh-oleh, peta, kamera, foto, mefoto, berenang, langi, nyilem, jemur, matan ai (recycled), pasih (recycled), pura (recycled), sawah, carik (recycled), sewa montor, rega pas, jalan-jalan
*(Note: `bule` is the familiar (blunt but everyday) word for a foreigner; `toris` and `tamiu` are safer/politer. `plesir` (refined) and `melali` (familiar) both mean "to travel for pleasure".)*

1. Tamiu. -> Guest. [recycled from Lesson 4 — fresh sentences]
2. Tamiu rauh ring jero titiang. -> Guests arrive at my house.
3. Ada tamiu di banjar. -> There are guests at the banjar.
4. Tamiu nika saking dija? -> Where are those guests from?
5. Toris. -> Tourist. [recycled — fresh sentences]
6. Toris liu di pantai jani. -> There are many tourists at the beach now.
7. Toris nika malajah Basa Bali. -> The tourist learns Balinese.
8. Toris demen ogoh-ogoh. -> Tourists love the ogoh-ogoh.
9. Bule. -> Foreigner (familiar/blunt).
10. Ada bule di peken! -> There's a foreigner at the market!
11. Bule nika bisa Basa Bali? -> Can that foreigner speak Balinese?
12. Bule lan toris pateh? -> Are bule and toris the same?
13. Hotel. -> Hotel.
14. Hotel nika gede gati. -> The hotel is very big.
15. Toris nongos ring hotel. -> Tourists stay at the hotel.
16. Hotel nika dija? -> Where is that hotel?
17. Penginapan. -> Guesthouse.
18. Penginapan nika alit nanging kedas. -> The guesthouse is small but clean.
19. Penginapan nika aji kuda? -> How much is that guesthouse?
20. Ada penginapan paek di pasih. -> There is a guesthouse near the beach.
21. Tiket. -> Ticket. [recycled from Lesson 25 — fresh sentences]
22. Tiket pesawat nika mahal. -> The plane ticket is expensive.
23. Icang lakar meli tiket. -> I will buy a ticket.
24. Tiket nika baang sira? -> For whom is that ticket?
25. Pandu wisata. -> Tour guide (phrase).
26. Pandu wisata nika uning liu satua. -> The tour guide knows many stories.
27. Cai dadi pandu wisata? -> Are you a tour guide?
28. Pandu wisata nika alus mabaos. -> The tour guide speaks politely.
29. Pemandu. -> Guide (neutral).
30. Pemandu nika becik gati. -> The guide is very good.
31. Pemandu lan pandu wisata pateh? -> Are pemandu and pandu wisata the same?
32. Pemandu nika ajak toris ka pura. -> The guide goes with tourists to the temple.
33. Paspor. -> Passport.
34. Paspor icang dija? -> Where is my passport?
35. Toris nika ngaba paspor. -> The tourist carries a passport.
36. Paspor nika penting gati. -> The passport is very important.
37. Bandara. -> Airport. [recycled from Lesson 38 — fresh sentences]
38. Tiang ka bandara ajak timpal. -> I go to the airport with a friend.
39. Bandara nika gede tur baru. -> The airport is big and new.
40. Toris rauh ka bandara jani. -> Tourists arrive at the airport now.
41. Penerbangan. -> Flight.
42. Penerbangan nika pidan? -> When is the flight?
43. Penerbangan ka Jawa mahal? -> Is the flight to Java expensive?
44. Tiket penerbangan icang dija? -> Where is my flight ticket?
45. Liburan. -> Vacation (neutral).
46. Liburan icang ka Bali! -> My vacation is to Bali!
47. Cai liburan pidan? -> When is your vacation?
48. Liburan nika liang gati. -> The vacation is so fun.
49. Melali. -> To travel / hang out (familiar). [recycled from Lesson 33 — fresh sentences]
50. Icang lakar melali ka danu. -> I will travel to the lake.
51. Cai dot melali kija jani? -> Where do you want to travel now?
52. Melali ajak timpal liang gati. -> Traveling with friends is so fun.
53. Plesir. -> To travel (refined).
54. Titiang jagi plesir ka segara. -> I will travel to the sea.
55. Ida plesir ring dija? -> Where does he / she (honored) travel?
56. Plesir lan melali pateh? -> Are plesir and melali the same?
57. Oleh-oleh. -> Souvenir.
58. Icang lakar meli oleh-oleh. -> I will buy souvenirs.
59. Oleh-oleh nika baang nyen? -> For whom are the souvenirs?
60. Oleh-oleh Bali melah gati. -> Balinese souvenirs are so nice.
61. Peta. -> Map.
62. Peta Bali nika dija? -> Where is that map of Bali?
63. Icang nolih peta. -> I look at the map.
64. Peta nika nulungin toris. -> The map helps the tourists.
65. Kamera. -> Camera.
66. Kamera icang baru. -> My camera is new.
67. Toris nika ngaba kamera gede. -> The tourist carries a big camera.
68. Kamera nika mahal? -> Is that camera expensive?
69. Foto. -> Photo.
70. Foto nika melah gati. -> The photo is so nice.
71. Ada foto icang ring pura. -> There is a photo of me at the temple.
72. Foto cai dija? -> Where is your photo?
73. Mefoto. -> To take a photo (familiar).
74. Toris mefoto ring segara. -> The tourist takes photos at the sea.
75. Nyai mefoto ajak timpal? -> Do you take photos with friends?
76. Mefoto di pura dadi? -> Is it okay to take photos at the temple?
77. Berenang. -> To swim (neutral).
78. Panak icang berenang di pasih. -> My child swims at the beach.
79. Cai bisa berenang? -> Can you swim?
80. Berenang ring danu dadi? -> Is it okay to swim in the lake?
81. Langi. -> To swim (familiar).
82. Icang langi di segara. -> I swim in the sea.
83. Cai langi pidan? -> When do you swim?
84. Langi lan berenang pateh? -> Are langi and berenang the same?
85. Nyilem. -> To dive.
86. Toris nyilem ring segara. -> The tourists dive in the sea.
87. Cai bisa nyilem? -> Can you dive?
88. Nyilem nika keweh? -> Is diving hard?
89. Jemur. -> To sunbathe / to sun-dry.
90. Toris jemur di pasih. -> The tourists sunbathe at the beach.
91. Meme jemur padi. -> Mother sun-dries the paddy.
92. Cai jemur baju di natah? -> Do you dry clothes in the courtyard?
93. Matan ai. -> Sun. [recycled from Lesson 23 — fresh sentences]
94. Matan ai nyentak, toris demen jemur. -> The sun is scorching — tourists love to sunbathe.
95. Matan ai becik baang padi. -> The sun is good for the paddy.
96. Matan ai sunar, icang ka pasih. -> The sun is bright — I go to the beach.
97. Pasih. -> Beach. [recycled from Lesson 12 — fresh sentences]
98. Pasih Bali melah gati! -> Bali's beaches are so beautiful!
99. Toris berenang di pasih. -> Tourists swim at the beach.
100. Pasih nika dija, cai uning? -> Where is that beach — do you know?
101. Pura. -> Temple. [recycled — fresh sentences]
102. Toris mabalih upacara ring pura. -> Tourists watch a ceremony at the temple.
103. Pura nika genah suci baang krama. -> The temple is a sacred place for the people.
104. Ka pura nganggo kamben, nggih. -> To the temple wearing a sarong, okay.
105. Sawah. -> Rice terraces (neutral).
106. Sawah Bali melah gati. -> Bali's rice terraces are so beautiful.
107. Toris demen ningal sawah. -> Tourists love seeing rice terraces.
108. Sawah lan carik pateh? -> Are sawah and carik the same?
109. Carik. -> Rice field. [recycled — fresh sentences]
110. Carik nika ijo gati jani. -> The rice field is so green now.
111. Toris mefoto ring carik. -> The tourist takes photos in the rice field.
112. Carik icang nampek ring hotel. -> My rice field is near the hotel.
113. Sewa montor. -> Renting a motorbike (phrase).
114. Toris sewa montor di Bali. -> Tourists rent motorbikes in Bali.
115. Sewa montor nika aji kuda? -> How much is it to rent a motorbike?
116. Cai lakar sewa montor? -> Will you rent a motorbike?
117. Rega pas. -> Fixed price (phrase).
118. Rega pas, nenten dadi tawar. -> Fixed price — cannot bargain.
119. Rega pas nika kuda? -> How much is the fixed price?
120. Toko nika rega pas. -> That store has fixed prices.
121. Jalan-jalan. -> Sightseeing / strolling (phrase).
122. Icang jalan-jalan ka pasih. -> I go sightseeing to the beach.
123. Jalan-jalan lan melali pateh? -> Are jalan-jalan and melali the same?
124. Toris jalan-jalan ring desa. -> The tourist strolls through the village.

### Lesson 60: Adverbs of Degree

**Focus:** the degree toolkit — very (`pisan`, `sajan`, `gati`), too (`bes`, `bakat`, `kaliwat`, `liwat`, `luyu`), just a little, more/less, almost, only (`tuah`, `wantah`, `dogen`, `kemanten`), exactly, no wonder, and not yet/finished.
**Grammar note:** the intensifiers finally unlock: `pisan` (refined, after adjective), `sajan` (familiar, before/after), `gati` (familiar, after). "Only" has four flavors: `tuah`/`dogen` (familiar), `wantah`/`kemanten` (refined).
**New in this lesson (teaching order):** pisan (unlocked!), sajan, gati (recycled), bakat (recycled — "too much"), bes, bes bedik, bedik (recycled), kidik (recycled), akidik (recycled), abedik (recycled), lebih (recycled), lebian (recycled), kuang (recycled), kirang (recycled), hampir, nyaris, pas (recycled), paling (recycled), tuah, wantah, dogen (recycled), kemanten (recycled), pantes (recycled), patut (recycled), kaliwat, liwat, luyu, tonden (recycled), durung (recycled), suud (recycled), puput (recycled)

1. Pisan. -> Very (refined). [unlocked!]
2. Melah pisan tari nika. -> The dance is very beautiful.
3. Suksma pisan ring ragane. -> Thank you very much.
4. Ida lingsir pisan. -> He / she (honored) is very old.
5. Sajan. -> Very / really (familiar).
6. Sajan melah gumi Bali! -> Bali really is beautiful!
7. Jegeg sajan nyai! -> You really are beautiful!
8. Sajan nika beneh. -> That really is correct.
9. Gati. -> Very (familiar). [recycled from Lesson 11 — fresh sentences]
10. Jaen gati kopi niki! -> This coffee is so delicious!
11. Kebus gati jani! -> It's so hot now!
12. Mahal gati baju nika. -> That shirt is so expensive.
13. Bakat. -> Too much (familiar). [recycled from Lesson 31 — new sense]
14. Bakat lalah jukut niki! -> These vegetables are too spicy!
15. Bakat mahal nika. -> That's too expensive.
16. Bakat kebus jani, icang bedak. -> It's too hot now — I'm thirsty.
17. Bes. -> Too (familiar).
18. Bes mahal baju nika. -> That shirt is too expensive.
19. Bes lalah, icang nenten nyidaang madaar. -> Too spicy — I can't eat it.
20. Bes gede montor nika. -> That motorbike is too big.
21. Bes bedik. -> Too little (phrase).
22. Jinah icang bes bedik. -> My money is too little.
23. Bes bedik toya niki. -> There's too little water here.
24. Gajian nika bes bedik. -> That salary is too small.
25. Bedik. -> A little / few (familiar). [recycled from Lesson 8 — fresh sentences]
26. Jinah icang bedik jani. -> My money is little now.
27. Bedik, nenten liu. -> Little, not much.
28. Buku icang bedik, buku caine liu. -> My books are few, yours are many.
29. Kidik. -> A little / few (refined). [recycled — fresh sentences]
30. Jinah tiang kidik. -> My money is little.
31. Kidik nanging becik. -> Little but good.
32. Galah ida kidik jani. -> He / she (honored) has little time now.
33. Akidik. -> Just a little (refined). [recycled — fresh sentences]
34. Tiang neda akidik jani. -> I eat just a little now.
35. Akidik galah, nggih. -> Just a little time, okay.
36. Tiang nginem akidik kopi. -> I drink just a little coffee.
37. Abedik. -> Just a little (familiar). [recycled — fresh sentences]
38. Abedik be, suksma. -> Just a little meat, thanks.
39. Abedik jinah icang. -> I have just a little money.
40. Nunas abedik toya. -> (May I) ask for just a little water.
41. Lebih. -> More. [recycled from Lesson 39 — fresh sentences]
42. Cai dot lebih? -> Do you want more?
43. Lebih melah niki teken nika. -> This is better than that.
44. Tiang dot lebih kopi. -> I want more coffee.
45. Lebian. -> More than / too much (familiar). [recycled — fresh sentences]
46. Lebian uyah niki! -> Too much salt in this!
47. Lebian teken dasa buku. -> More than ten books.
48. Gula nika lebian, manis rasanne. -> Too much sugar — it tastes very sweet.
49. Kuang. -> Less (familiar). [recycled from Lesson 21 — fresh sentences]
50. Kuang uyah, anyep rasanne. -> Too little salt — it tastes bland.
51. Jinah icang kuang. -> My money is not enough.
52. Kuang siki. -> One short.
53. Kirang. -> Less / lacking (refined). [recycled — fresh sentences]
54. Kirang toya ring paon. -> There is not enough water in the kitchen.
55. Galah ida kirang. -> He / she (honored) lacks time.
56. Jinah tiang kirang baang nika. -> My money is not enough for that.
57. Hampir. -> Almost (neutral).
58. Hampir ujan jani. -> It's almost raining now.
59. Icang hampir pules di bale. -> I almost fell asleep at the pavilion.
60. Hampir dasa buku icang maca. -> I've read almost ten books.
61. Nyaris. -> Almost (neutral variant).
62. Nyaris icang paling di alas! -> I almost got lost in the forest!
63. Nyaris ujan bales. -> It almost rained heavily.
64. Nyaris icang bakat blabar. -> I almost got caught by the flood.
65. Pas. -> Exactly (neutral). [recycled from Lesson 21 — fresh sentences]
66. Pas dasa piring. -> Exactly ten plates.
67. Jam pas roras ia teka. -> He came at exactly twelve o'clock.
68. Jinah caine pas? -> Is your money exact?
69. Paling. -> Most. [recycled from Lesson 39 — fresh sentences]
70. Segara nika paling melah baang icang. -> The sea is the most beautiful for me.
71. Nyen paling jemet ring desa nika? -> Who is the most diligent in that village?
72. Kopi Bali paling jaen! -> Balinese coffee is the most delicious!
73. Tuah. -> Only (familiar).
74. Tuah icang dini. -> Only I am here.
75. Tuah nika icang uning. -> That's all I know.
76. Tuah abedik jinah icang. -> I have only a little money.
77. Wantah. -> Only (refined).
78. Wantah tiang rauh. -> Only I came.
79. Wantah nika tiang uning. -> That alone I know.
80. Wantah akidik galah ida. -> He / she (honored) has only a little time.
81. Dogen. -> Only / just (familiar). [recycled from Lesson 56 — fresh sentences]
82. Icang dogen nenten uning. -> Only I don't know.
83. Nika dogen icang dot. -> That alone is what I want.
84. Dogen nika? -> Just that?
85. Kemanten. -> Only (refined). [recycled — fresh sentences]
86. Nika kemanten tiang uning. -> That alone do I know.
87. Akidik kemanten toya nika. -> There is only a little water.
88. Kemanten nika, nggih? -> Just that, okay?
89. Pantes. -> No wonder / appropriate. [recycled from Lesson 34 — fresh sentences]
90. Pantes ia paling, desa nika linggah. -> No wonder he got lost — the village is wide.
91. Pantes ia kenyel, ia megawe kanti peteng. -> No wonder he's tired — he works until night.
92. Nika pantes baang ida. -> That is appropriate for him / her (honored).
93. Patut. -> Proper / no wonder (refined). [recycled from Lesson 1 — fresh sentences]
94. Patut ida ledang. -> No wonder he / she (honored) is pleased.
95. Patut krama ngayah, nika dharma. -> It's proper for people to serve — that is dharma.
96. Patut pisan gatrane. -> The news is indeed right.
97. Kaliwat. -> Exceedingly (familiar).
98. Jaen kaliwat jukut niki! -> These vegetables are exceedingly delicious!
99. Kaliwat mahal! -> Exceedingly expensive!
100. Kebus kaliwat jani. -> It's exceedingly hot now.
101. Liwat. -> Exceeding (familiar).
102. Liwat mahal baju nika. -> That shirt is exceedingly expensive.
103. Liwat panas matan ai jani. -> The sun is exceedingly hot now.
104. Kebus liwat, icang bedak gati. -> Too hot — I'm very thirsty.
105. Luyu. -> Extremely (familiar).
106. Jegeg luyu bajang nika! -> That young woman is extremely beautiful!
107. Luyu mahal nika. -> That is extremely expensive.
108. Luyu liang icang jani! -> I'm extremely happy now!
109. Tonden. -> Not yet (familiar). [recycled from Lesson 2 — fresh sentences]
110. Kopi icang tonden puput. -> My coffee isn't finished yet.
111. Ida tonden rauh kanti jani. -> He / she (honored) hasn't come until now.
112. Tonden madaar, icang luwe. -> Haven't eaten — I'm hungry.
113. Durung. -> Not yet (refined). [recycled — fresh sentences]
114. Durung puput pakaryan ida. -> His / her (honored) work isn't finished yet.
115. Tiang durung ngaksi ida. -> I haven't seen him / her (honored) yet.
116. Durung, nyanan. -> Not yet — later.
117. Suud. -> Finished / stop. [recycled from Lesson 30 — fresh sentences]
118. Upacarane suud jam kuda? -> What time does the ceremony finish?
119. Suud mebakti, krama mantuk. -> After praying, the people go home.
120. Satua nika suud, panak icang pules. -> The story is over — my child sleeps.
121. Puput. -> Finished (refined). [recycled from Lesson 8 — fresh sentences]
122. Puput upacara nika wau. -> The ceremony just finished.
123. Pakaryan titiang sampun puput. -> My work is already finished.
124. Puput, ngiring mantuk! -> Finished — let's go home!

### Lesson 61: Verbs 6 — Movement

**Focus:** up/down, home (three levels!), out/in, searching, hiding, jumping, pulling/pushing, carrying (shoulder/head), grabbing, blocking, running, chasing, falling.
**Grammar note:** going home has three levels: `mulih` (familiar), `mantuk` (refined), `budal` (highest — royalty/high priests). Out: `pesu`/`medal`; in: `masuk`/`manjing`. Carrying: `negen` (shoulder), `nyuun` (head — the classic market pose).
**New in this lesson (teaching order):** negehang, munggah (recycled), nuunang, tedun, mulih (recycled), mantuk (recycled), budal, pesu, medal, masuk, manjing, ngalih (recycled), ngrereh (recycled), mengked, mengkeb, nyumput, makecog, mategul, ngebut, nganteng, ngedorong, ngungsi (recycled), negen, nyuun, nyambak, ngandang, mlayu (recycled), malaib (recycled), ngebeg, ulung, runtuh

1. Negehang. -> To climb / go up (familiar).
2. Bojog negehang punyan. -> The monkey climbs the tree.
3. Cai negehang bukit? -> Do you climb the hill?
4. Negehang nika keweh? -> Is climbing hard?
5. Munggah. -> To go up / ascend (refined). [recycled from Lesson 25 — fresh sentences]
6. Tiang munggah ka bukit. -> I go up the hill.
7. Ida munggah ka bale. -> He / she (honored) ascends to the pavilion.
8. Munggah lan negehang pateh? -> Are munggah and negehang the same?
9. Nuunang. -> To bring down / lower (familiar).
10. Icang nuunang buah uli punyan. -> I bring down fruit from the tree.
11. Nuunang sampi ka carik. -> Bring the cow down to the field.
12. Cai nuunang apa? -> What are you bringing down?
13. Tedun. -> To descend (refined).
14. Ida tedun saking bale. -> He / she (honored) descends from the pavilion.
15. Tedun lan munggah nenten pateh. -> Descend and ascend are not the same.
16. Ipun tedun alon-alon. -> He / she descends slowly.
17. Mulih. -> To go home (familiar). [recycled from Lesson 11 — fresh sentences]
18. Icang lakar mulih jani. -> I will go home now.
19. Cai mulih jam kuda? -> What time do you go home?
20. Mulih, icang kenyel gati. -> Going home — I'm very tired.
21. Mantuk. -> To return / go home (refined). [recycled — fresh sentences]
22. Ida mantuk ring puri. -> He / she (honored) returns to the palace.
23. Mantuk dumogi rahayu. -> Go home blessed.
24. Ipun sampun mantuk saking kota. -> He / she has returned from the city.
25. Budal. -> To go home (highest refined).
26. Ida budal ring puri. -> He / she (honored) goes home to the palace.
27. Ida sampun budal? -> Has he / she (honored) gone home?
28. Budal lan mantuk nenten pateh. -> Budal and mantuk are not the same (level).
29. Pesu. -> To go out (familiar).
30. Icang pesu jumah. -> I go out of the house.
31. Cai pesu kija? -> Where are you going out to?
32. Pesu jani, ujan suud! -> Come out now — the rain has stopped!
33. Medal. -> To go out / emerge (refined).
34. Ida medal ring jero. -> He / she (honored) comes out of the house.
35. Ipun medal raris memargi. -> He / she comes out, then walks.
36. Medal lan pesu pateh? -> Are medal and pesu the same?
37. Masuk. -> To go in / enter (neutral).
38. Icang masuk ka kamar. -> I enter the room.
39. Cai masuk pura nganggo kamen? -> Do you enter the temple wearing a sarong?
40. Toris masuk ka hotel. -> The tourist enters the hotel.
41. Manjing. -> To enter (refined).
42. Ida manjing ring pura. -> He / she (honored) enters the temple.
43. Manjing lan masuk pateh? -> Are manjing and masuk the same?
44. Ipun manjing ring griya. -> He / she enters the priest's house.
45. Ngalih. -> To look for (familiar). [recycled from Lesson 24 — fresh sentences]
46. Icang ngalih cicing icang. -> I look for my dog.
47. Cai ngalih apa di carik? -> What are you looking for in the field?
48. Ia ngalih timpalne di peken. -> He looks for his friend at the market.
49. Ngrereh. -> To seek (refined). [recycled — fresh sentences]
50. Tiang ngrereh buku ring toko. -> I seek the book at the store.
51. Ipun ngrereh pakaryan. -> He / she seeks work.
52. Ngrereh lan ngalih pateh? -> Are ngrereh and ngalih the same?
53. Mengked. -> To hide (familiar).
54. Bikul mengked di beten kasur. -> The mouse hides under the mattress.
55. Cai mengked dija? -> Where are you hiding?
56. Mengked, da pesu! -> Hide — don't come out!
57. Mengkeb. -> To hide (familiar variant).
58. Ia mengkeb di duri pintu. -> He hides behind the door.
59. Mengkeb lan mengked pateh? -> Are mengkeb and mengked the same?
60. Cicing nika mengkeb di sisi jero. -> The dog hides outside the house.
61. Nyumput. -> To hide (variant).
62. Panak icang nyumput di meten. -> My child hides in the sleeping room.
63. Cai nyumput, icang ngalih! -> You hide — I'll seek!
64. Maen nyumput liang. -> Playing hide-and-seek is fun.
65. Makecog. -> To jump (familiar).
66. Bojog makecog tegeh. -> The monkey jumps high.
67. Cai bisa makecog? -> Can you jump?
68. Makecog ka tukad? -> Jump into the river?
69. Mategul. -> To be tied / tie oneself (familiar).
70. Sampi mategul ring punyan. -> The cow is tied to the tree.
71. Jaran nika mategul di tali. -> The horse is tied with a rope.
72. Mategul lan negul pateh? -> Are mategul and negul the same?
73. Ngebut. -> To speed (neutral).
74. Cai ngebut di jalan, awas! -> You speed on the road — watch out!
75. Montor nika ngebut gati! -> That motorbike speeds so fast!
76. Da ngebut, baya! -> Don't speed — danger!
77. Nganteng. -> To pull (familiar).
78. Icang nganteng tali. -> I pull the rope.
79. Cai nganteng apa? -> What are you pulling?
80. Nganteng sampi nika keweh. -> Pulling that cow is hard.
81. Ngedorong. -> To push (familiar).
82. Icang ngedorong montor. -> I push the motorbike.
83. Cai ngedorong meja? -> Are you pushing the table?
84. Ngedorong mobil nika baat. -> Pushing that car is heavy.
85. Ngungsi. -> To evacuate / seek refuge. [recycled from Lesson 42 — fresh sentences]
86. Krama ngungsi krana linuh. -> The people evacuate because of the earthquake.
87. Cai ngungsi kija? -> Where do you evacuate?
88. Ngungsi ring banjar liu krama. -> Many people take refuge at the banjar.
89. Negen. -> To carry on the shoulder (familiar).
90. Bapa negen kayu. -> Father carries wood on his shoulder.
91. Cai negen apa? -> What are you carrying on your shoulder?
92. Negen tiing nika baat. -> Shouldering that bamboo is heavy.
93. Nyuun. -> To carry on the head (familiar).
94. Meme nyuun jukut saking peken. -> Mother carries vegetables home from the market on her head.
95. Nyuun nika keweh? -> Is head-carrying hard?
96. Dadong nyuun gebogan ka pura. -> Grandmother carries the gebogan to the temple on her head.
97. Nyambak. -> To grab (familiar).
98. Icang nyambak tali. -> I grab the rope.
99. Nyambak liman icang! -> Grab my hand!
100. Ia nyambak tiing dumun. -> He grabs the bamboo first.
101. Ngandang. -> To block / pen in (familiar).
102. Bapa ngandang sampi di carik. -> Father pens the cows in the field.
103. Batu nika ngandang jalan. -> That stone blocks the road.
104. Cai ngandang apa? -> What are you blocking?
105. Mlayu. -> To run (refined). [recycled from Lesson 11 — fresh sentences]
106. Ipun mlayu enggal ka pasar. -> He / she runs quickly to the market.
107. Kucing nika mlayu gati. -> The cat runs so fast.
108. Mlayu lan malaib pateh? -> Are mlayu and malaib the same?
109. Malaib. -> To run (familiar). [recycled — fresh sentences]
110. Icang malaib ka peken. -> I run to the market.
111. Panak icang malaib liang. -> My child runs joyfully.
112. Cai malaib enggal gati! -> You run so fast!
113. Ngebeg. -> To chase (familiar).
114. Cicing ngebeg bikul! -> The dog chases the mouse!
115. Cai ngebeg nyen? -> Whom are you chasing?
116. Ngebeg maling nika, enggalang! -> Chase that thief — hurry!
117. Ulung. -> To fall (familiar).
118. Icang ulung di jalan! -> I fell on the road!
119. Don punyan ulung liu. -> Many leaves fall.
120. Awas, cai lakar ulung! -> Watch out — you'll fall!
121. Runtuh. -> To fall / collapse (refined).
122. Tembok nika runtuh. -> The wall collapsed.
123. Runtuh sangkan linuh. -> It collapsed because of the earthquake.
124. Punyan agung nika runtuh? -> Did that big tree fall?

### Lesson 62: Materials and Textures

**Focus:** touch and texture — soft, stiff, wet, dry, hot/cold, rough/smooth, heavy/light, sharp/dull, thick/thin, mushy/crispy/tough, pointed, round/flat, holey, full/empty.
**Grammar note:** texture words follow the noun like all adjectives. Note the homograph: `tegeh` meant "tall" (Lesson 15); in this lesson's list it also carries "stiff" — context decides.
**New in this lesson (teaching order):** lemuh, empuk, tegeh (recycled — "stiff" sense), kaku, beles, belus (recycled), tuh (recycled), kering (recycled), kebus (recycled), panas (recycled), dingin (recycled), kasar (recycled), alus (recycled), berat, baat (recycled), abot (recycled), ringan, inggan (recycled), tajem, muntul, kandel (recycled), tipis (recycled), benyek, renyah, alot, lanying, bunter, lempeh, bolong, bek, puyung

1. Lemuh. -> Soft (familiar).
2. Kasur icang lemuh. -> My mattress is soft.
3. Lemuh gati bantal nika! -> That pillow is so soft!
4. Nasi nika lemuh. -> The rice is soft.
5. Empuk. -> Soft / tender (neutral).
6. Be sampi nika empuk. -> The beef is tender.
7. Empuk gati jukut nika. -> The vegetables are so tender.
8. Empuk lan lemuh pateh? -> Are empuk and lemuh the same?
9. Tegeh. -> Stiff (familiar); also tall (Lesson 15). [recycled — new sense]
10. Kain nika tegeh. -> That cloth is stiff.
11. Tegeh, nenten lemuh. -> Stiff, not soft.
12. Buku kuna nika tegeh. -> That old book is stiff.
13. Kaku. -> Stiff / rigid (neutral).
14. Awak icang kaku sesampun megawe. -> My body is stiff after working.
15. Kaku lan tegeh pateh? -> Are kaku and tegeh the same?
16. Baju daki nika kaku. -> Those dirty clothes are stiff.
17. Beles. -> Soaked / wet (familiar).
18. Baju icang beles! -> My clothes are soaked!
19. Beles gati, ujan bales. -> Soaked — heavy rain.
20. Beles, icang dingin gati. -> Soaked — I'm very cold.
21. Belus. -> Wet (familiar). [recycled from Lesson 36 — fresh sentences]
22. Kain nika belus. -> That cloth is wet.
23. Belus, jemur dumun! -> Wet — dry it first!
24. Belus lan beles pateh? -> Are belus and beles the same?
25. Tuh. -> Dry. [recycled — fresh sentences]
26. Jalan nika tuh jani. -> The road is dry now.
27. Jemur baju mangda tuh. -> Dry the clothes in the sun.
28. Tuh lan belus nenten pateh. -> Dry and wet are not the same.
29. Kering. -> Dry (neutral). [recycled from Lesson 23 — fresh sentences]
30. Musim nika kering gati. -> The season is very dry.
31. Bibih icang kering, icang bedak. -> My lips are dry — I'm thirsty.
32. Kering lan tuh pateh? -> Are kering and tuh the same?
33. Kebus. -> Hot (familiar). [recycled from Lesson 23 — fresh sentences]
34. Kopi nika kebus, alon-alon nginem! -> The coffee is hot — drink slowly!
35. Kebus gati wai niki! -> Today is so hot!
36. Toya kebus baang kopi. -> Hot water for coffee.
37. Panas. -> Hot (refined). [recycled — fresh sentences]
38. Ajengan nika panas dumun. -> The food is still hot.
39. Panas gati, icang ngrasa lempuyeng. -> So hot — I feel dizzy.
40. Tiang mandi toya panas. -> I bathe with hot water.
41. Dingin. -> Cold (neutral). [recycled — fresh sentences]
42. Toya niki dingin gati. -> This water is very cold.
43. Dingin, icang dot kopi panas. -> Cold — I want hot coffee.
44. Angin dingin semeng niki. -> The wind is cold this morning.
45. Kasar. -> Rough. [recycled from Lesson 36 — fresh sentences]
46. Kulit kayu nika kasar. -> The tree bark is rough.
47. Kasar gati tembok nika. -> That wall is so rough.
48. Kasar lan alus nenten pateh. -> Rough and smooth are not the same.
49. Alus. -> Smooth / fine. [recycled — fresh sentences]
50. Kain sutra nika alus. -> The silk cloth is smooth.
51. Alus gati wastra nika. -> That cloth is so fine.
52. Bias nika alus. -> The sand is fine.
53. Berat. -> Heavy (neutral).
54. Batu nika berat. -> That stone is heavy.
55. Berat gati buku nika! -> That book is so heavy!
56. Berat lan baat pateh? -> Are berat and baat the same?
57. Baat. -> Heavy (familiar). [recycled from Lesson 36 — fresh sentences]
58. Baat gati kayu nika! -> That wood is so heavy!
59. Icang nenten nyidaang ngaba, baat! -> I can't carry it — too heavy!
60. Baat, nenten ingan. -> Heavy, not light.
61. Abot. -> Heavy (refined). [recycled — fresh sentences]
62. Abot gati batu nika. -> That stone is very heavy.
63. Tiang nenten nyidaang makta, abot. -> I cannot bring it — too heavy.
64. Abot lan inggan nenten pateh. -> Heavy and light are not the same.
65. Ringan. -> Light in weight (neutral).
66. Helm nika ringan. -> That helmet is light.
67. Ringan gati, icang nyidaang ngaba. -> So light — I can carry it.
68. Ringan lan berat nenten pateh. -> Light and heavy are not the same.
69. Inggan. -> Light in weight (refined). [recycled from Lesson 36 — fresh sentences]
70. Wastra nika inggan gati. -> That cloth is very light.
71. Inggan, dadi tiang makta. -> Light — I can carry it.
72. Inggan lan abot nenten pateh. -> Light and heavy are not the same.
73. Tajem. -> Sharp (familiar).
74. Batu nika tajem. -> That stone is sharp.
75. Awas, tajem! -> Watch out — sharp!
76. Tajem nika nebek bisa. -> That sharp thing can pierce.
77. Muntul. -> Dull / blunt (familiar).
78. Muntul gati, nenten dadi ngetep. -> So dull — it can't cut.
79. Muntul lan tajem nenten pateh. -> Dull and sharp are not the same.
80. Batu nika muntul. -> That stone is blunt.
81. Kandel. -> Thick. [recycled from Lesson 36 — fresh sentences]
82. Kain nika kandel. -> That cloth is thick.
83. Kandel lan tipis nenten pateh. -> Thick and thin are not the same.
84. Kulit buaya nika kandel. -> The crocodile's hide is thick.
85. Tipis. -> Thin. [recycled — fresh sentences]
86. Kertas nika tipis. -> The paper is thin.
87. Tipis gati kamen nika jani. -> That sarong is so thin now.
88. Kaca nika tipis, awas uug! -> The glass is thin — careful, it breaks!
89. Benyek. -> Mushy (familiar).
90. Nasi nika benyek. -> The rice is mushy.
91. Benyek gati buah nika! -> That fruit is so mushy!
92. Benyek lan empuk pateh? -> Are benyek and empuk the same?
93. Renyah. -> Crispy (neutral).
94. Kacang nika renyah. -> The peanuts are crispy.
95. Renyah lan alot nenten pateh. -> Crispy and tough are not the same.
96. Cai demen renyah? -> Do you like crispy food?
97. Alot. -> Tough / chewy (neutral).
98. Be sampi nika alot. -> The beef is tough.
99. Alot gati, gigi icang sakit! -> So tough — my teeth hurt!
100. Alot lan empuk nenten pateh. -> Tough and tender are not the same.
101. Lanying. -> Pointed (familiar).
102. Batu nika lanying. -> That stone is pointed.
103. Lanying, awas nebek! -> Pointed — careful, it stabs!
104. Lanying lan muntul nenten pateh. -> Pointed and blunt are not the same.
105. Bunter. -> Round (familiar).
106. Bulan nika bunter. -> The moon is round.
107. Bunter gati batu nika. -> That stone is so round.
108. Bunter lan lempeh nenten pateh. -> Round and flat are not the same.
109. Lempeh. -> Flat (familiar).
110. Bias di pasih lempeh. -> The beach sand is flat.
111. Lempeh gati tanah nika. -> The ground is so flat.
112. Batu lempeh baang negak. -> A flat stone for sitting.
113. Bolong. -> Full of holes (familiar).
114. Baju icang bolong! -> My shirt has holes!
115. Jalan nika bolong liu. -> That road has many holes.
116. Bolong, cai nyait nika! -> It's holed — you sew that!
117. Bek. -> Full (familiar).
118. Gelas nika bek toya. -> The glass is full of water.
119. Bek gati peken jani! -> The market is so full now!
120. Kamar icang bek buku. -> My room is full of books.
121. Puyung. -> Empty (familiar).
122. Gelas icang puyung. -> My glass is empty.
123. Puyung lan bek nenten pateh. -> Empty and full are not the same.
124. Warung nika puyung jani. -> The stall is empty now.

### Lesson 63: Interjections & Fillers

**Focus:** the sounds that make speech alive — wow, ouch, oh my, hey, there!, come on!, of course, not really, that's how it is — plus the little particles `to`, `ni`, `je`, `ja` that pepper everyday Balinese.
**Cultural note:** `Ratu` is the respectful way to address someone ("sir/madam/lord"); `aratu`/`dewaratu`/`mimih` are exclamations of surprise. Particles: `to` = "there/see!", `ni` = "this here", `je`/`ja` soften or urge ("do come on"), `dong` = "surely/of course".
**New in this lesson (teaching order):** nah, nggih (recycled), beh, aduh, aratu, ratu, mimih, dewaratu, ah, ih, oh, pe, aruh, saja (recycled), yakti, engken (recycled), kengken (recycled), apa ne, to, ni, je, ja, dong (recycled), sing ja, ten je, nyanan (recycled), jani (recycled), nah keto, keto suba, patut (recycled), beneh (recycled)

1. Nah. -> Well / okay (familiar).
2. Nah, icang setuju. -> Well, I agree.
3. Nah, jalan jani! -> Well, let's go now!
4. Nah, cai suba uning. -> There — you know already.
5. Nggih. -> Yes / okay. [recycled from Lesson 1 — fresh sentences]
6. Nggih, tiang uning. -> Yes, I know.
7. Nggih, nggih, icang rauh. -> Yes, yes, I'm coming.
8. Nggih, patut nika. -> Yes, that's right.
9. Beh. -> Wow (surprise / annoyance).
10. Beh, gede gati umah caine! -> Wow, your house is so big!
11. Beh, jaen gati jukut niki! -> Wow, these vegetables are so delicious!
12. Beh, cai teka jani! -> Hey, you came (at last)!
13. Aduh. -> Ouch / oh no.
14. Aduh, batis icang sakit! -> Ouch, my foot hurts!
15. Aduh, icang ulung! -> Ouch, I fell!
16. Aduh, gigi icang! -> Ouch, my tooth!
17. Aratu. -> Oh my (refined exclamation).
18. Aratu, ida rauh! -> Oh my — he / she (honored) is coming!
19. Aratu, gede gati linuh nika! -> Oh my, that earthquake was huge!
20. Aratu, tiang iwang! -> Oh my, I was wrong!
21. Ratu. -> Lord / sir / madam (refined address).
22. Ratu, nunas tulung! -> Sir, please help!
23. Ratu, ampura titiang. -> Sir, my apologies.
24. Nggih ratu. -> Yes, sir.
25. Mimih. -> Oh my (surprise, familiar).
26. Mimih, jegeg sajan nyai! -> Oh my, you are so beautiful!
27. Mimih, mahal gati! -> Oh my, so expensive!
28. Mimih, icang jejeh! -> Oh my, I'm scared!
29. Dewaratu. -> Good heavens (familiar).
30. Dewaratu, gede gati blabar nika! -> Good heavens, the flood is so big!
31. Dewaratu, icang kepaling! -> Good heavens, I got lost!
32. Dewaratu, api ditu! -> Good heavens — fire there!
33. Ah. -> Ah.
34. Ah, nika biasa. -> Ah, that's normal.
35. Ah, icang nenten percaya! -> Ah, I don't believe it!
36. Ah, keto? -> Oh, like that?
37. Ih. -> Hey.
38. Ih, mai cai! -> Hey, come here!
39. Ih, ngudiang cai? -> Hey, what are you doing?
40. Ih, eda keto! -> Hey, don't (do) that!
41. Oh. -> Oh.
42. Oh, nika cai! -> Oh, it's you!
43. Oh, tiang uning jani. -> Oh, I know now.
44. Oh, nggih, beneh. -> Oh, yes, correct.
45. Pe. -> Wow (familiar).
46. Pe, gede gati punyan nika! -> Wow, that tree is so big!
47. Pe, cai dadi guru jani! -> Wow, you're a teacher now!
48. Pe, jegeg gati nganten nika! -> Wow, the bride is so beautiful!
49. Aruh. -> Oh dear (sigh).
50. Aruh, kenyel gati icang. -> Oh dear, I'm so tired.
51. Aruh, suba peteng. -> Oh dear, it's already night.
52. Aruh, tiang sebet gati. -> Oh dear, I'm so sad.
53. Saja. -> Truly / really. [recycled from Lesson 1 — fresh sentences]
54. Saja gati nika! -> That's really true!
55. Saja, icang nawang ia. -> Truly, I know him.
56. Saja cai ngomong nika? -> Did you really say that?
57. Yakti. -> Truly / certainly (refined).
58. Yakti nika leres. -> That is truly correct.
59. Yakti, tiang rauh benjang. -> Truly, I will come tomorrow.
60. Yakti pisan ida becik. -> He / she (honored) is truly very kind.
61. Engken. -> What's up / how. [recycled from Lesson 10 — fresh sentences]
62. Engken, cai? -> What's up, you?
63. Engken keneh caine jani? -> How is your heart now?
64. Engken jani, suba luungan? -> How is it now — better?
65. Kengken. -> How / what's up (variant). [recycled from Lesson 40 — fresh sentences]
66. Kengken unduk caine? -> How is your matter?
67. Kengken icang meli tiket? -> How do I buy a ticket?
68. Kengken jani keneh ragane? -> How is your heart now?
69. Apa ne. -> What's this? (phrase)
70. Apa ne, cai? -> What's this, you?
71. Apa ne di jalan? -> What's this on the road?
72. Ih, apa ne? -> Hey, what's this?
73. To. -> There / see! (familiar particle).
74. To cicing icang! -> There's my dog!
75. To ia teka! -> There he comes!
76. To buku caine! -> There's your book!
77. Ni. -> This (familiar particle).
78. Ni buku icang. -> This (is) my book.
79. Ni melah gati. -> This is really nice.
80. Ni nyen, cai uning? -> Who is this — do you know?
81. Je. -> Please / come on (particle).
82. Dingehang je! -> Do listen!
83. Jalan je jani! -> Let's go now, come on!
84. Mai je, nggih. -> Do come here, okay.
85. Ja. -> Just / do (particle).
86. Kija ja cai? -> Where on earth are you?
87. Ja keto, nggih. -> So it's like that, yes.
88. Ngoyong ja dini. -> Just stay here.
89. Dong. -> Surely / of course. [recycled from Lesson 32 — fresh sentences]
90. Dong icang uning! -> Of course I know!
91. Cai dong lakar rauh? -> You're surely coming?
92. Dong nika seken! -> Surely that's true!
93. Sing ja. -> Not really (phrase).
94. Sing ja icang uning. -> I don't really know.
95. Sing ja mahal nika. -> It's not really expensive.
96. Ia sing ja liang. -> He's not really happy.
97. Ten je. -> Not really (refined phrase).
98. Ten je tiang uning. -> I don't really know.
99. Ten je mahal nika. -> That's not really expensive.
100. Ten je ida krodha. -> He / she (honored) isn't really angry.
101. Nyanan. -> Later. [recycled from Lesson 14 — fresh sentences]
102. Nyanan je icang mulih. -> I'll go home later.
103. Nyanan, nah? -> Later, okay?
104. Nyanan icang lakar ngalih cai. -> Later I'll look for you.
105. Jani. -> Now. [recycled from Lesson 14 — fresh sentences]
106. Jani je, jalan! -> Now come on, let's go!
107. Jani, icang ngerti. -> Now, I understand.
108. Jani ja icang uning! -> Now I know!
109. Nah keto. -> That's how it is / there you go (phrase).
110. Nah keto, icang setuju. -> That's it — I agree.
111. Nah keto ja, cai jemet! -> That's the way — you're diligent!
112. Nah keto, jalan jani! -> That's right — let's go now!
113. Keto suba. -> That's just how it is (phrase).
114. Keto suba, icang sabar. -> That's how it is — I'm patient.
115. Keto suba unduk nika. -> That's just how that matter is.
116. Keto suba, nah. -> That's how it is, okay.
117. Patut. -> Correct. [recycled from Lesson 1 — fresh sentences]
118. Patut, nggih! -> Correct, yes!
119. Patut ja nika! -> That's indeed correct!
120. Nah, patut keto. -> Well, that's the right way.
121. Beneh. -> Correct (familiar). [recycled from Lesson 1 — fresh sentences]
122. Beneh ja cai! -> You're really right!
123. Beneh, icang setuju gati. -> Right — I totally agree.
124. Nah, beneh nika! -> Well, that's correct!

### Lesson 64: Work and Business

**Focus:** the working world — companies, meetings, tasks, wages, debt, profit and loss, votes, workers, bankruptcy and success.
**Grammar note:** meetings have three flavors: `rapat` (neutral), `sangkep` (the banjar's gathering), `pasewakan` (refined assembly). "Worker" as a phrase: `anak megae` (lit. "person working").
**New in this lesson (teaching order):** kantor (recycled), perusahaan, bisnis, dagang (recycled), ngadep (recycled), ngadol (recycled), meli (recycled), numbas (recycled), untung, rugi, modal, gaji, bayaran, naur (recycled), mayar (recycled), utang (recycled), suara, rapat, sangkep, pasewakan, tugas, karya, gawe, bos (recycled), anak megae, buruh, tani, nelayan (recycled), hasil (recycled), bangkrut, sukses

1. Kantor. -> Office. [recycled from Lesson 12 — fresh sentences]
2. Kantor icang nampek di jumah. -> My office is near home.
3. Kantor ida agung gati. -> His / her (honored) office is very grand.
4. Cai megawe di kantor apa? -> What office do you work in?
5. Perusahaan. -> Company.
6. Perusahaan nika gede. -> The company is big.
7. Ia megawe ring perusahaan. -> He works at a company.
8. Perusahaan nika ring kota. -> The company is in the city.
9. Bisnis. -> Business.
10. Bisnis icang cenik. -> My business is small.
11. Cai ngae bisnis apa? -> What business are you building?
12. Bisnis nika melah jani. -> The business is good now.
13. Dagang. -> Merchant / trade. [recycled from Lesson 33 — fresh sentences]
14. Dagang nika ngadep jukut lan buah. -> The merchant sells vegetables and fruit.
15. Dagang ring peken liu. -> There are many merchants at the market.
16. Cai dot dadi dagang? -> Do you want to be a merchant?
17. Ngadep. -> To sell. [recycled from Lesson 17 — fresh sentences]
18. Ia ngadep kain Bali. -> He sells Balinese cloth.
19. Ngadep ring dija cai? -> Where do you sell?
20. Ia ngadep lan numbas di peken. -> He sells and buys at the market.
21. Ngadol. -> To sell (refined). [recycled — fresh sentences]
22. Ipun ngadol wastra ring pasar. -> He / she sells cloth at the market.
23. Ngadol napi ida? -> What does he / she (honored) sell?
24. Ngadol lan ngadep pateh? -> Are ngadol and ngadep the same?
25. Meli. -> To buy. [recycled — fresh sentences]
26. Perusahaan nika meli tanah liu. -> The company buys much land.
27. Cai meli modal saking dija? -> Where do you get capital?
28. Meli mudah, ngadep mahal. -> Buy cheap, sell dear.
29. Numbas. -> To buy (refined). [recycled — fresh sentences]
30. Tiang numbas kain baang bisnis. -> I buy cloth for the business.
31. Ipun numbas tanah ring desa. -> He / she buys land in the village.
32. Numbas lan meli pateh? -> Are numbas and meli the same?
33. Untung. -> Profit.
34. Bisnis icang untung jani. -> My business profits now.
35. Untung caine kuda? -> How much is your profit?
36. Untung liu, icang liang! -> Big profit — I'm happy!
37. Rugi. -> Loss.
38. Bisnis icang rugi dibi. -> My business lost money yesterday.
39. Rugi gati, icang sebet. -> Such a loss — I'm sad.
40. Untung lan rugi nenten pateh. -> Profit and loss are not the same.
41. Modal. -> Capital.
42. Modal icang bedik. -> My capital is small.
43. Modal nika saking nyen? -> From whom is the capital?
44. Cai ngalih modal dija? -> Where do you find capital?
45. Gaji. -> Salary.
46. Gaji icang cenik. -> My salary is small.
47. Gaji nika mayar pidan? -> When is the salary paid?
48. Gaji lan gajian pateh? -> Are gaji and gajian the same?
49. Bayaran. -> Payment / wages.
50. Bayaran icang alit. -> My payment is small.
51. Bayaran nika kuda? -> How much is the payment?
52. Bayaran nika melah? -> Is the payment good?
53. Naur. -> To pay (refined). [recycled from Lesson 21 — fresh sentences]
54. Tiang naur utang icang. -> I pay my debt.
55. Ida naur bayaran buruh. -> He / she (honored) pays the laborers' wages.
56. Sampun naur ragane? -> Have you paid?
57. Mayar. -> To pay (familiar). [recycled — fresh sentences]
58. Cai mayar utang caine? -> Do you pay your debt?
59. Mayar jani, suksma. -> Pay now, thanks.
60. Ia suba mayar bayaran nika. -> He already paid those wages.
61. Utang. -> Debt. [recycled from Lesson 21 — fresh sentences]
62. Utang icang liu gati. -> My debt is so much.
63. Utang caine kuda jani? -> How much is your debt now?
64. Lunas utang icang jani! -> My debt is paid off now!
65. Suara. -> Voice / vote.
66. Suara caine penting. -> Your vote is important.
67. Suara icang siki. -> My vote is one.
68. Krama ngicen suara ring sira? -> To whom do the people give their vote?
69. Rapat. -> Meeting (neutral).
70. Ada rapat ring kantor jani. -> There is a meeting at the office now.
71. Rapat nika jam kuda? -> What time is the meeting?
72. Cai rauh ka rapat? -> Do you come to the meeting?
73. Sangkep. -> Meeting (of the banjar) (familiar).
74. Sangkep banjar pidan? -> When is the banjar meeting?
75. Krama rauh ka sangkep. -> The people come to the meeting.
76. Sangkep lan rapat pateh? -> Are sangkep and rapat the same?
77. Pasewakan. -> Meeting / assembly (refined).
78. Pasewakan ida ring griya. -> His / her (honored) assembly is at the griya.
79. Pasewakan nika napi undukne? -> What is the meeting about?
80. Wénten pasewakan benjang. -> There is an assembly tomorrow.
81. Tugas. -> Duty / task.
82. Tugas icang liu jani. -> My tasks are many now.
83. Tugas caine sampun puput? -> Is your task finished?
84. Tugas nika baang nyen? -> For whom is that task?
85. Karya. -> Work / creation (refined noun).
86. Karya ida agung. -> His / her (honored) work is grand.
87. Karya nika becik pisan. -> That work is very good.
88. Pakaryan lan karya pateh? -> Are pakaryan and karya the same?
89. Gawe. -> Work (familiar noun).
90. Gawe icang liu jani. -> My work is much now.
91. Cai ngae gawe apa? -> What work do you do?
92. Gawe nika keweh nanging bayaran melah. -> The work is hard but the pay is good.
93. Bos. -> Boss. [recycled from Lesson 33 — fresh sentences]
94. Bos icang ngae rapat jani. -> My boss holds a meeting now.
95. Bos perusahaan nika sira? -> Who is the company's boss?
96. Bos nika sugih. -> The boss is rich.
97. Anak megae. -> Worker (phrase).
98. Anak megae liu ring perusahaan nika. -> There are many workers at that company.
99. Anak megae nika bayaran kuda? -> How much are the workers paid?
100. Anak megae megawe kanti sanja. -> The workers work until evening.
101. Buruh. -> Laborer.
102. Buruh nika jemet gati. -> The laborer is very diligent.
103. Buruh ring carik liu. -> There are many laborers in the fields.
104. Bayaran buruh nika patut? -> Are the laborers' wages fair?
105. Tani. -> Farming.
106. Tani nika gawe bapa icang. -> Farming is my father's work.
107. Cai demen tani? -> Do you like farming?
108. Tani ring carik keweh. -> Farming in the fields is hard.
109. Nelayan. -> Fisherman. [recycled from Lesson 33 — fresh sentences]
110. Nelayan ring pasih nika liu. -> There are many fishermen at that beach.
111. Nelayan lan petani nenten pateh. -> Fishermen and farmers are not the same.
112. Nelayan ngalih iwak wengi? -> Do fishermen seek fish at night?
113. Hasil. -> Result / yield. [recycled from Lesson 48 — fresh sentences]
114. Hasil tani icang liu. -> My farm yield is plentiful.
115. Hasil bisnis nika untung. -> The business's result is profit.
116. Hasil gawene melah? -> Is the work's result good?
117. Bangkrut. -> Bankrupt.
118. Bisnis nika bangkrut! -> The business went bankrupt!
119. Bangkrut krana rugi liu. -> Bankrupt because of many losses.
120. Ia bangkrut, nanging ia nenten sebet. -> He went bankrupt, but he is not sad.
121. Sukses. -> Successful.
122. Dagang nika sukses gati! -> That merchant is so successful!
123. Bisnis caine sukses? -> Is your business successful?
124. Dumogi bisnis icang sukses! -> May my business be successful!

### Lesson 65: Advanced Emotions

**Focus:** the deeper emotional life — stress, relief, grief, disappointment, envy, grudges, sincerity, arrogance, stubbornness, bravery, regret — and peace of mind.
**New in this lesson (teaching order):** suntuk, lega, ledang (recycled), bangga (recycled), jengah (recycled), kuatir, sungsut, sebet (recycled), kecewa, iri, cemburu (recycled), dendam, sabar (recycled), ikhlas, las, nyebetang, ngaledangang, sombong, bengal, bengkung, jemet (recycled), anteng, mayus (recycled), elek (recycled), kimud (recycled), takut (recycled), wanen, wani, nyesel, lega keneh
*(Notes: `nyebetang` (make sad) and `ngaledangang` (make glad) are causatives. `bengal`/`bengkung` = stubborn; `wanen` (familiar) / `wani` (refined) = brave. `las` = to let go completely — the Balinese ideal after a disappointment.)*

1. Suntuk. -> Depressed / stressed (familiar).
2. Icang suntuk gati jani. -> I'm so stressed now.
3. Cai suntuk kenapa? -> Why are you stressed?
4. Suntuk, icang lakar melali dumun. -> Stressed — I'll go for an outing first.
5. Lega. -> Relieved (familiar).
6. Lega icang jani! -> I'm relieved now!
7. Cai lega utang caine lunas? -> Are you relieved your debt is paid off?
8. Lega gati keneh icang. -> My heart is so relieved.
9. Ledang. -> Relieved / pleased (refined). [recycled from Lesson 16 — fresh sentences]
10. Ledang manah ida mireng gatra becik. -> His / her heart is pleased hearing the good news.
11. Titiang ledang pakaryan titiang puput. -> I am relieved my work is finished.
12. Ledang lan lega pateh? -> Are ledang and lega the same?
13. Bangga. -> Proud. [recycled from Lesson 16 — fresh sentences]
14. Icang bangga ring panak icang. -> I am proud of my child.
15. Cai bangga malajah Basa Bali? -> Are you proud to learn Balinese?
16. Bangga nenten dados sombong. -> Pride must not become arrogance.
17. Jengah. -> Humiliated / shame-driven. [recycled — fresh sentences]
18. Ia jengah, dadi ia jemet. -> He felt jengah — so he became diligent.
19. Jengah nika dadi tenaga. -> Jengah becomes energy.
20. Cai ngrasa jengah? -> Do you feel jengah?
21. Kuatir. -> Worried (neutral).
22. Icang kuatir ring cai. -> I worry about you.
23. Kuatir kenapa nyai? -> Why are you worried?
24. Sampunang kuatir, ida rahayu. -> Don't worry — he / she (honored) is safe.
25. Sungsut. -> Sad / grieving (refined).
26. Manah ipun sungsut. -> His / her heart grieves.
27. Sungsut nenten luung baang angga. -> Grieving is not good for the body.
28. Ida sungsut mireng gatra kaon. -> He / she (honored) grieves hearing bad news.
29. Sebet. -> Sad. [recycled — fresh sentences]
30. Sebet gati keneh icang jani. -> My heart is so sad now.
31. Sampunang sebet, dumogi becik. -> Don't be sad — may it be good.
32. Sebet lan sungsut pateh? -> Are sebet and sungsut the same?
33. Kecewa. -> Disappointed (neutral).
34. Icang kecewa gati! -> I'm so disappointed!
35. Cai kecewa ring icang? -> Are you disappointed in me?
36. Kecewa nika rasa kaon. -> Disappointment is a bad feeling.
37. Iri. -> Jealous / envious.
38. Cai iri ring ia? -> Are you envious of him?
39. Iri nenten luung. -> Envy is not good.
40. Ia iri krana icang sukses. -> He is envious because I'm successful.
41. Cemburu. -> Jealous (romantic). [recycled from Lesson 43 — fresh sentences]
42. Tunangan icang cemburu. -> My fiancée is jealous.
43. Cemburu lan iri pateh? -> Are cemburu and iri the same?
44. Cemburu nika ngae gedeg. -> Jealousy makes (one) angry.
45. Dendam. -> Grudge / revenge.
46. Dendam nika dosa. -> Holding grudges is a sin.
47. Dendam ngae manah kaon. -> Grudges make the heart bad.
48. Sampunang dendam, ngidih pelih nika luung. -> Don't hold grudges — forgiving is good.
49. Sabar. -> Patient. [recycled from Lesson 16 — fresh sentences]
50. Tiang sabar ngantos ida. -> I patiently wait for him / her (honored).
51. Cai sabar gati, nggih. -> You are so patient, yes.
52. Dadi sabar nika melah. -> Being patient is good.
53. Ikhlas. -> Sincere / letting go.
54. Tiang ikhlas ngayah ring pura. -> I serve at the temple sincerely.
55. Ikhlas nika melah baang manah. -> Sincerity is good for the heart.
56. Cai ikhlas nulungin icang? -> Do you help me sincerely?
57. Las. -> To let go completely (familiar).
58. Las ja unduk nika. -> Just let that matter go.
59. Tiang las, tiang nenten dendam. -> I let go — I hold no grudge.
60. Las nika keweh nanging melah. -> Letting go is hard but good.
61. Nyebetang. -> To sadden (someone) (familiar causative).
62. Sampunang nyebetang manah tiang. -> Don't sadden my heart.
63. Cai nyebetang nyen? -> Whom are you saddening?
64. Nyebetang timpal nika jelek. -> Saddening a friend is bad.
65. Ngaledangang. -> To please / gladden (refined causative).
66. Tiang ngaledangang manah ida. -> I gladden his / her (honored) heart.
67. Ngaledangang panak nika dangan. -> Gladdening the child is easy.
68. Cai ngaledangang nyen jani? -> Whom are you pleasing now?
69. Sombong. -> Arrogant.
70. Ia sombong gati! -> He is so arrogant!
71. Sombong nenten becik baang timpal. -> Arrogance is not good among friends.
72. Cai sombong krana sugih? -> Are you arrogant because you're rich?
73. Bengal. -> Stubborn (familiar).
74. Adi icang bengal gati! -> My younger sibling is so stubborn!
75. Bengal nenten luung. -> Stubbornness is not good.
76. Cai bengal, ngudiang? -> You're stubborn — why?
77. Bengkung. -> Stubborn (familiar variant).
78. Bengkung gati ia! -> He is so stubborn!
79. Bengkung lan bengal pateh? -> Are bengkung and bengal the same?
80. Sampi nika bengkung. -> That cow is stubborn.
81. Jemet. -> Diligent / hardworking. [recycled from Lesson 15 — fresh sentences]
82. Jemet gati anak megae nika. -> That worker is so diligent.
83. Jemet malajah, dadi uning. -> Study diligently — (you) become knowledgeable.
84. Jemet lan mayus nenten pateh. -> Diligent and lazy are not the same.
85. Anteng. -> Well-behaved / diligent (neutral).
86. Panak nika anteng gati. -> That child is so well-behaved.
87. Anteng lan jemet pateh? -> Are anteng and jemet the same?
88. Cai anteng di sekolah? -> Are you well-behaved at school?
89. Mayus. -> Lazy. [recycled from Lesson 15 — fresh sentences]
90. Mayus gati icang wai niki! -> I'm so lazy today!
91. Mayus nenten luung baang pakaryan. -> Laziness is not good for work.
92. Mayus nenten dadi sukses. -> Laziness doesn't become success.
93. Elek. -> Ashamed / shy (familiar). [recycled from Lesson 16 — fresh sentences]
94. Icang elek ngomong ring ajeng liu. -> I'm embarrassed to speak in front of many.
95. Elek lan kimud pateh? -> Are elek and kimud the same?
96. Cai elek kenapa jani? -> Why are you embarrassed now?
97. Kimud. -> Shy (refined). [recycled — fresh sentences]
98. Tiang kimud mabaos ring ajeng krama. -> I am shy to speak before the people.
99. Kimud nenten jelek. -> Shyness is not bad.
100. Panak nika kimud ningal tamiu. -> The child is shy seeing guests.
101. Takut. -> Scared. [recycled — fresh sentences]
102. Icang takut ningeh krebek. -> I'm scared hearing thunder.
103. Cai takut ring sampi? -> Are you afraid of cows?
104. Takut lan jejeh pateh? -> Are takut and jejeh the same?
105. Wanen. -> Brave (familiar).
106. Wanen gati cai! -> You're so brave!
107. Wanen lan takut nenten pateh. -> Brave and scared are not the same.
108. Pahlawan nika wanen gati. -> The hero is very brave.
109. Wani. -> Brave / dare (refined).
110. Tiang wani mabaos ring ajeng ida. -> I dare to speak before him / her (honored).
111. Wani lan wanen pateh? -> Are wani and wanen the same?
112. Ipun wani nulungin tiang. -> He / she dares to help me.
113. Nyesel. -> To regret.
114. Icang nyesel jani. -> I regret it now.
115. Cai nyesel ngudiang? -> What do you regret?
116. Nyesel dumun, raris eling. -> Regret first, then remember.
117. Lega keneh. -> Peace of mind (phrase).
118. Lega keneh icang jani. -> I have peace of mind now.
119. Lega keneh lan suntuk nenten pateh. -> Peace of mind and stress are not the same.
120. Dumogi lega keneh ragane. -> May you have peace of mind.
