### Lesson 66: Politics & Society

**Focus:** village governance and civic life — banjar and desa, regency and province, leaders (gubernur, bupati, kepala desa, kelian, kelian adat), pecalang, law and awig-awig, elections, taxes, rules, speeches, community work, meetings, fines, donations, rights and duties.
**Cultural note:** Bali runs on two parallel systems: the `dinas` (state administration: desa, kepala desa, kabupaten) and the `adat` (customary: banjar, kelian adat, awig-awig). `Pecalang` are the customary security officers; `gotong royong` and `ngayah` are communal work; `dana punia` is religious giving.
**New in this lesson (teaching order):** banjar (recycled), desa (recycled), kabupaten, provinsi, pemerintah, gubernur, bupati, kepala desa, kelian (recycled), kelian adat, pecalang, hukum, awig-awig, adat, dinas, rakyat, krama (recycled), pemilu, pajak, aturan, pidato, darma wacana, gotong royong, ngayah (recycled), paruman, sangkep (recycled), denda, sumbangan, dana punia, hak, kewajiban

1. Banjar. -> Banjar (village ward). [recycled from Lesson 12 — fresh sentences]
2. Banjar icang ngae sangkep wengi niki. -> My banjar holds a meeting tonight.
3. Banjar nika kelianne sira? -> Who is that banjar's head?
4. Banjar lan desa nenten pateh. -> Banjar and desa are not the same.
5. Desa. -> Village. [recycled — fresh sentences]
6. Desa icang asri gati. -> My village is so lush.
7. Desa icang nampek ring kota. -> My village is near the city.
8. Desa nika akeh pura. -> That village has many temples.
9. Kabupaten. -> Regency.
10. Kabupaten nika linggah. -> That regency is wide.
11. Kabupaten lan provinsi nenten pateh. -> Regency and province are not the same.
12. Kabupaten nika akeh desa. -> That regency has many villages.
13. Provinsi. -> Province.
14. Bali nika provinsi. -> Bali is a province.
15. Provinsi nika gubernurne sira? -> Who is the province's governor?
16. Provinsi lan kabupaten nenten pateh. -> Province and regency are not the same.
17. Pemerintah. -> Government.
18. Pemerintah ngae jalan baru. -> The government builds new roads.
19. Pemerintah lan rakyat nenten pateh. -> Government and citizens are not the same.
20. Pemerintah nika ring kota. -> The government (office) is in the city.
21. Gubernur. -> Governor.
22. Gubernur Bali sira? -> Who is the governor of Bali?
23. Gubernur rauh ka desa icang. -> The governor comes to my village.
24. Gubernur lan bupati nenten pateh. -> Governor and regent are not the same.
25. Bupati. -> Regent.
26. Bupati nika nyen? -> Who is that regent?
27. Bupati rauh ka banjar. -> The regent comes to the banjar.
28. Bupati ring kabupaten, gubernur ring provinsi. -> The regent (serves) in the regency, the governor in the province.
29. Kepala desa. -> Village head (phrase).
30. Kepala desa icang nyen? -> Who is my village head?
31. Kepala desa rauh ka sangkep. -> The village head comes to the meeting.
32. Kepala desa lan kelian nenten pateh. -> Village head and kelian are not the same.
33. Kelian. -> Head of the banjar. [recycled from Lesson 28 — fresh sentences]
34. Kelian banjar icang jemet. -> My banjar head is diligent.
35. Kelian nika mabaos ring sangkep. -> The head speaks at the meeting.
36. Kelian lan kepala desa nenten pateh. -> Kelian and village head are not the same.
37. Kelian adat. -> Head of customary affairs (phrase).
38. Kelian adat nika nyen? -> Who is the customary head?
39. Kelian adat nika lingsir. -> The customary head is elderly.
40. Kelian adat jemet ngayah. -> The customary head serves diligently.
41. Pecalang. -> Traditional security officer.
42. Pecalang ada ring odalan. -> There are pecalang at the odalan.
43. Pecalang nika nyen? -> Who is that pecalang?
44. Pecalang lan polisi nenten pateh. -> Pecalang and police are not the same.
45. Hukum. -> Law.
46. Hukum nika penting. -> Law is important.
47. Cai uning hukum? -> Do you know the law?
48. Hukum lan awig-awig nenten pateh. -> Law and awig-awig are not the same.
49. Awig-awig. -> Traditional village law.
50. Desa icang ngae awig-awig. -> My village makes awig-awig.
51. Awig-awig nika saking krama. -> The awig-awig comes from the people.
52. Cai uning awig-awig banjar caine? -> Do you know your banjar's awig-awig?
53. Adat. -> Custom / tradition.
54. Adat Bali becik pisan. -> Balinese custom is very good.
55. Adat nika saking dumun. -> The custom is from long ago.
56. Tiang demen adat Bali. -> I love Balinese custom.
57. Dinas. -> Official / administrative (sphere).
58. Kelian dinas lan kelian adat nenten pateh. -> The official head and the customary head are not the same.
59. Kantor dinas ring kabupaten. -> The government office is in the regency.
60. Dinas lan adat nenten pateh. -> The official and customary spheres are not the same.
61. Rakyat. -> Citizens / the people.
62. Rakyat ring Bali liu. -> The people in Bali are many.
63. Rakyat lan pemerintah nenten pateh. -> Citizens and government are not the same.
64. Rakyat demen damai. -> The people love peace.
65. Krama. -> Community members. [recycled from Lesson 28 — fresh sentences]
66. Krama banjar icang liu. -> My banjar's members are many.
67. Krama ngae sangkep wengi niki. -> The people hold a meeting tonight.
68. Krama lan rakyat pateh? -> Are krama and rakyat the same?
69. Pemilu. -> Election.
70. Pemilu pidan jani? -> When is the election now?
71. Pemilu nika rame. -> The election is lively.
72. Krama ngicen suara ring pemilu. -> The people cast votes in the election.
73. Pajak. -> Tax.
74. Pajak nika baang pemerintah. -> The tax is for the government.
75. Cai mayar pajak? -> Do you pay tax?
76. Pajak nika penting baang desa. -> Tax is important for the village.
77. Aturan. -> Rule.
78. Aturan nika saking pemerintah. -> The rule is from the government.
79. Aturan banjar nika akeh. -> The banjar's rules are many.
80. Aturan nika penting baang krama. -> The rules are important for the people.
81. Pidato. -> Speech.
82. Gubernur ngae pidato. -> The governor gives a speech.
83. Pidato nika panjang. -> The speech is long.
84. Cai mireng pidato? -> Do you listen to the speech?
85. Darma wacana. -> Religious speech (phrase).
86. Pandita ngae darma wacana. -> The priest gives a religious speech.
87. Darma wacana nika indik dharma. -> The religious speech is about dharma.
88. Krama mireng darma wacana ring pura. -> The people listen to the religious speech at the temple.
89. Gotong royong. -> Communal mutual work (phrase).
90. Krama gotong royong ngedasang desa. -> The people clean the village together.
91. Gotong royong nika adat Bali. -> Gotong royong is Balinese custom.
92. Cai taen gotong royong? -> Have you ever done gotong royong?
93. Ngayah. -> Voluntary service. [recycled from Lesson 35 — fresh sentences]
94. Ngayah lan gotong royong pateh? -> Are ngayah and gotong royong the same?
95. Krama ngayah ngae penjor. -> The people voluntarily make penjor.
96. Ngayah nika ikhlas. -> Service is sincere.
97. Paruman. -> Village meeting (refined).
98. Paruman desa pidan? -> When is the village meeting?
99. Krama rauh ka paruman. -> The people come to the meeting.
100. Paruman lan sangkep pateh? -> Are paruman and sangkep the same?
101. Sangkep. -> Meeting (of the banjar). [recycled from Lesson 64 — fresh sentences]
102. Sangkep banjar wengi niki rame. -> Tonight's banjar meeting is lively.
103. Sangkep nika indik awig-awig. -> The meeting is about awig-awig.
104. Cai rauh ka sangkep pidan? -> When do you come to the meeting?
105. Denda. -> Fine / penalty.
106. Denda nika kuda? -> How much is the fine?
107. Mayar denda ring banjar. -> Pay the fine at the banjar.
108. Denda nika aturan banjar. -> The fine is the banjar's rule.
109. Sumbangan. -> Donation.
110. Sumbangan baang krama blabar. -> Donations for the flood victims.
111. Cai ngicen sumbangan? -> Do you give a donation?
112. Sumbangan nika liu. -> The donations are many.
113. Dana punia. -> Religious donation (phrase).
114. Dana punia baang pura. -> Religious donation for the temple.
115. Krama ngicen dana punia. -> The people give religious donations.
116. Dana punia nika ikhlas. -> The religious donation is sincere.
117. Hak. -> Right.
118. Hak krama nika penting. -> The people's rights are important.
119. Cai uning hak caine? -> Do you know your rights?
120. Hak lan kewajiban nenten pateh. -> Rights and duties are not the same.
121. Kewajiban. -> Obligation / duty.
122. Kewajiban krama ngayah ring pura. -> The people's duty is temple service.
123. Kewajiban nika akeh. -> The obligations are many.
124. Hak lan kewajiban melah baang desa. -> Rights and duties are good for the village.

### Lesson 67: Myths and Legends

**Focus:** the mythic universe — barong and rangda, leyak, ancestors, souls, heaven/hell/liberation, deities, demons, sacred objects (keris), and their consecration.
**Cultural note:** `barong` (protective lion-creature) and `rangda` (the fearsome widow-witch) embody the eternal balance of good and evil. `Kanda pat` are the four spiritual siblings born with every person. `Pura Dalem` faces death/transformation; `pasupati` spiritually "awakens" a keris or mask.
**New in this lesson (teaching order):** barong, rangda, leyak, boma, kanda pat, niskala (recycled), sekala (recycled), pura (recycled), dalem, pamangku (recycled), balian (recycled), sakti, tenung, sesuhunan (recycled), roh, atma, pitara, suarga, neraka, moksa, bhatara (recycled), dewa (recycled), dewi, raksasa (recycled), bhuta kala, tapa, mantra (recycled), tumbal, keris, pasupati, pica

1. Barong. -> Mythical protective lion-creature (good).
2. Barong nika melah gati. -> The barong is so beautiful.
3. Icang mabalih barong ring banjar. -> I watch barong at the banjar.
4. Barong nika gede tur jegeg. -> The barong is big and majestic.
5. Rangda. -> Mythical demon-widow (evil).
6. Rangda lan barong nenten pateh. -> Rangda and barong are not the same.
7. Rangda nika kaon gati. -> The rangda is very evil(-looking).
8. Icang jejeh ningal rangda. -> I'm scared seeing the rangda.
9. Leyak. -> Black-magic practitioner / flying spirit.
10. Kocap ada leyak di desa nika. -> It is said there is a leyak in that village.
11. Leyak nika nyen? -> Who is that leyak?
12. Krama jejeh ring leyak. -> The people fear the leyak.
13. Boma. -> Guardian spirit (also the giant face carving over gates).
14. Ada boma ring ajeng pura. -> There is a boma (carving) in front of the temple.
15. Boma nika nyen? -> What is that boma?
16. Boma nika gede gati. -> The boma is very big.
17. Kanda pat. -> The four spiritual siblings (phrase).
18. Kanda pat nika nyen? -> Who are the kanda pat?
19. Krama Bali uning kanda pat. -> Balinese people know the kanda pat.
20. Kanda pat nika papat. -> The kanda pat are four.
21. Niskala. -> The unseen world. [recycled from Lesson 35 — fresh sentences]
22. Leyak nika ring niskala? -> Is the leyak in the unseen world?
23. Niskala lan sekala nenten pateh. -> Unseen and seen are not the same.
24. Krama percaya ring niskala. -> The people believe in the unseen world.
25. Sekala. -> The seen world. [recycled — fresh sentences]
26. Sekala nika apa? -> What is the seen world?
27. Tiang uning sekala, nenten uning niskala. -> I know the seen world, not the unseen.
28. Sekala nika genah icang jani. -> The seen world is where I am now.
29. Pura. -> Temple. [recycled from Lesson 12 — fresh sentences]
30. Pura nika genah bhatara. -> The temple is the deity's place.
31. Pura di Bali liu gati. -> There are very many temples in Bali.
32. Pura nika suci tur kuna. -> The temple is holy and ancient.
33. Dalem. -> Temple of death/transformation (Pura Dalem); deep.
34. Pura dalem nika ring dija? -> Where is the pura dalem?
35. Pura dalem nika pura apa? -> What kind of temple is the pura dalem?
36. Krama mebakti ring pura dalem. -> The people pray at the pura dalem.
37. Pamangku. -> Temple priest. [recycled from Lesson 28 — fresh sentences]
38. Pamangku nika ngicen tirta. -> The priest gives holy water.
39. Pamangku pura dalem nyen? -> Who is the pura dalem's priest?
40. Pamangku nika uning mantra. -> The priest knows mantras.
41. Balian. -> Traditional healer. [recycled from Lesson 42 — fresh sentences]
42. Balian nika nulungin krama sungkan. -> The healer helps sick people.
43. Cai taen ka balian? -> Have you ever been to a healer?
44. Balian lan dokter nenten pateh. -> Healer and doctor are not the same.
45. Sakti. -> Powerful / magical.
46. Leyak nika sakti gati. -> That leyak is very powerful.
47. Sakti lan kuat nenten pateh. -> Sakti and strong are not the same.
48. Dewa nika sakti. -> The god is powerful.
49. Tenung. -> Fortune-telling.
50. Balian nika uning tenung. -> The healer knows fortune-telling.
51. Tenung nika apa? -> What is fortune-telling?
52. Cai percaya tenung? -> Do you believe in fortune-telling?
53. Sesuhunan. -> Revered deity. [recycled from Lesson 35 — fresh sentences]
54. Sesuhunan nika sira? -> Who is that revered deity?
55. Krama ngaturang banten ring sesuhunan. -> The people offer banten to the revered deity.
56. Sesuhunan nika sakti. -> The revered deity is powerful.
57. Roh. -> Spirit.
58. Roh nika wénten ring niskala. -> The spirit exists in the unseen world.
59. Cai percaya roh? -> Do you believe in spirits?
60. Roh nika nenten dadi ningal. -> The spirit cannot be seen.
61. Atma. -> Soul.
62. Atma nika suci. -> The soul is holy.
63. Atma lan awak nenten pateh. -> Soul and body are not the same.
64. Atma nika wénten ring dija? -> Where does the soul exist?
65. Pitara. -> Ancestor spirit (refined).
66. Krama muspa ring pitara. -> The people pray to the ancestors.
67. Pitara nika sira? -> Who are those ancestors?
68. Tiang eling ring pitara. -> I remember the ancestors.
69. Suarga. -> Heaven.
70. Suarga nika ring dija? -> Where is heaven?
71. Atma becik ka suarga. -> A good soul goes to heaven.
72. Suarga lan neraka nenten pateh. -> Heaven and hell are not the same.
73. Neraka. -> Hell.
74. Neraka nika genah kaon. -> Hell is an evil place.
75. Cai jejeh ring neraka? -> Are you afraid of hell?
76. Neraka lan suarga nenten pateh. -> Hell and heaven are not the same.
77. Moksa. -> Spiritual liberation.
78. Moksa nika napi? -> What is moksa?
79. Moksa lan suarga nenten pateh. -> Moksa and heaven are not the same.
80. Pandita ngrereh moksa. -> The priest seeks liberation.
81. Bhatara. -> God / deity. [recycled from Lesson 35 — fresh sentences]
82. Bhatara nika sakti gati. -> The deity is very powerful.
83. Krama ngaturang banten ring bhatara. -> The people offer banten to the deity.
84. Bhatara lan dewa pateh? -> Are bhatara and dewa the same?
85. Dewa. -> God. [recycled from Lesson 52 — fresh sentences]
86. Dewa nika ring suarga. -> The god is in heaven.
87. Dewa lan dewi nenten pateh. -> God and goddess are not the same.
88. Dewa ngicen rahayu ring desa. -> The god grants safety to the village.
89. Dewi. -> Goddess.
90. Dewi nika jegeg gati. -> The goddess is so beautiful.
91. Dewi lan dewa pateh? -> Are goddess and god the same?
92. Krama muspa ring dewi. -> The people pray to the goddess.
93. Raksasa. -> Giant / demon. [recycled from Lesson 52 — fresh sentences]
94. Raksasa nika sakti tur kaon. -> The giant is powerful and evil.
95. Raksasa ring satua Bali liu. -> There are many giants in Balinese stories.
96. Dewa menang ring raksasa. -> The god wins over the giant.
97. Bhuta kala. -> Negative spirits (phrase).
98. Bhuta kala nika nyen? -> Who are the bhuta kala?
99. Krama jejeh ring bhuta kala. -> The people fear the negative spirits.
100. Bhuta kala lan raksasa pateh? -> Are bhuta kala and raksasa the same?
101. Tapa. -> Meditation / asceticism.
102. Pandita nika tapa ring alas. -> The priest practices asceticism in the forest.
103. Tapa nika keweh? -> Is asceticism hard?
104. Tapa ngrereh sakti. -> Asceticism seeks power.
105. Mantra. -> Mantra / spell. [recycled from Lesson 35 — fresh sentences]
106. Mantra nika sakti. -> The mantra is powerful.
107. Balian nika uning mantra liu. -> The healer knows many mantras.
108. Mantra lan kidung pateh? -> Are mantra and kidung the same?
109. Tumbal. -> Sacrificial offering (against evil).
110. Tumbal nika pabuat desa. -> The offering is for the village.
111. Krama ngae tumbal. -> The people make the tumbal offering.
112. Tumbal lan banten pateh? -> Are tumbal and banten the same?
113. Keris. -> Sacred dagger.
114. Keris nika sakti gati. -> The keris is very powerful.
115. Keris ida ring dija? -> Where is his / her (honored) keris?
116. Keris nika kuna tur melah. -> The keris is old and beautiful.
117. Pasupati. -> To spiritually activate (a sacred object).
118. Keris nika suba pasupati? -> Has that keris been spiritually activated?
119. Pasupati nika upacara apa? -> What kind of ceremony is pasupati?
120. Pandita nika ngae pasupati. -> The priest performs the pasupati ritual.
121. Pica. -> Divine gift / blessing (refined).
122. Pica nika saking bhatara. -> The blessing is from the deity.
123. Titiang nunas pica ring sesuhunan. -> I ask the revered deity for a blessing.
124. Pica lan tirta nenten pateh. -> Pica and holy water are not the same.

### Lesson 68: Idioms & Proverbs (Everyday usage)

**Focus:** living Balinese idioms — comparisons (`buka`/`sekadi`), character insults and warnings (dog, rock, cow, dirty neck, long hand, loud mouth), social sayings (no reason, how could you, as it should be, that's how it is), and behavior idioms (copying, greed, pretending, noise).
**Grammar note:** idioms are fixed phrases — learn them whole. Many end in **-ne** (definite): `buka batune` "like the rock". `Pantesne` = "as it should be" (compare `pantes` "no wonder", Lesson 34).
**New in this lesson (teaching order):** buka, sekadi, tiwas (recycled), lacur (recycled), sugih (recycled), ngomong kosong, buka batune, ngadep gumi, gedeg basang, manis di bibih, tusing nyak kalah, nyemak gawe (recycled), buka sampine, aluh-aluh, sing ada unduk, dadi ati, pantesne, keto suba (recycled), sing nyidaang ngomong, ngalih gawe, pules-pulesan, uyut

1. Buka. -> Like (comparison, familiar).
2. Ia buka raksasa! -> He is like a giant!
3. Cai buka dewa baang icang. -> You are like a god to me.
4. Gumi Bali buka suarga. -> The land of Bali is like heaven.
5. Sekadi. -> Like (comparison, refined).
6. Ida sekadi bhatara. -> He / she (honored) is like a deity.
7. Sekadi lan buka pateh? -> Are sekadi and buka the same?
8. Baos ida sekadi kidung. -> His / her speech is like a sacred song.
13. Tiwas. -> Poor. [recycled from Lesson 39 — fresh sentences]
14. Tiwas nanging liang, nenten sebet. -> Poor but happy, not sad.
15. Tiwas lan sugih nenten pateh. -> Poor and rich are not the same.
16. Ia tiwas, nanging jemet gati. -> He is poor, but very diligent.
17. Lacur. -> Poor / unlucky. [recycled — fresh sentences]
18. Lacur gati nasib icang jani. -> My fate is so unlucky now.
19. Lacur lan tiwas nenten pateh. -> Lacur and tiwas are not the same.
20. Ia lacur, icang nulungin. -> He is unlucky — I help him.
21. Sugih. -> Rich. [recycled — fresh sentences]
22. Sugih nenten ja bagia. -> Rich does not (always) mean happy.
23. Cai sugih, icang tiwas. -> You are rich, I am poor.
24. Sugih nika jinah liu? -> Does rich mean much money?
25. Ngomong kosong. -> Empty talk (phrase).
26. Nika ngomong kosong! -> That's empty talk!
27. Sampunang ngomong kosong! -> Don't talk nonsense!
28. Ngomong kosong nika jelek. -> Empty talk is bad.
29. Buka batune. -> Like the rock — stubborn (idiom).
30. Cai buka batune, bengal gati! -> You're like a rock — so stubborn!
31. Ia bengkung buka batune. -> He is stubborn as a rock.
32. Bengal buka batune! -> Stubborn as a rock!
33. Ngadep gumi. -> To sell the land — to betray (idiom).
34. Ngadep gumi nika jelek gati. -> Betraying the homeland is very bad.
35. Cai ngadep gumi? -> Would you betray your land?
36. Sampunang ngadep gumi caine! -> Don't betray your land!
37. Gedeg basang. -> Angry — lit. "hot belly" (idiom).
38. Icang gedeg basang ningeh omong nika! -> I'm furious hearing that talk!
39. Gedeg basang nika nenten luung. -> Fury is not good.
40. Cai gedeg basang kenapa? -> Why are you furious?
41. Manis di bibih. -> Sweet on the lips — insincere (idiom).
42. Omongne manis di bibih dogen. -> His words are just sweet on the lips.
43. Manis di bibih nenten seken. -> Sweet lips are not sincere.
44. Sampunang manis di bibih! -> Don't be just sweet on the lips!
45. Tusing nyak kalah. -> Won't accept defeat (phrase).
46. Ia tusing nyak kalah! -> He won't accept defeat!
47. Tusing nyak kalah nika jengah? -> Is refusing to lose a kind of jengah?
48. Cai tusing nyak kalah, ngudiang? -> You won't accept losing — why?
49. Nyemak gawe. -> To take on work (phrase). [recycled from Lesson 37 — fresh sentences]
50. Icang nyemak gawe liu jani. -> I take on lots of work now.
51. Nyemak gawe nika jemet. -> Taking on work shows diligence.
52. Cai nyemak gawe apa jani? -> What work do you take on now?
53. Buka sampine. -> Like a cow — docile/following blindly (idiom).
54. Cai buka sampine! -> You're like a cow!
55. Sampunang buka sampine! -> Don't be like a cow!
56. Ia buka sampine, nenten mapineh. -> He's like a cow — he doesn't think.
65. Aluh-aluh. -> Taking it easy (phrase).
66. Aluh-aluh dogen cai! -> You just take it easy!
67. Aluh-aluh jani, megae nyanan. -> Take it easy now, work later.
68. Aluh-aluh nenten dadi biasa. -> Taking it easy must not become a habit.
69. Sing ada unduk. -> There's no reason (phrase).
70. Ia gedeg, sing ada unduk. -> He's angry for no reason.
71. Sing ada unduk, sampunang sebet. -> For no reason — don't be sad.
72. Cai pedih sing ada unduk? -> Are you angry for no reason?
73. Dadi ati. -> To have the heart (to do something bad) (phrase).
74. Dadi ati cai ngomong keto! -> How could you say that!
75. Dadi ati ia ngadep gumi! -> He had the heart to betray his land!
76. Dadi ati nika jelek gati. -> Having that heart is very bad.
77. Pantesne. -> As it should be (phrase).
78. Pantesne cai jemet malajah. -> As it should be, you study diligently.
79. Pantesne icang nulungin ia. -> I should help him.
80. Pantesne lan patut pateh? -> Are pantesne and patut the same?
81. Keto suba. -> That's how it is. [recycled from Lesson 63 — fresh sentences]
82. Keto suba, tiang las. -> That's how it is — I let go.
83. Keto suba unduk icang. -> That's just how my situation is.
84. Keto suba, nah, sampunang sebet. -> That's how it is — come on, don't be sad.
85. Sing nyidaang ngomong. -> Speechless (phrase).
86. Icang sing nyidaang ngomong ningeh gatra nika! -> I'm speechless hearing that news!
87. Cai sing nyidaang ngomong? -> Are you speechless?
88. Sing nyidaang ngomong, saking bengong. -> Speechless — out of amazement.
89. Ngalih gawe. -> To look for work (phrase).
90. Icang ngalih gawe ring kota. -> I look for work in the city.
91. Ngalih gawe nika keweh? -> Is looking for work hard?
92. Ia ngalih gawe di hotel. -> He looks for work at a hotel.
117. Pules-pulesan. -> Pretending to sleep (phrase).
118. Cai pules-pulesan, icang nawang! -> You're pretending to sleep — I know!
119. Pules-pulesan nika ngudiang? -> Why pretend to sleep?
120. Sampunang pules-pulesan! -> Don't pretend to sleep!
121. Uyut. -> Noisy / making a fuss.
122. Uyut gati di banjar jani! -> It's so noisy at the banjar now!
123. Sampunang uyut, icang lakar pules! -> Don't be noisy — I'm going to sleep!
124. Uyut nika ngae gedeg. -> The noise makes (one) angry.

---

### Lesson 69: Final Review (Part 1 — Grammar)

**Focus:** a full grammar sweep — thanks, already, what, if, like-that, this/that, all the pronouns, who, where, when, how, how-many, ever, will. Every entry is recycled: each gets three fresh review sentences combining skills from the whole course.
**New in this lesson:** *(none — pure review)*

1. Suksma. -> Thank you (familiar). [review — Lesson 3]
2. Suksma gati, cai suba nulungin icang. -> Thanks so much for helping me.
3. Suksma, icang suba liang. -> Thanks — I'm happy already.
4. Suksma pisan ring ragane. -> Thank you very much.
5. Suksma. -> Thank you (polite). [review — Lesson 3]
6. Suksma pisan ring ragane. -> Thank you very much.
7. Suksma, titiang sampun ngajeng. -> Thank you — I have eaten.
8. Suksma, nggih. -> Thank you, yes.
9. Sampun. -> Already (refined). [review — Lesson 2]
10. Sampun puput upacarane. -> The ceremony has finished.
11. Titiang sampun uning. -> I know already.
12. Ida sampun budal. -> He / she (honored) has gone home.
13. Suba. -> Already (familiar). [review — Lesson 2]
14. Suba pragat gawe icang? -> Is my work finished already?
15. Cai suba madaar? -> Have you eaten already?
16. Suba, icang suba uning. -> Already — I know already.
17. Napi. -> What (refined). [review — Lesson 7]
18. Napi gatra anyar? -> What's the new news?
19. Ida kayun napi? -> What does he / she (honored) want?
20. Napi mawinan ida ten rauh? -> Why didn't he / she (honored) come?

22. Apa undukne jani? -> What's the matter now?
23. Cai lakar ngae apa? -> What will you make?

29. Yen. -> If (familiar). [review — Lesson 30]
30. Yen ujan, da melali! -> If it rains, don't travel!
31. Yen seken, icang liang gati! -> If it's true, I'm so happy!
32. Yen keto, jalan ka peken! -> If so, let's go to the market!

34. Kenten unduk ipun. -> That's how his / her matter is.
35. Sampun kenten, ida mantuk. -> After that, he / she (honored) went home.
36. Nenten kenten, nika iwang. -> Not like that — that's wrong.
37. Keto. -> Like that (familiar). [review — Lesson 40]
38. Keto suba unduk nika. -> That's just how that matter is.
39. Keto ja, icang uning jani. -> Oh, like that — I know now.
40. Nah keto, melah gati! -> There, like that — very good!
41. Niki. -> This (refined). [review — Lesson 1]
42. Niki buku titiang. -> This is my book.
43. Niki becik, nika kaon. -> This is good, that is bad.
44. Aji kuda niki? -> How much is this?
45. Ene. -> This (familiar). [review — Lesson 1]
46. Ene gelah icang. -> This is mine.
47. Ene jaen gati! -> This is so delicious!
48. Ene lan ento nenten pateh. -> This and that are not the same.

51. Nika napi? -> What is that?

64. Tiang lakar mantuk jani. -> I will go home now.
65. Icang. -> I (familiar). [review — Lesson 1]
66. Icang suba bisa ngomong Bali akidik. -> I can speak a little Balinese now.
67. Icang lan cai timpal jani. -> You and I are friends now.
68. Icang dot melali ka pasih. -> I want to travel to the beach.
69. Ida. -> He / she (honored). [review — Lesson 1]

72. Ida lingsir nanging kuat. -> He / she (honored) is old but strong.
73. Ipun. -> He / she (refined). [review — Lesson 1]
74. Ipun makarya ring hotel. -> He / she works at a hotel.
75. Ipun sampun mantuk saking pasar. -> He / she has returned from the market.
76. Ipun lan tiang sawitra. -> He / she and I are friends.
77. Ia. -> He / she (familiar). [review — Lesson 1]
78. Ia suba makurenan. -> He is married already.
79. Ia lakar ngalih gawe. -> He will look for work.
80. Ia jemet gati malajah. -> He is very diligent studying.
81. Nyen. -> Who (familiar). [review — Lesson 4]
82. Nyen nika ring jaba? -> Who is that outside?
83. Nyen lakar rauh ka odalan? -> Who will come to the odalan?
84. Nyen adan caine? -> What's your name?
85. Sira. -> Who (refined). [review — Lesson 4]
86. Sira nika ring puri? -> Who is that at the palace?
87. Sira pesengan ragane? -> What is your name?
88. Sira jagi rauh benjang? -> Who will come tomorrow?
89. Dija. -> Where (familiar). [review — Lesson 10]
90. Dija cicing icang jani? -> Where is my dog now?

98. Pidan odalan ring pura desa? -> When is the village temple's odalan?

101. Kenken. -> How (familiar). [review — Lesson 2]
102. Kenken kabare jani? -> How are you now?
103. Kenken unduk caine? -> How is your situation?

105. Sapunapi. -> How (refined). [review — Lesson 2]
106. Sapunapi gatrane? -> How are you?

108. Sapunapi, ragane ledang? -> So — are you pleased?

110. Kuda aji kamene? -> How much is the sarong?

115. Pinten oka ida? -> How many children does he / she (honored) have?

### Lesson 70: Comprehensive A2 Final Exam (Review Part 2)

**Focus:** the grand finale — 31 essential set phrases from across the course, each given three fresh sentences: greetings and thanks, apologies, opinions, done/not-done, going-where, buying and bargaining, bringing, don't-forget, let's-go, don't-know, going-home, and blessings.
**New in this lesson:** *(none — pure review of set phrases)*

1. Matur suksma. -> Thank you very much. [review — Lesson 3]
2. Matur suksma pisan ring ida. -> Thank you very much to him / her (honored).
3. Matur suksma, titiang sampun ledang. -> Thank you very much — I am pleased already.
4. Matur suksma baang pitulung ragane. -> Thank you for your help.
5. Ngidih pelih. -> I apologize (familiar phrase).
6. Ngidih pelih, icang iwang. -> Sorry — I was wrong.
7. Ngidih pelih ja, icang engsap. -> Sorry — I forgot.
8. Ngidih pelih ring cai, timpal. -> Apologies to you, friend.
9. Rahajeng rauh. -> Welcome. [review — Lesson 2]
10. Rahajeng rauh ring Bali! -> Welcome to Bali!
11. Rahajeng rauh ring jero titiang. -> Welcome to my home.
12. Rahajeng rauh, dumogi rahayu. -> Welcome — may you be blessed.
13. Sapunapi gatrane. -> How are you? [review — Lesson 2]
14. Om Swastiastu, sapunapi gatrane? -> Hello — how are you?
15. Sapunapi gatrane jani? -> How are you now?
16. Sapunapi gatrane, tiang becik. -> How are you? — I am fine.
17. Tiang mapineh. -> I think (familiar phrase). [review — Lesson 34]
18. Tiang mapineh nika melah baang cai. -> I think that is good for you.
19. Tiang mapineh, ida jagi rauh. -> I think he / she (honored) will come.
20. Tiang mapineh unduk nika patut. -> I think that matter is right.
21. Titiang mamanah. -> I think (refined phrase). [review — Lesson 34]
22. Titiang mamanah nika becik pisan. -> I think that is very good.
23. Titiang mamanah ida ledang. -> I think he / she (honored) is pleased.
24. Titiang mamanah, sampunang kuatir. -> I think — don't worry.
25. Sampun wusan. -> Already finished (refined phrase).
26. Upacarane sampun wusan. -> The ceremony has finished.
27. Sampun wusan, ngiring mantuk. -> Finished — let's go home.
28. Pakaryan ida sampun wusan. -> His / her (honored) work is finished.
29. Suba pragat. -> Already finished (familiar). [review — Lesson 40]
30. Gawe icang suba pragat. -> My work is finished.
31. Suba pragat, icang lakar melali! -> Finished — I'll go have fun!
32. Suba pragat upacarane. -> The ceremony is finished.
33. Jagi kija. -> Where are you going (refined). [review — Lesson 10]
34. Ida jagi kija mangkin? -> Where is he / she (honored) going now?
35. Jagi kija, ragane? -> Where are you going?
36. Ipun jagi kija benjang? -> Where is he / she going tomorrow?
37. Lakar kija. -> Where are you going (familiar). [review — Lesson 3]
38. Cai lakar kija jani? -> Where are you going now?
39. Ia lakar kija, cai uning? -> Where is he going — do you know?
40. Lakar kija nyai semeng-semeng? -> Where are you going every morning?
41. Napi wénten. -> Is there…? (refined phrase).
42. Napi wénten pura ring desa nika? -> Is there a temple in that village?
43. Napi wénten gatra anyar? -> Is there new news?
44. Napi wénten tamiu ring jero? -> Are there guests at the home?
45. Apa ada. -> Is there…? (familiar phrase).
46. Apa ada luu dini? -> Is there trash here?
47. Apa ada gatra melah? -> Is there good news?
48. Apa ada banten di paon? -> Is there banten in the kitchen?
49. Sira pesengan ragane. -> What is your name (refined). [review — Lesson 4]
50. Ampura, sira pesengan ragane? -> Excuse me — what is your name?
51. Sira pesengan ragane, titiang lali. -> What is your name — I forgot.
52. Sira pesengan ragane, nggih? -> Your name, please?
53. Nyen adan caine. -> What's your name (familiar). [review — Lesson 4]
54. Ih, nyen adan caine? -> Hey, what's your name?
55. Nyen adan caine, icang engsap! -> What's your name — I forgot!
56. Aa, nyen adan caine? -> Hey, what's your name?
57. Aji kuda niki. -> How much is this (refined). [review — Lesson 21]
58. Aji kuda niki, ampura? -> How much is this, excuse me?
59. Wastra niki — aji kuda niki? -> This cloth — how much is it?
60. Aji kuda niki jani, diskon? -> How much now — any discount?
61. Kuda regan ene. -> How much is this (familiar phrase).
62. Kuda regan ene, cai? -> How much is this, mate?
63. Kuda regan ene jani? -> How much is it now?
64. Ene buku — kuda regan ene? -> This book — how much?
65. Dados kirang. -> Can it be less (refined). [review — Lesson 21]
66. Dados kirang akidik? -> Can it be a little less?

68. Dados kirang, tiang lakar numbas. -> If it can be less, I will buy.
69. Dadi kuang. -> Can it be less (familiar). [review — Lesson 21]

72. Dadi kuang, icang meli jani. -> If it can be less, I'll buy now.
73. Tiang jagi makta. -> I will bring (refined phrase).
74. Tiang jagi makta banten ring pura. -> I will bring offerings to the temple.
75. Nggih, tiang jagi makta nika. -> Yes, I will bring that.
76. Tiang jagi makta ajengan baang ida. -> I will bring food for him / her (honored).
77. Icang lakar ngaba. -> I will bring (familiar phrase).
78. Icang lakar ngaba montor. -> I'll bring the motorbike.
79. Nggih, icang lakar ngaba buku nika. -> Yes, I'll bring that book.
80. Icang lakar ngaba oleh-oleh. -> I'll bring souvenirs.
81. Sampunang lali. -> Don't forget (refined).
82. Sampunang lali mebakti semeng. -> Don't forget to pray in the morning.
83. Sampunang lali ring titiang! -> Don't forget me!
84. Sampunang lali, mebakti dumun. -> Don't forget — pray first.
85. Da engsap. -> Don't forget (familiar).
86. Da engsap ka odalan mani! -> Don't forget the odalan tomorrow!
87. Da engsap nyamane! -> Don't forget your family!
88. Da engsap, icang ngantos cai. -> Don't forget — I'm waiting for you.
89. Ngiring memargi. -> Let's go (refined). [review — Lesson 3]
90. Ngiring memargi ka pura. -> Let's go to the temple.
91. Ngiring memargi, suba tengai. -> Let's go — it's already afternoon.
92. Ngiring memargi sareng-sareng. -> Let's go together.
93. Jalan majalan. -> Let's walk (familiar). [review — Lesson 3]
94. Jalan majalan ka pasih! -> Let's walk to the beach!
95. Jalan majalan, suba sanja! -> Let's walk — it's already evening!
96. Jalan majalan alon-alon. -> Let's walk slowly.
97. Nenten uning. -> Don't know (refined). [review — Lesson 4]
98. Titiang nenten uning unduk nika. -> I don't know about that matter.
99. Nenten uning, ampura. -> Don't know — sorry.
100. Ipun nenten uning sira ida. -> He / she doesn't know who he (honored) is.
101. Sing nawang. -> Don't know (familiar). [review — Lesson 4]
102. Icang sing nawang ia. -> I don't know him.
103. Sing nawang, ampura ja. -> Don't know — sorry.
104. Cai sing nawang dija umahne? -> Don't you know where his house is?
105. Tiang jagi mantuk. -> I will go home (refined phrase).
106. Tiang jagi mantuk dumun, suksma. -> I'll go home first, thanks.
107. Sampun sanja, tiang jagi mantuk. -> It's already evening — I'll go home.
108. Nggih, tiang jagi mantuk benjang. -> Yes, I'll go home tomorrow.
109. Icang lakar mulih. -> I'm going home (familiar phrase).
110. Icang lakar mulih jani, cai! -> I'm going home now, mate!
111. Suba peteng, icang lakar mulih. -> It's already night — I'm going home.
112. Nggih, icang lakar mulih mani semeng. -> Yes, I'll go home tomorrow morning.
113. Dumogi rahayu. -> May you be blessed. [review — Lesson 2]
114. Dumogi rahayu, sampunang kuatir. -> May you be safe — don't worry.
115. Dumogi rahayu ring margi. -> May you be safe on the road.
116. Dumogi rahayu, titiang mapamit. -> May you be blessed — I take my leave.
117. Pamit. -> Goodbye / leave. [review — Lesson 2]
118. Pamit, icang lakar mulih. -> Goodbye — I'm going home.
119. Pamit dumun, nggih. -> Taking leave first, okay.
120. Pamit, suksma gati! -> Goodbye — thanks so much!
121. Suksma mewali. -> You're welcome (refined). [review — Lesson 58]
122. Suksma mewali, nggih. -> You're welcome, yes.
123. Suksma mewali ring ragane. -> You're welcome (to you).
124. Matur suksma! Suksma mewali! -> "Thank you!" — "You're welcome!"

---

## Section 3 Completion Summary — A2 Level Achieved!

After Lesson 70, the learner has completed the full A1→A2 curriculum:

- **Narrate in time:** past (`suba taen`, `dibi`, `mara wau`), future (`lakar`, `jagi`, `kel`, `benjang`), and sequence (`lantas`, `raris`, `sedurung`, `sesampun`).
- **Express inner life deeply:** stress, relief, grief, envy, sincerity, bravery, regret — and peace of mind (`lega keneh`).
- **Talk about work and money:** companies, wages, profit/loss, debt, taxes, and full shopping dialogues with bargaining.
- **Handle emergencies:** call for help, describe injuries, find doctors/healers, evacuate.
- **Discuss society:** the dual dinas/adat governance, elections, awig-awig, gotong royong, rights and duties.
- **Enter the sacred world:** ceremonies, offerings, the calendar festivals (Galungan–Kuningan, Nyepi, Saraswati, Siwaratri), life-cycle rites (otonan, metatah, pawiwahan, ngaben), and the mythic universe (barong–rangda, leyak, suarga–neraka–moksa).
- **Tell stories:** the full narrative frame from `ada kone` to `pamuput`, with giants, heroes, and sudden turns.
- **Enjoy the arts:** gamelan instruments, wayang, dance, sacred song — and the ineffable `taksu`.
- **Speak idiomatically:** 30+ living proverbs and idioms (`buka batune`, `keto suba`).
- **Master the registers:** the full sor-singgih system — `titiang/ida`, `ngambil/makta`, `pesu/medal`, `mulih/mantuk/budal`, `mati/padem/seda`.

**Selamat! Rahajeng! The complete A1–A2 Balinese course content (Lessons 1–70) is now ready for the app.**
